## Spear and Fang A Tale of the Cavemen / 矛与牙：穴居人的故事
===Original===

A‑æa crouched close to the cave mouth, watching Ga‑nor with wondering eyes. Ga‑nor’s occupation interested her, as well as Ga‑nor himself. As for Ga‑nor, he was too occupied with his work to notice her. A torch stuck in a niche in the cave wall dimly illuminated the roomy cavern, and by its light Ga‑nor was laboriously tracing figures on the wall. With a piece of flint he scratched the outline and then with a twig dipped in ocher paint completed the figure. The result was crude, but gave evidence of real artistic genius, struggling for expression.
It was a mammoth that he sought to depict, and little A‑æa’s eyes widened with wonder and admiration. Wonderful! What though the beast lacked a leg and had no tail? It was tribesmen, just struggling out of utter barbarism, who were the critics, and to them Ga‑nor was a past master.
However, it was not to watch the reproduction of a mammoth that A‑æa hid among the scanty bushes by Ga‑nor’s cave. The admiration for the painting was as nothing beside the look of positive adoration with which she favored the artist. Indeed, Ga‑nor was not unpleasing to the eye. Tall he was, towering well over six feet, leanly built, with mighty shoulders and narrow hips, the build of a fighting man. Both his hands and his feet were long and slim; and his features, thrown into bold profile by the flickering torchlight, were intelligent, with a high, broad forehead, topped by a mane of sandy hair.
A‑æa herself was very easy to look upon. Her hair, as well as her eyes, was black and fell about her slim shoulders in a rippling wave. No ocher tattooing tinted her cheek, for she was still unmated.
Both the girl and the youth were perfect specimens of the great Cro-Magnon race which came from no man knows where and announced and enforced their supremacy over beast and beast-man.
A‑æa glanced about nervously. All ideas to the contrary, customs and taboos are much more narrow and vigorously enforced among savage peoples.
The more primitive a race, the more intolerant their customs. Vice and licentiousness may be the rule, but the appearance of vice is shunned and contemned. So if A‑æa had been discovered, hiding near the cave of an unattached young man, denunciation as a shameless woman would have been her lot, and doubtless a public whipping.

===Chinese===

阿-艾娅（A‑æa）蜷伏在洞口附近，用满是好奇的目光注视着加-诺尔（Ga‑nor）。加-诺尔在做的事让她深感着迷，而加-诺尔本人更是如此。至于加-诺尔，他正全神贯注于手头的劳作，根本不曾察觉她的存在。插在洞壁石龛中的火把昏暗地照亮了这处宽阔的岩洞，加-诺尔借着火光，费力地在岩壁上描画图形。他先用一块燧石刻画出轮廓，接着用一根蘸着赭石颜料的细枝补全线条。画作虽显粗粝，却分明展露出挣扎着寻求宣泄的真正艺术天赋。

他想描画的是一头猛犸象，娇小的阿-艾娅看得睁圆了双眼，满心惊奇与钦佩。何等神妙！哪怕那巨兽少了一条腿、没了尾巴又何妨？挑剔评价的不过是刚从彻底蒙昧中挣扎而出的部落族人，在他们眼里，加-诺尔已是不折不扣的旷世行家。

然而，阿-艾娅藏在加-诺尔洞口旁稀疏的灌木丛中，绝非单纯为了看一幅猛犸象岩画。对画作的赞叹，与她凝视作画者时流露出的赤裸爱慕相比，根本不值一提。诚然，加-诺尔生得颇为英挺俊美。他身材高大，身量足有六英尺有余，体型精悍，宽肩窄腰，生就一副战士体格。他的双手与双脚俱修长纤瘦；摇曳火光勾勒出他轮廓分明的面庞，透着一股聪慧之气，高广的前额上方覆着一头黄褐色的浓密长发。

阿-艾娅自己也是个极为俏丽的姑娘。她的秀发与眼眸皆黑如点漆，长发如涟漪般披散在她纤巧的双肩上。她的面颊上尚未绘上赭石纹身，因为她仍是待字闺中的处子。

无论少年还是少女，皆是伟大克罗马农人（Cro-Magnon）的完美典范——这一种族不知自何方降临大地，以铁血昭示并确立了对野兽与兽人无可撼动的统治地位。

阿-艾娅不安地环顾四周。与人们想象相反，在蛮荒部族中，规矩与禁忌往往更为严苛狭隘、执行更为雷厉风行。

一个种族越是原始，其风俗便越不容逾矩。哪怕放纵邪淫暗地成风，表面上的不检点却必遭唾弃排斥。因此，阿-艾娅若是被人发觉潜伏在一个未婚青年的洞穴旁，等待她的必将是淫妇的斥责唾骂，少不了一顿当众笞刑。

===Original===

To be proper, A‑æa should have played the modest, demure maiden, perhaps skilfully arousing the young artist’s interest without seeming to do so. Then, if the youth was pleased, would have followed public wooing by means of crude love-songs and music from reed pipes. Then barter with her parents and then﻿—marriage. Or no wooing at all, if the lover was wealthy.
But little A‑æa was herself a mark of progress. Covert glances had failed to attract the attention of the young man who seemed engrossed with his artistry, so she had taken to the unconventional way of spying upon him, in hopes of finding some way to win him.
Ga‑nor turned from his completed work, stretched and glanced toward the cave mouth. Like a frightened rabbit, little A‑æa ducked and darted away.
When Ga‑nor emerged from the cave, he was puzzled by the sight of a small, slender footprint in the soft loam outside the cave.
A‑æa walked primly toward her own cave, which was, with most of the others, at some distance from Ga‑nor’s cave. As she did so, she noticed a group of warriors talking excitedly in front of the chief’s cave.
A mere girl might not intrude upon the councils of men, but such was A‑æa’s curiosity, that she dared a scolding by slipping nearer. She heard the words “footprint” and “gur-na” (man-ape).
The footprints of a gur-na had been found in the forest, not far from the caves.
“Gur-na” was a word of hatred and horror to the people of the caves, for the creatures whom the tribesmen called “gur-na,” or man-apes, were the hairy monsters of another age, the brutish men of the Neandertal. More feared than mammoth or tiger, they had ruled the forests until the Cro-Magnon men had come and waged savage warfare against them. Of mighty power and little mind, savage, bestial and cannibalistic, they inspired the tribesmen with loathing and horror﻿—a horror transmitted through the ages in tales of ogres and goblins, of werewolves and beast-men.
They were fewer and more cunning, then. No longer they rushed roaring to battle, but cunning and frightful, they slunk about the forests, the terror of all beasts, brooding in their brutish minds with hatred for the men who had driven them from the best hunting grounds.
And ever the Cro-Magnon men trailed them down and slaughtered them, until sullenly they had withdrawn far into the deep forests.

===Chinese===

按常理规矩，阿-艾娅理应扮演矜持温顺的少女，不露痕迹地撩起青年画匠的兴致。若青年动了心，随后便该借由粗犷的情歌与芦管乐声公开求欢。接着便是与少女父母的聘礼折价交涉，再之后——便是成婚。若求婚者家境殷实，甚至连求欢之仪亦可径直略去。

然而娇小的阿-艾娅本身便是部落开化的象征。暗送秋波并未打动那位沉浸于画作的青年，于是她索性采取了逾矩之举，暗中尾随窥视，盼着能寻得虏获其心的良机。

加-诺尔从完工的壁画前转过身，伸了个懒腰，目光投向洞口。娇小的阿-艾娅仿若受惊的野兔，倏地伏下身子，飞也似地窜开去。

当加-诺尔走出洞穴时，洞外松软泥土上一串细小纤长的脚印令他满腹疑窦。

阿-艾娅端庄文静地朝自家族穴走去，那地方与部落多数洞穴一样，距加-诺尔的洞穴颇有一段路程。行进间，她瞥见一群战士正神情激动地聚在首领洞穴前交头接耳。

一个少女本无权涉足男人的议事重地，但阿-艾娅实在按捺不住好奇，宁冒受训之险悄悄凑近。她听到了“脚印”和“古尔-纳”（gur-na，猿人）等字眼。

就在离洞穴不远的密林里，有人发现了古尔-纳的足迹。

对穴居人而言，“古尔-纳”是一个饱含仇恨与恐惧的字眼——部族口中的“古尔-纳”或猿人，实为另一个古老纪元残存的多毛巨怪，亦即凶残野蛮的尼安德特人（Neandertal）。这群恶煞比猛犸与剑齿虎更令人胆寒，在克罗马农人降临并向其发起殊死征战之前，整片森林皆在其魔爪统治之下。它们力大无穷而心智低下，野蛮嗜血，残暴食人，令部族族人闻风丧胆、深恶痛绝——这股代代相传的恐怖，最终化作后世食人魔、地精、狼人与兽人的阴森传说。

如今它们数量凋零，却变得愈发狡诈。它们不再咆哮着正面冲阵，而是阴鸷险恶地在林间潜伏游荡，令百兽辟易；那残忍蠢笨的脑海中，日夜翻涌着对夺走其最佳猎场之人类的滔天仇恨。

而克罗马农人亦始终追踪围剿、屠戮不休，直至它们悻悻退缩进幽林深处。

===Original===

But the fear of them remained with the tribesmen, and no woman went into the jungle alone.
Sometimes children went, and sometimes they returned not; and searchers found but signs of a ghastly feast, with tracks that were not the tracks of beasts, nor yet the tracks of men.
And so a hunting party would go forth and hunt the monster down. Sometimes it gave battle and was slain, and sometimes it fled before them and escaped into the depths of the forest, where they dared not follow. Once a hunting party, reckless with the chase, had pursued a fleeing gur-na into the deep forest and there, in a deep ravine, where overhanging limbs shut out the sunlight, numbers of the Neandertalers had come upon them.
So no more entered the forests.
---
A-æe turned away, with a glance at the forest. Somewhere in its depths lurked the beast-man, piggish eyes glinting crafty hate, malevolent, frightful.
Someone stepped across her path. It was Ka‑nanu, the son of a councilor of the chief.
She drew away with a shrug of her shoulders. She did not like Ka‑nanu and she was afraid of him. He wooed her with a mocking air, as if he did it merely for amusement and would take her whenever he wished, anyway. He seized her by the wrist.
“Turn not away, fair maiden,” said he. “It is your slave, Ka‑nanu.”
“Let me go,” she answered. “I must go to the spring for water.”
“Then I will go with you, moon of delight, so that no beast may harm you.”
And accompany her he did, in spite of her protests.
“There is a gur-na abroad,” he told her sternly. “It is lawful for a man to accompany even an unmated maiden, for protection. And I am Ka‑nanu,” he added, in a different tone; “do not resist me too far, or I will teach you obedience.”
A‑æa knew somewhat of the man’s ruthless nature. Many of the tribal girls looked with favor on Ka‑nanu, for he was bigger and taller even than Ga‑nor, and more handsome in a reckless, cruel way. But A‑æa loved Ga‑nor and she was afraid of Ka‑nanu. Her very fear of him kept her from resisting his approaches too much. Ga‑nor was known to be gentle with women, if careless of them, while Ka‑nanu, thereby showing himself to be another mark of progress, was proud of his success with women and used his power over them in no gentle fashion.
A‑æa found Ka‑nanu was to be feared more than a beast, for at the spring just out of sight of the caves, he seized her in his arms.

===Chinese===

然而对它们的畏惧依然笼罩着族人，断无女子敢独身擅入丛林。

偶尔有孩童误入林间，往往一去不返；搜寻之人往往只见到一场惨绝人寰的饕餮痕迹，以及既非兽爪亦非人足的古怪足印。

于是狩猎队便会倾巢而出，追杀巨怪。有时巨怪负隅顽抗而被诛杀，有时则仓皇遁走，逃入密林深处，令人不敢深追。曾有一支猎队因贪功追击，一路将逃窜的古尔-纳追入深山老林；在那幽深的峡谷之中，垂落的枝柯遮天蔽日，大群尼安德特人陡然杀出，将他们围困击溃。

自此，再无人敢深涉老林。

---

阿-艾娅收回目光，心有余悸地瞥了一眼森林。在那阴暗深处，定有兽人潜藏游弋，狭小如猪崽的双目中闪烁着狡黠而恶毒的凶光，可怖骇人。

一道人影横步挡住了去路。那是卡-纳努（Ka‑nanu），首领参议之子。

她耸肩避退。她厌恶卡-纳努，更打心底惧怕他。他追求她时总带着一股嘲弄的神情，仿佛纯为寻欢作乐，且坚信无论何时想要她都能随手得手。他一把扣住她的手腕。

“别转身走开啊，俏佳人，”他说道，“我是你的奴仆卡-纳努。”

“放开我，”她答道，“我得去泉边打水。”

“那我便陪你同去，我心头的明月，免得猛兽伤了你。”

尽管她连番抗拒，他依然不由分说地跟了上来。

“外头有古尔-纳游荡，”他厉声说道，“男子护送未婚姑娘同行，完全合乎族规。况且我可是卡-纳努，”他换了一副语调补充道，“别惹恼了我，否则我会教你学会顺从。”

阿-艾娅素知此人冷酷凶残。部族中许多姑娘都对卡-纳努青眼有加，因他生得比加-诺尔还要高大魁梧，眉宇间带着一股放荡狠戾的俊朗。然而阿-艾娅心系加-诺尔，对卡-纳努唯有畏惧。正因心怀畏惧，她反倒不敢过分反抗他的纠缠。加-诺尔对女子向来温和，哪怕漫不经心；而卡-纳努——同样作为部族进化的表征——则对自己玩弄女人的手段洋洋自得，肆意施展权威，手段绝无温柔可言。

阿-艾娅很快便发觉，卡-纳努比林中野兽更为可怖——因为刚走到离开洞穴视线的泉水边，他便一把将她揽入怀中。

===Original===

“A‑æa,” he whispered, “my little antelope, I have you at last. You shall not escape me.”
In vain she struggled and pleaded with him. Lifting her in his mighty arms he strode away into the forest.
Frantically she strove to escape, to dissuade him.
“I am not powerful enough to resist you,” she said, “but I will accuse you before the tribe.”
“You will never accuse me, little antelope,” he said, and she read another, even more sinister intention in his cruel countenance.
On and on into the forest he carried her, and in the midst of a glade he paused, his hunter’s instinct alert.
From the trees in front of them dropped a hideous monster, a hairy, misshapen, frightful thing.
A‑æa’s scream reechoed through the forest, as the thing approached. Ka‑nanu, white-lipped and horrified, dropped A‑æa to the ground and told her to run. Then, drawing knife and ax, he advanced.
The Neandertal man plunged forward on short, gnarled legs. He was covered with hair and his features were more hideous than an ape’s because of the grotesque quality of the man in them. Flat, flaring nostrils, retreating chin, fangs, no forehead whatever, great, immensely long arms dangling from sloping, incredible shoulders, the monster seemed like the devil himself to the terrified girl. His apelike head came scarcely to Ka‑nanu’s shoulders, yet he must have outweighed the warrior by nearly a hundred pounds.
On he came like a charging buffalo, and Ka‑nanu met him squarely and boldly. With flint ax and obsidian dagger he thrust and smote, but the ax was brushed aside like a toy and the arm that held the knife snapped like a stick in the misshapen hand of the Neandertaler. The girl saw the councilor’s son wrenched from the ground and swung into the air, saw him hurled clear across the glade, saw the monster leap after him and rend him limb from limb.
Then the Neandertaler turned his attention to her. A new expression came into his hideous eyes as he lumbered toward her, his great hairy hands horridly smeared with blood, reaching toward her.
Unable to flee, she lay dizzy with horror and fear. And the monster dragged her to him, leering into her eyes. He swung her over his shoulder and waddled away through the trees; and the girl, half-fainting, knew that he was taking her to his lair, where no man would dare come to rescue her.
---
Ga‑nor came down to the spring to drink.

===Chinese===

“阿-艾娅，”他低语道，“我的小羚羊，我终于得到你了。你休想逃出我的手心。”

无论她如何挣扎哀求皆是徒劳。他用强有力的臂弯将她一把抱起，大步流星朝森林走去。

她拼命挣扎，试图劝阻他。

“我力气不如你，无法反抗，”她说，“但我会在全族面前控告你。”

“你绝无机会控告我的，小羚羊，”他说，而她在他残忍的面孔上读出了一抹更加阴毒的杀机。

他抱着她一路深入森林，来到一处林间空地中央，他突然驻足，猎人的本能让他警觉起来。

前方的树冠上轰然坠下一头狰狞的巨怪——一个浑身生毛、畸形怪诞、令人心惊肉跳的怪物。

怪物逼近之际，阿-艾娅的尖叫声在林间久久回荡。卡-纳努面如土色，惊骇之下将阿-艾娅扔在地上，喝令她快逃。接着他拔出石刀与石斧，迎上前去。

那尼安德特人迈动粗短结节的双腿向前猛冲。他全身覆满粗毛，因杂糅了人类的特征而显得比猿猴更为狰狞可怖。扁平外翻的鼻孔、严重后缩的下巴、森森獠牙、几乎全无的前额，长得不可思议的巨臂从倾斜可怖的肩头垂落——在惊骇欲绝的少女眼中，这头怪物犹如自幽冥现身的恶魔。他那颗似猿的脑袋仅能齐到卡-纳努的肩膀，但体重恐怕比这位战士重出足有一百磅。

它如狂怒的野牛般狂暴冲来，卡-纳努毫无惧色地正面迎敌。他挥舞燧石斧与黑曜石匕首奋力劈刺，然而石斧如同玩具般被对方一把拍飞，握刀的手臂更在尼安德特人畸形的大手中如枯枝般应声折断。少女惊恐地看见参议之子被硬生生从地上揪起、轮向半空，又被狠狠摔过整片空地；只见那怪物合身扑上，将他撕得四分五裂。

接着，尼安德特人的视线转向了她。那双丑陋凶残的眼中浮现出一股异样的神情，它蹒跚踱步而来，毛茸茸的巨掌上沾满了鲜血，直直向她抓来。

她瘫软在地无法逃脱，惊惧交加，头晕目眩。怪物一把将她扯到面前，斜睨着她的双眸狞笑。它将她甩上肩头，摇摇晃晃地穿过林木走去；少女半昏半醒间明白，它正将自己掳往巢穴，那是绝无人类胆敢涉足营救的魔窟。

---

加-诺尔来到泉边饮水。

===Original===

Idly he noticed the faint footprints of a couple who had come before him. Idly he noticed that they had not returned.
Each footprint has its individual characteristic. That of the man he know to be Ka‑nanu. The other track was the same as that in front of his cave. He wondered, idly as Ga‑nor was wont to do all things except the painting of pictures.
Then, at the spring, he noticed that the footprints of the girl ceased, but that the man’s turned toward the jungle and were more deeply imprinted than before. Therefore Ka‑nanu was carrying the girl.
Ga‑nor was no fool. He knew that a man carries a girl into the forest for no good purpose. If she had been willing to go, she would not have been carried.
Now Ga‑nor (another mark of progress) was inclined to meddle in things not pertaining to him. Perhaps another man would have shrugged his shoulders and gone his way, reflecting that it would not be well to interfere with a son of a councilor. But Ga‑nor had few interests, and once his interest was roused he was inclined to see a thing through. Moreover, though not renowned as a fighter, he feared no man.
Therefore, he loosened ax and dagger in his belt, shifted his grip on his spear, and took up the trail.
---
On and on, deeper and deeper into the forest, the Neandertaler carried little A‑æa.
The forest was silent and evil, no birds, no insects broke the stillness. Through the overhanging trees no sunlight filtered. On padded feet that made no noise the Neandertaler hurried on.
Beasts slunk out of his path. Once a great python came slithering through the jungle and the Neandertaler took to the trees with surprising speed for one of his gigantic bulk. He was not at home in the trees, however, not even as much as A‑æa would have been.
Once or twice the girl glimpsed another such monster as her captor. Evidently they had gone far beyond the vaguely defined boundaries of her race. The other Neandertal men avoided them. It was evident that they lived as do beasts, uniting only against some common enemy and not often then. Therein had lain the reason for the success of the Cro-Magnards’ warfare against them.
Into a ravine he carried the girl, and into a cave, small and vaguely illumined by the light from without. He threw her roughly to the floor of the cave, where she lay, too terrified to rise.
The monster watched her, like some demon of the forest.

===Chinese===

他漫不经心地注意到先前有人留下的一对淡淡脚印。他又漫不经心地发觉，他们并未原路返回。

每个人的脚印皆有独特印记。男人的足印他一眼认出是卡-纳努的。而另一串痕迹，恰与方才留在自己洞穴外的纤细脚印一模一样。加-诺尔暗自纳罕——除却绘画之外，他对待万事向来漫不经心。

接着在泉水旁，他注意到少女的足印戛然而止，而男人的脚印则拐向密林深处，陷入泥土的深度远甚先前。显而易见，卡-纳努正抱着那少女。

加-诺尔绝非蠢人。他深知一个男子强行将少女抱入密林绝非善类所为。若她是自愿同行，便无须由人抱持。

而加-诺尔（部族进化的又一表征）偏生喜好多管闲事。换作旁人，或许耸耸肩便自顾离去，心想万万不可招惹参议之子。但加-诺尔素来少有兴致，一旦兴致被勾起，便定要一探到底。况且他虽无勇士之名，却生平无所畏惧。

当下他松动腰间的石斧与匕首，调准握矛的力道，循着踪迹追了上去。

---

尼安德特人扛着娇小的阿-艾娅，在密林中愈行愈深。

森林死寂而阴森，无鸟鸣啼，无虫啁啾。重叠交错的树冠下透不进半点阳光。尼安德特人迈动悄无声息的厚实脚掌，匆匆疾行。

野兽纷纷避退逃散。途中曾有一条巨蟒在丛林间蜿蜒穿行，尼安德特人以与其庞大身躯极不相称的敏捷蹿上大树。然而他在树冠上并不如鱼得水，甚至不如阿-艾娅来得灵巧。

少女偶尔还瞥见一两头与劫持者同类的巨怪。显然，他们早已远远越过了部族模糊划定的领地边界。其他尼安德特人皆避而远之。显而易见，它们如同野兽般独来独往，唯有遭遇共同强敌时才会结伙，且即便彼时亦极为罕见。这正是克罗马农人能够在对决中屡建奇功的根由所在。

它将少女掳入一处幽深峡谷，钻进一座仅由外透进微光的狭小岩洞。它粗暴地将她摔在洞底地上，少女蜷缩在地，惊惧得爬不起身。

巨怪如林间恶鬼般森然注视着她。

===Original===

He did not even jabber at her, as an ape would have done. The Neandertalers had no form of speech whatever.
He offered her meat of some kind﻿—uncooked, of course. Her mind reeling with horror, she saw that it was the arm of a Cro-Magnard child. When he saw she would not eat, he devoured it himself, tearing the flesh with great fangs.
He took her between his great hands, bruising her soft flesh. He ran rough fingers through her hair, and when he saw that he hurt her he seemed filled with a fiendish glee. He tore out handfuls of her hair, seeming to enjoy devilishly the torturing of his fair captive. A‑æa set her teeth and would not scream as she had done at first, and presently he desisted.
The leopard-skin garment she wore seemed to enrage him. The leopard was his hereditary foe. He plucked it from her and tore it to pieces.
And meanwhile Ga‑nor was hurrying through the forest. He was racing now, and his face was a devil’s mask, for he had come upon the bloody glade and had found the monster’s tracks leading away from it.
And in the cave in the ravine the Neandertaler reached for A‑æa.
She sprang back and he plunged toward her. He had her in a corner but she slipped under his arm and sprang away. He was still between her and the outside of the cave.
Unless she could get past him, he would corner her and seize her. So she pretended to spring to one side. The Neandertaler lumbered in that direction, and quick as a cat she sprang the other way and darted past him, out into the ravine.
With a bellow he charged after her. A stone rolled beneath her foot, flinging her headlong; before she could rise his hand seized her shoulder. As he dragged her into the cave, she screamed, wildly, frenziedly, with no hope of rescue, just the scream of a woman in the grasp of a beast.
Ga‑nor heard that scream as he bounded down into the ravine. He approached the cave swiftly but cautiously. As he looked in, he saw red rage. In the vague light of the cave, the great Neandertaler stood, his piggish eyes on his foe, hideous, hairy, blood-smeared, while at his feet, her soft white body contrasting with the shaggy monster, her long hair gripped in his bloodstained hand, lay A‑æa.
The Neandertaler bellowed, dropped his captive and charged. And Ga‑nor met him, not matching brute strength with his lesser might, but leaping back and out of the cave.

===Chinese===

它甚至不像猿猴那般吱哇乱叫。尼安德特人全无任何言语体系。

它递给她某种肉块——自然是血淋淋的生肉。少女吓得魂飞魄散，一眼看出那是一个克罗马农孩童的断臂。见她不肯进食，它便自行大快朵颐，以巨齿獠牙疯狂撕咬吞咽血肉。

它伸出毛茸茸的粗粝巨手将她抓起，掐得她柔嫩的皮肉一片乌青。它粗暴的手指穿过她的秀发，发觉弄痛了她时，脸上竟流露出狂暴的恶毒欢愉。它一把扯下一大绺发丝，仿佛在折磨这位娇美俘虏中获得了魔鬼般的快感。阿-艾娅紧咬牙关，不再像起初那样尖叫，巨怪这才悻悻作罢。

她身上裹着的豹皮衣裳似乎激怒了它。花豹是其世仇宿敌。它一把扯掉皮衣，撕成齑粉。

而此时此刻，加-诺尔正在密林中风驰电掣地追踪。他狂奔不已，面庞冷酷如修罗恶鬼，因他已然穿过了那处鲜血淋漓的空地，并发现了巨怪离去的脚印。

峡谷岩洞中，尼安德特人再次伸出手抓向阿-艾娅。

她纵身向后一跃，巨怪合身扑来。它将她逼入死角，但她从其手臂下方滑过，飞身脱出。然而巨怪依旧挡在她与洞口之间。

除非能绕过它，否则必将被它逼入绝境生擒。于是她佯装向一侧跃去。尼安德特人笨拙地朝那方倾轧，她如灵猫般骤然掉头，疾冲越过巨怪，窜入了峡谷中。

巨怪咆哮着狂追而出。一块碎石在她脚下滚翻，将她狠狠摔倒在地；尚未等她爬起身，巨爪已死死钳住了她的肩头。当它将她拖回岩洞时，她发出凄厉狂乱的尖叫，全无获救之望，那纯粹是一个女子落入兽爪时的绝望嘶喊。

加-诺尔跃入峡谷之际，正听到了这声凄厉尖啸。他迅捷而审慎地靠近洞口。定睛一看，只见一片血色狂怒。在岩洞昏暗的光影中，庞大的尼安德特人巍然挺立，狭小的猪眼死死盯着来敌，面目狰狞、毛发蓬乱、浑身溅血；而在其脚下，阿-艾娅柔嫩白皙的身躯与毛茸茸的巨怪形成鲜明对照，她乌黑的长发被那只沾满血迹的巨掌紧紧攥住，瘫倒在地。

尼安德特人狂吼一声，扔下俘虏猛扑上来。加-诺尔迎了上去，却不用凡人之力硬撼其蛮力，而是抽身后跃，纵出洞外。

===Original===

His spear leaped and the monster bellowed as it tore through his arm. Leaping back again, the warrior jerked his spear and crouched. Again the Neandertaler rushed, and again the warrior leaped away and thrust, this time for the great hairy chest. And so they battled, speed and intelligence against brute strength and savagery.
Once the great, lashing arm of the monster caught Ga‑nor upon the shoulder and hurled him a dozen feet away, rendering that arm nearly useless for a time. The Neandertaler bounded after him, but Ga‑nor flung himself to one side and leaped to his feet. Again and again his spear drew blood, but it seemed only to enrage the monster.
Then before the warrior knew it, the wall of the ravine was at his back and he heard A‑æa shriek as the monster rushed in. The spear was torn from his hand and he was in the grasp of his foe. The great arms encircled his neck and shoulders, the great fangs sought his throat. He thrust his elbow under the retreating chin of his antagonist, and with his free hand struck the hideous face again and again; blows that would have felled an ordinary man but which the Neandertal beast did not even notice.
Ga‑nor felt consciousness going from him. The terrific arms were crushing him, threatening to break his neck. Over the shoulder of his foe he saw the girl approaching with a great stone, and he tried to motion her back.
With a great effort he reached down over the monster’s arm and found his ax. But so close were they clinched together that he could not draw it. The Neandertal man set himself to break his foe to pieces as one breaks a stick. But Ga‑nor’s elbow was thrust under his chin, and the more the Neandertal man tugged, the deeper drove the elbow into his hairy throat. Presently he realized that fact and flung Ga‑nor away from him. As he did so, the warrior drew his ax, and striking with the fury of desperation, clove the monster’s head.
---
For a minute Ga‑nor stood reeling above his foe, then he felt a soft form within his arms and saw a pretty face, close to his.
“Ga‑nor!” A‑æa whispered, and Ga‑nor gathered the girl in his arms.
“What I have fought for I will keep,” said he.
And so it was that the girl who went forth into the forest in the arms of an abductor came back in the arms of a lover and a mate.

===Chinese===

他的石矛疾刺而出，刺穿巨怪的手臂，引来一声暴怒的嚎叫。战士旋即后撤抽矛，压低重心。尼安德特人再次合身扑来，战士又一次侧跃避开并顺势疾刺，这一次正中毛茸茸的胸膛。两人便这般缠斗不休，敏捷与智谋周旋于蛮力与凶残之间。

巨怪挥舞的巨臂猛地扫中加-诺尔的肩膀，将他横扫出十几英尺远，震得他整条手臂一时近乎麻痹废脱。尼安德特人狂扑而至，加-诺尔滚向一侧，弹身跃起。长矛屡屡见血，反倒彻底激红了怪物的兽性。

电光石火间，战士已退无可退，背部抵上了峡谷石壁；随着怪物狂冲而上，他听到了阿-艾娅的尖叫。长矛被脱手震飞，他落入了死敌的魔爪。粗壮的巨臂死死扼住了他的脖颈与肩头，森白尖利的獠牙直冲咽喉噬来。他拼死将手肘死死顶住对手凹陷的下颌，空出的拳头一次次狠狠轰在狰狞丑陋的面门上；足以击毙寻常凡人的重击，尼安德特巨怪竟浑然不觉。

加-诺尔只觉神智渐渐模糊。狂暴的双臂几乎要将他的骨骼碾碎、将脖颈折断。越过仇敌的肩头，他瞥见少女正搬着一块巨石悄悄逼近，他挣扎着示意她退后。

他使尽浑身解数，越过怪物的臂膀向下摸索到了腰间的石斧。但二人紧紧纠缠在一起，他根本无法拔出斧头。尼安德特人正蓄力欲将他如折断木棍般彻底折碎。然而加-诺尔的手肘紧紧抵住其下颌，尼安德特人越是发力拉扯，手肘便越深地陷入其毛茸茸的咽喉软肋。少顷怪物终于察觉痛楚，一把将加-诺尔甩将出去。就在脱身的刹那，战士闪电般抽出了石斧，挟着拼死一搏的狂怒，一斧劈开了怪物的头颅。

---

加-诺尔在倒下的死敌身前摇晃站立了片刻，随即感到一具温软的娇躯投入怀抱，一张俏丽的面庞贴近眼前。

“加-诺尔！”阿-艾娅呢喃呼唤，加-诺尔一把将少女紧紧搂入怀中。

“我以血战赢来的，便归我所有，”他说道。

就这样，那位被劫掠者裹挟入深林的少女，在心上人与夫君的臂弯中回到了部落。

### Act / 幕
===Original===

I

===Chinese===

I

#### Scene / 场
===Original===

I
City streets. A crowd of students standing on a corner. It is raining.

===Chinese===

I

城市街道。一群学生伫立在街角。天正下着雨。

#### Scene / 场
===Original===

II
The train. **Tommy** is arguing with the conductor.

===Chinese===

II

列车上。汤米（Tommy）正与列车长争执不休。

#### Scene / 场
===Original===

III
The next night. **Bertie** seated in his room, before a warm fire. He appears very comfortable and satisfied. Enter **Tommy**. His clothes are muddy and wrinkled, and his toes are showing through his worn shoes. He wobbles on his feet and otherwise appears somewhat fatigued.

===Chinese===

III

次日夜晚。伯蒂（Bertie）坐在自个儿房中，烤着温暖的炉火。他神情安适惬意。汤米上场。他浑身泥泞，衣衫褴褛褶皱，破旧鞋尖处露出脚趾。他步履踉跄，显露出几分精疲力竭之态。

## I Call from Canaan / 一 迦南的召唤
===Original===

“Trouble on Tularoosa Creek!” A warning to send cold fear along the spine of any man who was raised in that isolated back-country, called Canaan, that lies between Tularoosa and Black River﻿—to send him racing back to that swamp-bordered region, wherever the word might reach him.
It was only a whisper from the withered lips of a shuffling black crone, who vanished among the throng before I could seize her; but it was enough. No need to seek confirmation; no need to inquire by what mysterious, black-folk way the word had come to her. No need to inquire what obscure forces worked to unseal those wrinkled lips to a Black River man. It was enough that the warning had been given﻿—and understood.
Understood? How could any Black River man fail to understand that warning? It could have but one meaning﻿—old hates seething again in the jungle-deeps of the swamplands, dark shadows slipping through the cypress, and massacre stalking out of the black, mysterious village that broods on the moss-festooned shore of sullen Tularoosa.
Within an hour New Orleans was falling further behind me with every turn of the churning wheel. To every man born in Canaan, there is always an invisible tie that draws him back whenever his homeland is imperiled by the murky shadow that has lurked in its jungled recesses for more than half a century.
The fastest boats I could get seemed maddeningly slow for that race up the big river, and up the smaller, more turbulent stream. I was burning with impatience when I stepped off on the Sharpsville landing, with the last fifteen miles of my journey yet to make. It was past midnight, but I hurried to the livery stable where, by tradition half a century old, there is always a Buckner horse, day or night.
As a sleepy black boy fastened the cinches, I turned to the owner of the stable, Joe Lafely, yawning and gaping in the light of the lantern he upheld. “There are rumors of trouble on Tularoosa?”
He paled in the lantern-light.
“I don’t know. I’ve heard talk. But you people in Canaan are a shut-mouthed clan. No one *outside* knows what goes on in there﻿—”
The night swallowed his lantern and his stammering voice as I headed west along the pike.
---
The moon set red through the black pines. Owls hooted away off in the woods, and somewhere a hound howled his ancient wistfulness to the night.

===Chinese===

“图拉鲁萨溪（Tularoosa Creek）出事了！”这一声警讯，足以让任何在这片地处图拉鲁萨溪与黑水河（Black River）之间、名为“迦南”（Canaan）的偏僻穷荒中长大的汉子脊背发凉，无论他在何处听到这消息，都会立刻飞马奔回这片沼泽环伺的故土。

那不过是一个步履蹒跚的黑人老妪枯槁双唇间的一声低语，话音未落，她便已隐入茫茫人海，让我抓她不住；然而这只言片语已然足够。无须求证核实，亦无须打探那消息是借由黑民怎样神出鬼没的门道传至她耳中；更无须探究究竟是何等晦暗的力量，竟让那满脸褶子的老妪向一个黑水河男子吐露天机。只要这声警讯已然传出——且已然明晓，便足矣。

明晓？黑水河的子弟又怎能不解其中深意？这唯有一个昭然若揭的含义——沼泽密林深处宿怨重燃，幽暗的黑影正穿梭在落羽杉丛中，一场血腥屠杀正自阴森图拉鲁萨溪畔那被苔藓缠绕、笼罩在重重迷雾中的神秘黑人村落里悄然潜行而出。

不到一个小时，随着翻滚飞轮的每一次疾转，新奥尔良便被我远远抛在身后。对每一个降生于迦南的男子而言，骨血中永远系着一条无形的羁绊，每当家园受到盘踞在其丛林深处半个多世纪的阴霾威胁时，这根纽带便会将他召回故土。

溯大河而上、再转入水流更为湍急的小河疾驰，哪怕能雇到的最快轮船，此刻也慢得令我抓狂。当我终于踏上夏普斯维尔（Sharpsville）码头时，心中焦灼如焚，而前路尚有最后十五英里（15英里）行程。此时虽已过子夜，但我径直赶往马厩——依照半个世纪以来的祖训，那儿无论昼夜，永远备有一匹巴克纳家族的快马。

正当一个睡眼惺忪的黑人小厮紧固马肚带时，我转向马厩掌柜乔·拉菲利（Joe Lafely），只见他高擎马灯，连打哈欠，双眼发直。“图拉鲁萨溪当真有作乱的传闻？”

他在马灯昏黄的光影下面无人色。

“我不知情。风言风语是听过些。可你们迦南人向来口风极紧，外头的人根本没人晓得里头到底在闹什么勾当——”

当我策马沿着碎石官道向西疾驰时，沉沉夜色瞬间吞没了他的马灯与结结巴巴的话音。

---

一轮猩红的残月在黑魆魆的松林梢头渐渐西沉。猫头鹰在密林深处凄厉啼叫，不知何处的猎犬正对着茫茫黑夜发出亘古苍凉的呜咽。

===Original===

In the darkness that foreruns dawn I crossed Nigger Head Creek, a streak of shining black fringed by walls of solid shadows. My horse’s hoofs splashed through the shallow water and clinked on the wet stones, startlingly loud in the stillness. Beyond Nigger Head Creek began the country men called Canaan.
Heading in the same swamp, miles to the north, that gives birth to Tularoosa, Nigger Head flows due south to join Black River a few miles west of Sharpsville, while the Tularoosa runs westward to meet the same river at a higher point. The trend of Black River is from northwest to southeast; so these three streams form the great irregular triangle known as Canaan.
In Canaan lived the sons and daughters of the white frontiersmen who first settled the country, and the sons and daughters of their slaves. Joe Lafely was right; we were an isolated, shut-mouthed breed, self-sufficient, jealous of our seclusion and independence.
Beyond Nigger Head the woods thickened, the road narrowed, winding through unfenced pinelands, broken by live-oaks and cypresses. There was no sound except the soft clop-clop of hoofs in the thin dust, the creak of the saddle. Then someone laughed throatily in the shadows.
I drew up and peered into the trees. The moon had set and dawn was not yet come, but a faint glow quivered among the trees, and by it I made out a dim figure under the moss-hung branches. My hand instinctively sought the butt of one of the dueling-pistols I wore, and the action brought another low, musical laugh, mocking yet seductive. I glimpsed a brown face, a pair of scintillant eyes, white teeth displayed in an insolent smile.
“Who the devil are you?” I demanded.
“Why do you ride so late, Kirby Buckner?” Taunting laughter bubbled in the voice. The accent was foreign and unfamiliar; a faintly negroid twang was there, but it was rich and sensuous as the rounded body of its owner. In the lustrous pile of dusky hair a great white blossom glimmered palely in the darkness.
“What are you doing here?” I demanded. “You’re a long way from any darky cabin. And you’re a stranger to me.”
“I came to Canaan since you went away,” she answered. “My cabin is on the Tularoosa. But now I’ve lost my way. And my poor brother has hurt his leg and cannot walk.”
“Where is your brother?” I asked, uneasily.

===Chinese===

在黎明前的无边暗夜中，我渡过了黑人头溪（Nigger Head Creek）——一条黑亮如缎的水流，两岸镶嵌着宛如铁壁般的浓重树影。我的坐骑涉过浅水，蹄铁踏在湿漉漉的卵石上溅起水花，激起清脆的铮鸣，在死寂的夜色中显得惊心动魄。越过黑人头溪，便踏入了人们口中的“迦南”之地。

黑人头溪发源于北面数英里外孕育了图拉鲁萨溪的同一片大沼泽，一路正指南流，在夏普斯维尔以西数英里处汇入黑水河；而图拉鲁萨溪则向西奔流，在更上游处注入黑水河。黑水河的走向是从西北斜插东南；这三条水系便勾勒出一座巨大的不规则三角形地带，亦即人尽皆知的“迦南”。

迦南居住着最早开辟这片荒蛮之地的白人拓荒先驱的子孙后代，以及当年黑奴的后裔。乔·拉菲利说得一点不错：我们是一个与世隔绝、守口如瓶的族群，自给自足，对自家的偏安避世与独立自主极为执拗护短。

越过黑人头溪，密林愈发幽暗森严，道路收窄，蜿蜒穿行于未经圈围的松林之中，其间杂生着栎树与落羽杉。除了马蹄在薄薄尘土上发出的轻柔踢踏声与马鞍的皮革吱呀声外，天地间一片寂然。突然，幽暗阴影中传来一声低沉诱人的轻笑。

我猛地勒住缰绳，凝眸望向树丛。残月已沉，黎明未至，但树木间泛着一缕微弱的微光；借着这点微光，我辨认出垂挂着松萝的枝桠下立着一道模糊的倩影。我的手本能地探向腰间决斗手枪的枪柄，这一动作惹来又一声轻柔悦耳的笑声，既含嘲弄，又透着一股勾魂摄魄的媚态。我瞥见一张棕色的面庞、一双熠熠生辉的眼眸，以及在那桀骜不逊的浅笑中露出的森白牙齿。

“你到底是他娘的什么人？”我厉声喝问。

“为何骑马走得这般晚，柯尔比·巴克纳（Kirby Buckner）？”轻佻戏谑的笑意在话音中回荡。那口音带着几分异域腔调，令人陌生；虽有一丝微弱的非裔腔，却醇厚饱满、丰腴妖娆，恰如其主人那曼妙丰满的身躯。在那堆叠如云的乌黑秀发中，一朵硕大的白色花朵在幽暗中泛着凄清的微光。

“你在此处做甚？”我逼问道，“这儿离任何黑人棚屋都远得很。而且我从没见过你。”

“你离开之后，我才搬来迦南的，”她答道，“我的窝棚在图拉鲁萨溪畔。可如今我迷路了。而且我可怜的哥哥摔伤了腿，动弹不得。”

“你哥哥人在何处？”我心生疑虑，不安地问道。

===Original===

Her perfect English was disquieting to me, accustomed as I was to the dialect of the black-folk.
“Back in the woods, there﻿—far back!” She indicated the black depths with a swaying motion of her supple body rather than a gesture of her hand, smiling audaciously as she did so.
I knew there was no injured brother, and she knew I knew it, and laughed at me. But a strange turmoil of conflicting emotions stirred in me. I had never before paid any attention to a black or brown woman. But this quadroon girl was different from any I had ever seen. Her features were regular as a white woman’s, and her speech was not that of a common wench. Yet she was barbaric, in the open lure of her smile, in the gleam of her eyes, in the shameless posturing of her voluptuous body. Every gesture, every motion she made set her apart from the ordinary run of women; her beauty was untamed and lawless, meant to madden rather than to soothe, to make a man blind and dizzy, to rouse in him all the unreined passions that are his heritage from his ape ancestors.
I hardly remember dismounting and tying my horse. My blood pounded suffocatingly through the veins in my temples as I scowled down at her, suspicious yet fascinated.
“How do you know my name? Who *are* you?”
With a provocative laugh, she seized my hand and drew me deeper into the shadows. Fascinated by the lights gleaming in her dark eyes, I was hardly aware of her action.
“Who does not know Kirby Buckner?” she laughed. “All the people of Canaan speak of you, white or black. Come! My poor brother longs to look upon you!” And she laughed with malicious triumph.
It was this brazen effrontery that brought me to my senses. Its cynical mockery broke the almost hypnotic spell in which I had fallen.
I stopped short, throwing her hand aside, snarling: “What devil’s game are you up to, wench?”
Instantly the smiling siren was changed to a blood-mad jungle cat. Her eyes flamed murderously, her red lips writhed in a snarl as she leaped back, crying out shrilly. A rush of bare feet answered her call. The first faint light of dawn struck through the branches, revealing my assailants, three gaunt black giants. I saw the gleaming whites of their eyes, their bare glistening teeth, the sheen of naked steel in their hands.
My first bullet crashed through the head of the tallest man, knocking him dead in full stride.

===Chinese===

她那字正腔圆的流利英语让我心神不宁，因我早已听惯了黑民的乡音土语。

“就在后头的密林里，就在那儿——极深的老林里！”她并未用手去指，而是款款扭动柔韧的身躯指向幽暗深处，脸上浮现出一抹放肆大胆的微笑。

我心知根本没有什么受伤的哥哥，而她也心知肚明我已经看破，反倒迎着我的目光巧笑嫣然。然而我心中却掀起了一股古怪剧烈的波澜。我平生从未将目光多停留在一个黑人或棕肤女子身上。但眼前这个有着四分之一混血（quadroon）的姑娘，却与我以往见过的所有女人大相径庭。她的面部轮廓端庄匀称，酷似白人女子，谈吐亦绝非粗俗仆妇可比。然而她骨子里却透着一股原始蛮荒的野性——在她毫不掩饰诱惑的浅笑中、在她闪烁的明眸中、在她那丰腴躯体肆无忌惮的姿态中毕露无遗。她的一举手一投足，皆将她与寻常凡俗女子截然分开；她的美狂野不羁、无法无天，生来不是为了安抚人心，而是为了令人神魂颠倒、目眩神迷，为了彻底唤醒男人从远古猿猴祖先血脉中继承而来的全部狂暴本能。

我几乎记不得自己是何时翻身下马并将坐骑拴好的。太阳穴两侧的青筋狂跳，血液如窒息般剧烈奔涌，我皱起眉头凝视着她，满心戒备却又难以自拔。

“你怎会知晓我的名字？你究竟是谁？”

伴着一声极具挑逗的笑声，她猛地牵起我的手，将我往更深的阴影里拉去。我被她黑眸中闪烁的异样光芒所慑，整个人近乎浑浑噩噩，任由她牵引。

“在迦南谁不认得柯尔比·巴克纳？”她娇笑道，“全迦南不论黑人白人，人人都在谈论你。快来吧！我那可怜的哥哥正眼巴巴盼着见你呢！”说罢，她发出一阵充满恶毒得逞意味的大笑。

正是这肆无忌惮的张狂嘴脸，猛然将我惊醒。那尖酸阴鸷的嘲讽瞬间击碎了险些将我笼罩的迷魂魔咒。

我骤然止步，一把甩开她的手，咆哮道：“你这贱妇，到底在耍什么鬼把戏？”

刹那间，方才还媚态横生的塞壬妖姬陡然化作一头嗜血狂暴的丛林山猫。她的眼中喷涌出凛冽的杀机，殷红的双唇扭曲成可怖的怒号，抽身暴退之际发出一声尖锐刺耳的啼鸣。一阵赤足疾奔的杂乱声响立刻应声而至。黎明的第一缕惨淡微光穿透枝桠，映照出我的伏击者——三名身材精瘦如柴却高大魁梧的黑人巨汉。我看见他们翻白的眼珠、龇出的白牙，以及手中森然闪烁的雪亮钢刃。

我的第一发枪弹轰然贯穿了身材最高之人的头颅，他在全速冲锋中应声倒毙。

===Original===

My second pistol snapped﻿—the cap had somehow slipped from the nipple. I dashed it into a black face, and as the man fell, half stunned, I whipped out my bowie knife and closed with the other. I parried his stab and my counter-stroke ripped across his belly-muscles. He screamed like a swamp-panther and made a wild grab for my knife wrist, but I struck him in the mouth with my clenched left fist, and felt his lips split and his teeth crumble under the impact as he reeled backward, his knife waving wildly. Before he could regain his balance I was after him, thrusting, and got home under his ribs. He groaned and slipped to the ground in a puddle of his own blood.
I wheeled about, looking for the other. He was just rising, blood streaming down his face and neck. As I started for him he sounded a panicky yell and plunged into the underbrush. The crashing of his blind flight came back to me, muffled with distance. The girl was gone.

===Chinese===

我的第二支手枪却击发落空——火帽不知怎的从火门底座上滑脱了。我扬手将手枪狠狠砸进一张黑脸，趁着那人被砸得晕头转向倒地之际，我刷地拔出博伊刀（bowie knife），与另一人贴身肉搏。我格开他的直刺，顺势反手一刀横剖，径直撕裂了他的腹肌。他发出如沼泽山猫般的凄厉哀嚎，发狂般伸手来扣我的持刀手腕，但我左手握拳猛轰其面门，伴随着骨肉相撞的闷响，只觉其嘴唇崩裂、牙齿粉碎；他踉跄后仰，手中的利刃狂乱挥舞。尚未等他稳住重心，我已如影随形扑上，一刀直捣黄龙，精准扎入其肋骨下方。他闷哼一声，软绵绵瘫倒在自己喷涌而出的血泊之中。

我猛然旋身，寻觅先前被砸倒的汉子。他正挣扎着爬起身，鲜血顺着面门与脖颈滚滚而落。见我持刀逼近，他发出一声惊恐至极的狂叫，一头撞进灌木丛仓皇逃窜。他盲目奔逃踩断枝桠的噼啪声自远处隐隐传来，渐不可闻。而那个妖异的姑娘，早已鸿飞冥冥。

## I The Killer in the Dark / 一 黑暗中的杀手
===Original===

Egyptian darkness! The phrase is too vivid for complete comfort, suggesting not only blackness, but unseen things lurking in that blackness; things that skulk in the deep shadows and shun the light of day; slinking figures that prowl beyond the edge of normal life.
Some such thoughts flitted vaguely through my mind that night as I groped along the narrow trail that wound through the deep pinelands. Such thoughts are likely to keep company with any man who dares invade, in the night, that lonely stretch of densely timbered river-country which the black people call Egypt, for some obscurely racial reason.
There is no blackness this side of Hell’s unlighted abyss as absolute as the blackness of the pine woods. The trail was but a half-guessed trace winding between walls of solid ebony. I followed it as much by the instincts of the piny woods dweller as by the guidance of the external senses. I went as hurriedly as I dared, but stealth was mingled with my haste, and my ears were whetted to knife-edge alertness. This caution did not spring from the uncanny speculations roused by the darkness and silence. I had good, material reason to be wary. Ghosts might roam the pinelands with gaping, bloody throats and cannibalistic hunger as the negroes maintained, but it was no ghost I feared. I listened for the snap of a twig under a great, splay foot, for any sound that would presage murder striking from the black shadows. The creature which, I feared, haunted Egypt was more to be dreaded than any gibbering phantom.
That morning the worst negro desperado in that part of the state had broken from the clutches of the law, leaving a ghastly toll of dead behind him. Down along the river, bloodhounds were baying through the brush and hard-eyed men with rifles were beating up the thickets.
They were seeking him in the fastnesses near the scattered black settlements, knowing that a negro seeks his own kind in his extremity. But I knew Tope Braxton better than they did; I knew he deviated from the general type of his race. He was unbelievably primitive, atavistic enough to plunge into uninhabited wilderness and live like a blood-mad gorilla in solitude that would have terrified and daunted a more normal member of his race.
So while the hunt flowed away in another direction, I rode toward Egypt, alone.

===Chinese===

埃及般的沉沉黑暗！这句短语过于生动逼真，乃至令人背脊生寒——它暗示的不仅是漆黑一团，更有隐匿在黑暗中的未知之物；潜伏在重重阴影深处避开白昼烈阳的凶邪；在凡俗生灵视线之外游荡徘徊的鬼祟身影。

那晚，当我在蜿蜒穿过松林深处的狭窄小径上摸索前行时，类似的念头在脑海中隐约翻腾。任何胆敢在黑夜中闯入这片沿河密林穷荒的汉子，多半都会被这般念头缠绕——出于某种晦涩难明的种族缘由，黑人们将这片孤僻死寂的地界称作“埃及”（Egypt）。

地狱无光的幽冥深渊之外，世间再无任何黑暗能比得上这片松林的深沉酷烈。脚下的小道不过是一条在如黑曜石般浓密漆黑的树墙间蜿蜒的若隐若现痕迹。我循路而行，一半仰赖外界感官的指引，一半则全凭松林居客与生俱来的本能。我走得极快，脚下却不敢有丝毫大意，耳力如刀锋般警觉地搜寻着风吹草动。这般小心翼翼绝非源自黑暗死寂所激起的怪力乱神臆想。我有着极为切实的戒备缘由。或许正如黑人们言之凿凿的那样，松林里确实游荡着咧开血淋淋喉管、饥肠辘辘择人而噬的冤魂，但我畏惧的绝非鬼魅。我倾听的是宽阔扁平的大脚踩碎枯枝的脆响，是任何预示着杀戮即将自黑影中骤然暴起的声息。我所惧怕的那头在“埃及”游弋出没的凶物，远比任何狂乱呓语的游魂恶煞更令人毛骨悚然。

就在今晨，本州这一带最穷凶极恶的黑人亡命之徒挣脱了法网铁梏，在身后留下了一连串触目惊心的尸骸。沿河下游，警犬正在灌木丛中狂吠搜寻，眼神凶悍的男人们正端着步枪在丛林深处拉网围剿。

他们料想那恶徒走投无路之下定会寻同胞避难，因而正沿着散落的黑人聚落周遭的穷荒四处搜捕。但我比他们更了解托普·布拉克斯顿（Tope Braxton）；我深知他绝非寻常族类。他原始蛮荒得难以置信，骨子里返祖的野性足以让他一头扎进杳无人烟的荒蛮腹地，像一头嗜血发狂的大猩猩般在连同族常人都会吓破胆的死寂荒野中苟活。

因此，当追捕大军向着另一个方向涌去之际，我独自一人策马奔向了“埃及”。

===Original===

But it was not altogether to look for Tope Braxton that I plunged into that isolated fastness. My mission was one of warning, rather than search. Deep in the mazy pine labyrinth, a white man and his servant lived alone, and it was the duty of any man to warn them that a red-handed killer might be skulking about their cabin.
I was foolish, perhaps, to be traveling on foot; but men who wear the name of Garfield are not in the habit of turning back on a task once attempted. When my horse unexpectedly went lame, I left him at one of the negro cabins which fringe the edge of Egypt, and went on afoot. Night overtook me on the path, and I intended remaining until morning with the man I was going to warn﻿—Richard Brent. He was a taciturn recluse, suspicious and peculiar, but he could scarcely refuse to put me up for the night. He was a mysterious figure; why he chose to hide himself in a southern pine forest none knew. He had been living in an old cabin in the heart of Egypt for about six months.
Suddenly, as I forged through the darkness, my speculations regarding the mysterious recluse were cut short, wiped clear out of my mind. I stopped dead, the nerves tingling in the skin on the backs of my hands. A sudden shriek in the dark has that effect, and this scream was edged with agony and terror. It came from somewhere ahead of me. Breathless silence followed that cry, a silence in which the forest seemed to hold its breath and the darkness shut in more blackly still.
Again the scream was repeated, this time closer. Then I heard the pound of bare feet along the trail, and a form hurled itself at me out of the darkness.
---
My revolver was in my hand, and I instinctively thrust it out to fend the creature off. The only thing that kept me from pulling the trigger was the noise the object was making﻿—gasping, sobbing noises of fear and pain. It was a man, and direly stricken. He blundered full into me, shrieked again, and fell sprawling, slobbering and yammering.
“Oh, my God, save me! Oh, God have mercy on me!”
“What the devil is it?” I demanded, my hair stirring on my scalp at the poignant agony in the gibbering voice.
The wretch recognized my voice; he clawed at my knees.
“Oh, Mas’ Kirby, don’ let him tetch me! He’s done killed my body, and now he wants my soul! It’s me﻿—po’ Jim Tike. Don’ let him git me!”

===Chinese===

然而，我孤身涉足这处遗世独立的险绝之地，并不全是为了搜寻托普·布拉克斯顿。比起搜捕，我的使命更在于示警。在迷宫般错综复杂的松林深处，一位白人男子与其仆从孤居一隅；告诫他们某个满手血腥的凶手可能正潜伏在其木屋四周，是任何血性汉子义不容辞的本分。

徒步夜行或许显得莽撞；但凡是顶着加菲尔德（Garfield）之名的人，向来没有遇事退缩的习惯。当我的马匹意外跛足之后，我便将它留在“埃及”边缘的一处黑人窝棚里，只身步行深入。夜幕在途中骤然降临，我本打算去那间向其示警的木屋歇宿至黎明——屋主理查德·布伦特（Richard Brent）是个沉默寡言的隐士，多疑孤僻、性情古怪，但他总不至于将我拒之门外。此人是个谜一般的人物，究竟为何偏要将自己隐匿在南方的松林深处，无人知晓。他在这处位于“埃及”腹地的老旧木屋里，已然居住了约莫六个月。

正当我在漆黑中疾行之际，对那位神秘隐士的揣度猛然被打断，瞬间被震得烟消云散。我倏地止步，手背上的汗毛根根倒竖。黑暗中骤然炸响的一声凄厉尖叫往往便有这般魔力，而这声嘶喊更是浸透了撕心裂肺的剧痛与绝望。叫声来自我正前方的某处。凄厉的哭嚎过后，便是一片令人窒息的死寂，整座森林仿佛都屏住了呼吸，黑暗愈发如铁幕般沉沉压下。

尖叫声再度撕裂夜空，这一次离我更近了。紧接着，我听到了赤足沿小道狂奔的急促声响，一道黑影自黑暗中猛然朝我合身撞来。

---

我的左轮手枪已然拔在手中，下意识抬起枪口以阻挡来物。唯一让我未曾扣动扳机的，是那东西口中发出的声响——那是因恐惧与剧痛而急促倒抽冷气、抽噎呜咽的人声。那是一个人，且受了致命重创。他一头撞进我的怀里，再次尖声痛嚎，随即四仰八叉摔倒在地，口水横流，语无伦次地啼哭求告。

“天啊，救救我！求上帝垂怜我！”

“到底怎么回事？”我厉声喝问，那呓语般的话音中透出的蚀骨剧痛，令我头皮发麻。

那可怜虫辨认出了我的声音，伸手死死抠住我的膝头。

“哎呀，柯尔比少爷（Mas’ Kirby），千万别让他碰着我！他已经害了我的肉身，如今还要夺我的魂魄！是我啊——可怜的吉姆·泰克（Jim Tike）！别让他捉住我啊！”

===Original===

I struck a match, and stood staring in amazement, while the match burned down to my fingers. A black man groveled in the dust before me, his eyes rolling up whitely. I knew him well﻿—one of the negroes who lived in their tiny log cabins along the fringe of Egypt. He was spotted and splashed with blood, and I believed he was mortally wounded. Only abnormal energy rising from frenzied panic could have enabled him to run as far as he had. Blood jetted from torn veins and arteries in breast, shoulder and neck, and the wounds were ghastly to see, great ragged tears, that were never made by bullet or knife. One ear had been torn from his head, and hung loose, with a great piece of flesh from the angle of his jaw and neck, as if some gigantic beast had ripped it out with his fangs.
“What in God’s name did this?” I ejaculated as the match went out, and he became merely an indistinct blob in the darkness below me. “A bear?” Even as I spoke I knew that no bear had been seen in Egypt for thirty years.
“*He* done it!” The thick, sobbing mumble welled up through the dark. “De white man dat come by my cabin and ask me to guide him to Mistuh Brent’s house. He said he had a toothache, so he had his head bandaged; but de bandages slipped and I seen his face﻿—he killed me for seein’ him.”
“You mean he set dogs on you?” I demanded, for his wounds were such as I have seen on animals worried by vicious hounds.
“No, suh,” whimpered the ebbing voice. “He done it hisself﻿—aaaggghhh!”
The mumble broke in a shriek as he twisted his head, barely visible in the gloom, and stared back the way he had come. Death must have struck him in the midst of that scream, for it broke short at the highest note. He flopped convulsively once, like a dog hit by a truck, and then lay still.
I strained my eyes into the darkness, and made out a vague shape a few yards away in the trail. It was erect and tall as a man; it made no sound.
I opened my mouth to challenge the unknown visitant, but no sound came. An indescribable chill flowed over me, freezing my tongue to my palate. It was fear, primitive and unreasoning, and even while I stood paralyzed I could not understand it, could not guess why that silent, motionless figure, sinister as it was, should rouse such instinctive dread.
Then suddenly the figure moved quickly toward me, and I found my voice. “Who comes there?”

===Chinese===

我划亮了一根火柴，满心骇异地僵立凝视，任由火苗直烧到指尖。一个黑人男子蜷伏在我脚下的泥土中，双眼翻白。我认得他——他是住在“埃及”边缘简陋木屋里的黑人之一。他浑身血迹斑斑，溅满污血，我断定他已受了致命伤。唯有源自歇斯底里恐慌的非人潜能，才能支撑着他一口气狂奔至此。鲜血从胸膛、肩膀与脖颈上撕裂的血管与动脉中汩汩喷涌，那些伤口触目惊心，皮肉翻卷撕裂，绝非子弹或刀刃所能造成。他的一只耳朵被硬生生从头上撕脱下来，耷拉在旁，连同下颌角与颈项处的一大块皮肉一同被硬生生扯掉，仿佛被某种巨兽用森森利齿活生生撕扯出来的一般。

“看在上帝的份上，这究竟是何物所伤？”火柴熄灭之际我失声惊呼，脚下的他在浓重黑暗中重新沦为一团模糊不清的黑影。“是熊吗？”话一出口我便意识到，三十年（30年）来，“埃及”境内从未有人见过熊的踪迹。

“是他干的！”浓浊呜咽的低语自黑暗中翻涌而起，“那个来到我窝棚、求我带他去布伦特先生屋里的白人。他说他牙痛，所以头上裹着厚厚的绷带；可绷带松脱了，我瞧见了他的真面目——他因为我撞破他的长相，就要杀我灭口。”

“你是说他放狗咬你？”我追问道，因他身上的撕裂伤口与我曾见过的被恶犬撕咬的牲畜伤痕如出一辙。

“不，老爷，”微弱下去的声音抽泣呻吟道，“是他自个儿咬的——啊啊啊！”

那含混的低语化作一声尖利的惨叫，他在幽暗中猛地扭过近乎看不真切的头颅，骇然望向自个儿逃来的路途。死神定是在这声尖嚎中夺去了他的性命，因那凄厉的嘶喊在最高亢处戛然而止。他像被重型卡车撞飞的猎犬般剧烈抽搐了一下，随即彻底瘫软不动。

我极力向黑暗深处极目望去，隐约辨认出前方几码外的小道上伫立着一个模糊的轮廓。那身影高大修长，宛如人形，昂然挺立，未发半点声息。

我张口欲喝问这位不速之客，却喉头哽咽，发不出半点声音。一股难以言状的彻骨森寒自周身漫延开来，将我的舌头死死冻结在上颚。那是毫无缘由的原始恐惧；甚至在我僵立麻痹之际，我仍无法参透，为何眼前那具一动不动、虽显阴森的身影，竟能勾起如此本能的绝望恐惧。

骤然间，那道黑影迅疾向我逼近，我终于找回了自己的声音：“是谁在那儿？”

===Original===

No answer; but the form came on in a rush, and as I groped for a match, it was almost upon me. I struck the match﻿—with a ferocious snarl the figure hurled itself against me, the match was struck from my hand and extinguished, and I felt a sharp pain on the side of my neck. My gun exploded almost involuntarily and without aim, and its flash dazzled me, obscuring rather than revealing the tall manlike figure that struck at me; then with a crashing rush through the trees my assailant was gone, and I staggered alone on the forest trail.
Swearing angrily, I felt for another match. Blood was trickling down my shoulder, soaking through my shirt. When I struck the match and investigated, another chill swept down my spine. My shirt was torn and the flesh beneath slightly cut; the wound was little more than a scratch, but the thing that roused nameless fear in my mind was the fact that *the wound was similar to those on poor Jim Tike*.

===Chinese===

没有回答；但那黑影已然狂冲而至，正当我伸手去摸火柴之际，它几乎已扑面而来。我划亮了火柴——伴随着一声狂暴凶残的咆哮，那身影猛然撞向我，火柴被瞬间拍落熄灭，我只觉脖颈一侧传来一阵撕裂般的锐痛。我的枪几乎是在下意识中不经瞄准便轰然走火，枪口喷吐的火光刺得我双眼发花，不仅未能照亮那个扑向我的高大人形，反倒遮蔽了视线；紧接着，伴随着树木被疯狂撞断的轰鸣，袭击者已然狂奔远遁，唯留我一人在幽暗的林间小道上踉跄喘息。

我愤怒地咒骂着，摸索着点亮了另一根火柴。鲜血正顺着肩头缓缓淌落，浸透了我的衬衣。当我借着火柴的光亮仔细查验时，又一阵彻骨的寒意顺着脊梁骨骤然滑过。衬衣已被撕裂，底下的皮肉被划开了一条口子；伤口虽不过是一道浅浅的皮外划痕，但真正让我心底升起莫名恐惧的，是这样一个事实——这道伤痕，竟与可怜的吉姆·泰克身上那触目惊心的撕裂齿痕如出一辙。

## Dig Me No Grave / 莫掘我坟
===Original===

The thunder of my old-fashioned doorknocker, reverberating eerily through the house, roused me from a restless and nightmare-haunted sleep. I looked out the window. In the last light of the sinking moon, the white face of my friend John Conrad looked up at me.
“May I come up, Kirowan?” His voice was shaky and strained.
“Certainly!” I sprang out of bed and pulled on a bathrobe as I heard him enter the front door and ascend the stairs.
A moment later he stood before me, and in the light which I had turned on I saw his hands tremble and noticed the unnatural pallor of his face.
“Old John Grimlan died an hour ago,” he said abruptly.
“Indeed? I had not known that he was ill.”
“It was a sudden, virulent attack of peculiar nature, a sort of seizure somewhat akin to epilepsy. He has been subject to such spells of late years, you know.”
I nodded. I knew something of the old hermit-like man who had lived in his great dark house on the hill; indeed, I had once witnessed one of his strange seizures, and I had been appalled at the writhings, howlings and yammerings of the wretch, who had groveled on the earth like a wounded snake, gibbering terrible curses and black blasphemies until his voice broke in a wordless screaming which spattered his lips with foam. Seeing this, I understood why people in old times looked on such victims as men possessed by demons.
“﻿—﻿—﻿some hereditary taint,” Conrad was saying. “Old John doubtless fell heir to some ingrown weakness brought on by some loathsome disease, which was his heritage from perhaps a remote ancestor﻿—such things occasionally happen. Or else﻿—well, you know old John himself pried about in the mysterious parts of the earth, and wandered all over the East in his younger days. It is quite possible that he was infected with some obscure malady in his wanderings. There are still many unclassified diseases in Africa and the Orient.”
“But,” said I, “you have not told me the reason for this sudden visit at this unearthly hour﻿—for I notice that it is past midnight.”
My friend seemed rather confused.
“Well, the fact is that John Grimlan died alone, except for myself.

===Chinese===

古式门环雷鸣般的撞击声在整座宅邸中阴森回荡，将我从辗转反侧、噩梦缠身的残睡中猛然惊醒。我探身望向窗外。在斜沉残月的最后一抹清辉下，好友约翰·康拉德（John Conrad）惨白的面庞正仰面凝视着我。

“基罗万（Kirowan），我能上去吗？”他的嗓音颤抖而紧绷。

“当然！”我翻身下床，披上一件晨袍，耳畔已传来他推开前门、拾级而上的急促脚步声。

片刻之后，他已伫立在我面前。借着按亮的灯光，我瞧见他的双手不住战栗，脸上泛着一股异样的死白。

“老约翰·格里姆兰（John Grimlan）一个小时前断气了，”他突兀地开口道。

“当真？我竟不知他染了恶疾。”

“发作得极为凶险骤烈，症状古怪之极，宛如某种癫痫暴发。你知道的，这几年他时常遭受这般怪症折磨。”

我微微颔首。对那个幽居在山巅幽暗巨宅中的孤僻老怪，我略有所知；事实上，我曾亲眼目睹过他的一次诡异发作，那可怜虫在地上宛如受伤的毒蛇般痛苦翻滚、凄厉嚎叫与疯狂抽搐的惨状，曾令我毛骨悚然；他嘴里含混地喷吐着可怖的诅咒与幽暗的亵渎之词，直至嗓音嘶哑化作不成声的狂嚎，嘴角溅满白沫。目睹此景，我方才体悟，为何古人会将这般受害者视作恶魔附体之人。

“……某种家族遗传的宿疾，”康拉德续道，“老约翰多半是从某个遥远的先祖那里，继承了由某种可憎秽疾所致的根深蒂固暗疾——世间偶有此类孽缘。又或者——哎，你也晓得，老约翰早年酷爱在世间神秘莫测的幽暗角落窥探钻营，年轻时踏遍了整个东方。极有可能是在流浪途中染上了某种晦涩难明的异疾。在非洲与东方，至今仍有许多未曾分类的诡疾。”

“然而，”我说道，“你尚未告诉我，何以在这般深更半夜的诡异时分骤然造访——我瞧着眼下早已过了子夜。”

我的友人显得颇为局促不安。

“实不相瞒，约翰·格里姆兰临终之时，身旁除了我，别无他人。

===Original===

He refused to receive any medical aid of any sort, and in the last few moments when it was evident that he was dying, and I was prepared to go for some sort of help in spite of him, he set up such a howling and screaming that I could not refuse his passionate pleas﻿—which were that he should not be left to die alone.
“I have seen men die,” added Conrad, wiping the perspiration from his pale brow, “but the death of John Grimlan was the most fearful I have ever seen.”
“He suffered a great deal?”
“He appeared to be in much physical agony, but this was mostly submerged by some monstrous mental or psychic suffering. The fear in his distended eyes and his screams transcended any conceivable earthly terror. I tell you, Kirowan, Grimlan’s fright was greater and deeper than the ordinary fear of the Beyond shown by a man of ordinarily evil life.”
I shifted restlessly. The dark implications of this statement sent a chill of nameless apprehension trickling down my spine.
“I know the country people always claimed that in his youth he sold his soul to the Devil, and that his sudden epileptic attacks were merely a visible sign of the Fiend’s power over him; but such talk is foolish, of course, and belongs in the Dark Ages. We all know that John Grimlan’s life was a peculiarly evil and vicious one, even toward his last days. With good reason he was universally detested and feared, for I never heard of his doing a single good act. You were his only friend.”
“And that was a strange friendship,” said Conrad. “I was attracted to him by his unusual powers, for despite his bestial nature, John Grimlan was a highly educated man, a deeply cultured man. He had dipped deep into occult studies, and I first met him in this manner; for as you know, I have always been strongly interested in these lines of research myself.
“But, in this as in all other things, Grimlan was evil and perverse. He had ignored the white side of the occult and delved into the darker, grimmer phases of it﻿—into devil-worship, and voodoo and Shintoism. His knowledge of these foul arts and sciences was immense and unholy. And to hear him tell of his researches and experiments was to know such horror and repulsion as a venomous reptile might inspire. For there had been no depths to which he had not sunk, and some things he only hinted at, even to me.

===Chinese===

“他严词拒绝接受任何医疗救助。在最后的时刻，眼见他大限将至，我本打算不顾其阻拦外出求援，他却骤然爆发出凄厉至极的号哭与狂叫，苦苦哀求我万万不可弃他一人孤独咽气——面对那般撕心裂肺的哀恳，我实在不忍离去。

“我曾见过旁人咽气，”康拉德揩去苍白额头上渗出的冷汗，补充道，“但约翰·格里姆兰的死相，却是我生平所见最为可怖的景象。”

“他受了极大的肉体折磨？”

“肉体上的苦痛固然剧烈，但那痛苦的大半，却被某种庞大畸形的心智与灵魂煎熬彻底吞没。他暴突的双目中所流露出的恐惧，以及那一声声惨嚎，超越了凡间所能想象的一切可怖。我敢对你说，基罗万，格里姆兰心中的骇惧，远甚于一个作恶多端的凡夫俗子面对冥界彼岸时所显露的寻常恐惧。”

我不安地挪动了下身子。这番话背后的阴暗寓意，令一股莫名的透骨恶寒顺着我的脊梁悄然滑过。

“我深知乡民向来声称他在青年时代便将灵魂出卖给了魔鬼，他那突如其来的癫痫发作，不过是魔王对其掌控的显形征兆；但这等妄谈自是荒诞不经，不过是中世纪的愚夫愚妇之见。我们皆知约翰·格里姆兰平生行事极度邪恶乖戾，乃至临终之年亦未有收敛。世人对他普遍憎恶畏惧，并非无因，因我从未听闻他行过一件善事。你乃是他唯一的友人。”

“而那也是一段古怪莫名的交情，”康拉德说，“吸引我接近他的，是他那异乎寻常的能耐。因尽管兽性未泯，约翰·格里姆兰却是一个学识极为渊博、修养深厚之人。他在神秘学（occult）领域钻研极深，我与他初识亦正因于此——如你所知，我自己向来对这些探索抱有极浓厚的兴致。

“然而，在此事上亦如他在万事中的做派一般，格里姆兰深陷邪佞与悖谬。他弃绝了神秘学的白魔法光明一面，一头扎进其更为幽暗凶险的泥沼——拜魔教、伏都巫术与异端祭仪。他对这些污秽术法与奥义的知晓浩瀚而亵渎。每当听他讲述那些钻研与秘仪实验，心头涌起的惊骇与厌恶，恰似面对剧毒蝰蛇时一般无二。因这世间再无他未曾涉足的堕落深渊，甚至即便对我，有些隐秘他也仅是隐晦提及。

===Original===

I tell you, Kirowan, it is easy to laugh at tales of the black world of the unknown, when one is in pleasant company under the bright sunlight, but had you sat at ungodly hours in the silent bizarre library of John Grimlan and looked on the ancient musty volumes and listened to his grisly talk as I did, your tongue would have cloven to your palate with sheer horror as mine did, and the supernatural would have seemed very real and near to you﻿—as it seemed to me!”
“But in God’s name, man!” I cried, for the tension was growing unbearable; “come to the point and tell me what you want of me.”
“I want you to come with me to John Grimlan’s house and help carry out his outlandish instructions in regard to his body.”
---
I had no liking for the adventure, but I dressed hurriedly, an occasional shudder of premonition shaking me. Once fully clad, I followed Conrad out of the house and up the silent road which led to the house of John Grimlan. The road wound uphill, and all the way, looking upward and forward, I could see that great grim house perched like a bird of evil on the crest of the hill, bulking black and stark against the stars. In the west pulsed a single dull red smear where the young moon had just sunk from view behind the low black hills. The whole night seemed full of brooding evil, and the persistent swishing of a bat’s wings somewhere overhead caused my taut nerves to jerk and thrum. To drown the quick pounding of my own heart, I said:
“Do you share the belief so many hold, that John Grimlan was mad?”
We strode on several paces before Conrad answered, seemingly with a strange reluctance, “But for one incident, I would say no man was ever saner. But one night in his study, he seemed suddenly to break all bonds of reason.
“He had discoursed for hours on his favorite subject﻿—black magic﻿—when suddenly he cried, as his face lit with a weird unholy glow: ‘Why should I sit here babbling such child’s prattle to you? These voodoo rituals﻿—these Shinto sacrifices﻿—feathered snakes﻿—goats without horns﻿—black leopard cults﻿—bah! Filth and dust that the wind blows away! Dregs of the real Unknown﻿—the deep mysteries! Mere echoes from the Abyss!
“ ‘I could tell you things that would shatter your paltry brain! I could breathe into your ear names that would wither you like a burnt weed! What do you know of Yog-Sothoth, of Kathulos and the sunken cities?

===Chinese===

“我告诉你，基罗万，当一个人在明媚阳光下身处良朋益友之间，大可对未知幽冥世界的传闻付之一笑；但倘若你曾在那不敬神明的阴森时辰，坐在约翰·格里姆兰那死寂而怪诞的书斋之中，目睹那些散发着霉烂气息的古老秘卷，倾听他那些令人毛骨悚然的惨白秘辛，你的舌头便会如我一般因纯粹的极度惊骇而僵死在上颚，超自然之力便会如真似幻、近在咫尺——恰如我所真切感受到的那般！”

“看在上帝的份上，我的老兄！”我高声呼喊，因屋内的紧绷压抑已到了令人窒息的边缘，“切入正题吧，告诉我你究竟要我做什么。”

“我要你同我一道前往约翰·格里姆兰的宅邸，协助执行他关于遗体处置的那份荒诞绝伦的遗嘱指示。”

---

我对这趟凶险之行全无好感，但仍匆匆穿戴齐整，心头不时泛起阵阵凶兆的不祥战栗。穿戴妥当后，我跟随康拉德走出宅门，踏上了通往约翰·格里姆兰宅邸的死寂山路。道路蜿蜒盘旋而上，一路上抬头前望，只见那座庞大阴森的黑宅如同一只不祥的凶禽般盘踞在山巅之上，在满天繁星的映衬下显得庞大而狰狞。在西方的天际线上，仅残留着一抹暗红色的污浊残痕，那是初升不久的弯月刚刚沉入低矮黑山背后的余晖。整座夜幕仿佛充斥着沉郁酝酿的凶邪，头顶某处蝙蝠双翼持续不断的扑棱声，引得我紧绷的神经剧烈抽搐战栗。为了压下自己狂乱的心跳，我开口道：

“你也赞同许多人的看法，认为约翰·格里姆兰早已发疯了吗？”

我们默默疾行了数步，康拉德方才带着一股异样的迟疑低声答道：“若非经历过那一桩事，我会说世间无人比他更为清醒理智。然而某一晚在他的书斋里，他似乎骤然挣断了全部理性的藩篱。

“他滔滔不绝地讲论了他最痴迷的课题——黑魔法，足足讲了数个小时；突然间，他的面庞泛起一抹诡异亵渎的幽光，狂呼道：‘我何必坐在此处对你喋喋不休吐露这般小儿科的呓语？这些伏都仪轨——这些神道献祭——羽蛇神——无角山羊——黑豹密教——呸！皆不过是风卷残云般的污秽尘埃！不过是真正未知的残渣渣滓——真正深渊秘辛的微弱回响！

“‘我若吐露真言，足以将你浅薄的心智碾成齑粉！我若将那些尊名吹入你的耳畔，顷刻便能令你如焦枯败草般枯萎凋零！对于犹格-索托斯（Yog-Sothoth）、卡图洛斯（Kathulos）以及沉没地底的太古诸城，你又究竟懂得几分？

===Original===

None of these names is even included in your mythologies. Not even in your dreams have you glimpsed the black cyclopean walls of Koth, or shriveled before the noxious winds that blow from Yuggoth!
“ ‘But I will not blast you lifeless with my black wisdom! I cannot expect your infantile brain to bear what mine holds. Were you as old as I﻿—had you seen, as I have seen, kingdoms crumble and generations pass away﻿—had you gathered as ripe grain the dark secrets of the centuries﻿—’
“He was raving away, his wildly lit face scarcely human in appearance, and suddenly, noting my evident bewilderment, he burst into a horrible cackling laugh.
“ ‘Gad!’ he cried in a voice and accent strange to me, ‘methinks I’ve frighted ye, and certes, it is not to be marveled at, sith ye be but a naked savage in the arts of life, after all. Ye think I be old, eh? Why, ye gaping lout, ye’d drop dead were I to divulge the generations of men I’ve known﻿—’
“But at this point such horror overcame me that I fled from him as from an adder, and his high-pitched, diabolical laughter followed me out of the shadowy house. Some days later I received a letter apologizing for his manner and ascribing it candidly﻿—too candidly﻿—to drugs. I did not believe it, but I renewed our relations, after some hesitation.”
“It sounds like utter madness,” I muttered.
“Yes,” admitted Conrad, hesitantly. “But﻿—Kirowan, have you ever seen anyone who knew John Grimlan in his youth?”
I shook my head.
“I have been at pains to inquire about him discreetly,” said Conrad. “He has lived here﻿—with the exception of mysterious absences often for months at a time﻿—for twenty years. The older villagers remember distinctly when he first came and took over that old house on the hill, and they all say that in the intervening years he seems not to have aged perceptibly. When he came here he looked just as he does now﻿—or did, up to the moment of his death﻿—of the appearance of a man about fifty.
“I met old Von Boehnk in Vienna, who said he knew Grimlan when a very young man studying in Berlin, fifty years ago, and he expressed astonishment that the old man was still living; for he said at that time Grimlan seemed to be about fifty years of age.”
I gave an incredulous exclamation, seeing the implication toward which the conversation was trending.
“Nonsense!

===Chinese===

“‘凡此种种尊名，甚至未曾收录于你们凡俗的伪造神话之中。甚至在梦魇深处，你也未曾窥见过科斯（Koth）那漆黑巍峨的巨石城垣，未曾在掠过犹格斯星（Yuggoth）的毒煞阴风前战栗枯萎！

“‘但我绝不会用我那幽暗的无上奥义将你轰杀得生机尽绝！我不能指望你那婴孩般的羸弱大脑能承受我颅中所蕴的狂澜。倘若你年岁与我一般古老——倘若你曾如我一般，亲眼目睹王朝日落、一代代生民化作过眼云烟——倘若你曾将千百年来的漆黑秘辛如成熟稻谷般收入囊中——’

“他这般狂乱狂吠着，那张被狂暴幽光照亮的面孔几乎已看不出半分人形；骤然间，他瞥见了我脸上显而易见的惊骇困惑，猛然爆发出一阵令人毛骨悚然的桀桀怪笑。

“‘天哪！’他用一种对我而言陌生之极的腔调与口音狂叫道，‘依老夫之见，我着实将汝吓得不轻，然细思之亦不足为奇，盖因论及造化之玄机，汝终究不过一赤身裸体的蛮夷罢了。汝道老夫年迈，哈？无知蠢汉，老夫若将所结识的一代代先人名讳吐露，汝顷刻便当暴毙当场——’

“然而至此，一股无边的极度恐惧将我彻底吞没，我如避长吻毒蛇般仓皇从他身旁逃遁，而他那尖细如魔鬼般的狞笑声，一路穿透重重阴影追逐着我逃出宅邸。数日之后，我收到了一封致歉信，信中为他的失态百般开脱，并将之坦率——过于坦率——归咎于麻醉药物的作用。我对此自是不信，但在迟疑再三之后，终究恢复了与他的往来。”

“听上去纯粹是一派彻头彻尾的疯话，”我喃喃低语道。

“正是，”康拉德迟疑着承认道，“可是——基罗万，你平生可曾遇见过任何知晓约翰·格里姆兰青年时代之人？”

我摇了摇头。

“我曾费尽心机暗中多方探寻，”康拉德说，“除了时常动辄数月行踪成谜的消失之外，他在此地已居住了二十年。村里的老人们清楚记得他当年初次到来并买下山顶那栋老宅的情景；他们异口同声地说，在漫长的岁月中，他竟未显露出丝毫衰老的痕迹。当年他初来乍到时是何等模样，如今——直至咽气之时——便依旧是何等模样，始终保持着年约五十岁上下的容貌。

“我曾在维也纳遇到过老冯·伯恩克教授（Professor Von Boehnk），他说五十年前在柏林求学时，便曾结识过极为年轻的格里姆兰，而他对这老怪居然尚在人世深表骇异；因他直言，彼时格里姆兰看上去便已然年约五十岁了。”

我难以置信地倒抽了一口凉气，瞬间洞悉了这番交谈所引向的骇人渊薮。

“荒谬绝伦！

===Original===

Professor Von Boehnk is past eighty himself, and liable to the errors of extreme age. He confused this man with another.” Yet as I spoke, my flesh crawled unpleasantly and the hairs on my neck prickled.
“Well,” shrugged Conrad, “here we are at the house.”
---
The huge pile reared up menacingly before us, and as we reached the front door a vagrant wind moaned through the nearby trees and I started foolishly as I again heard the ghostly beat of the bat’s wings. Conrad turned a large key in the antique lock, and as we entered, a cold draft swept across us like a breath from the grave﻿—moldy and cold. I shuddered.
We groped our way through a black hallway and into a study, and here Conrad lighted a candle, for no gas lights or electric lights were to be found in the house. I looked about me, dreading what the light might disclose, but the room, heavily tapestried and bizarrely furnished, was empty save for us two.
“Where﻿—where is﻿—*It*?” I asked in a husky whisper, from a throat gone dry.
“Upstairs,” answered Conrad in a low voice, showing that the silence and mystery of the house had laid a spell on him also. “Upstairs, in the library where he died.”
I glanced up involuntarily. Somewhere above our head, the lone master of this grim house was stretched out in his last sleep﻿—silent, his white face set in a grinning mask of death. Panic swept over me and I fought for control. After all, it was merely the corpse of a wicked old man, who was past harming anyone﻿—this argument rang hollowly in my brain like the words of a frightened child who is trying to reassure himself.
I turned to Conrad. He had taken a time-yellowed envelope from an inside pocket.
“This,” he said, removing from the envelope several pages of closely written, time-yellowed parchment, “is, in effect, the last word of John Grimlan, though God alone knows how many years ago it was written. He gave it to me ten years ago, immediately after his return from Mongolia. It was shortly after this that he had his first seizure.
“This envelope he gave me, sealed, and he made me swear that I would hide it carefully, and that I would not open it until he was dead, when I was to read the contents and follow their directions exactly. More, he made me swear that no matter what he said or did after giving me the envelope, I would go ahead as first directed.

===Chinese===

“冯·伯恩克教授自己都年过八旬，极易犯下风烛残年的昏聩错谬。他定是将旁人与此人张冠李戴了。”然而话音未落，我周身的皮肉便泛起一阵极不舒服的痉挛，颈后的寒毛根根直竖。

“罢了，”康拉德耸了耸肩，“我们已然到宅子跟前了。”

---

那座庞大的建筑群如巨兽般凶险地耸立在我们面前。正当我们行至正门前时，一股无名怪风在周遭林木间发出凄厉呜咽，而当我再次听闻蝙蝠双翼扑棱的鬼祟声响时，竟不由自主地打了个寒战。康拉德将一柄大钥匙插入古旧门锁中拧转，随着我们迈入房门，一股阴冷潮湿的气流如墓穴深处的森然尸气般扑面而来——带着腐朽发霉的彻骨寒意。我不禁浑身剧烈颤抖。

我们摸索着穿过漆黑的长廊，步入一间书房；康拉德在此划亮了一根蜡烛，因整栋大宅中寻不见半点煤气灯或电灯的踪影。我警惕地环顾四周，心中极度畏惧烛光所可能照出的凶邪之物；然而这间挂着厚重织锦挂毯、陈设诡异的书房里，除了我们二人之外空无一人。

“那……那具……‘尸身’在何处？”我喉头干渴，用沙哑得几乎变调的耳语问道。

“在楼上，”康拉德低声应道，显见这宅邸的死寂与诡谲同样将他紧紧笼罩，“在楼上他咽气的藏书室里。”

我不由自主地抬头仰望。就在我们头顶上方的某处，这栋凶宅的独居主人正僵直地躺在最后的死寂长眠之中——悄无声息，惨白的面孔凝固成一副狰狞怪笑的死亡面具。一股恐慌潮水般将我淹没，我拼命克制着情绪。说到底，那不过是一具行将就木的邪恶老翁遗蜕，再无伤人之力——这般辩解在我脑海中空洞回响，恰似受惊孩童为了自我宽慰而喃喃自语的荒唐胡话。

我转向康拉德。他从内衬口袋中掏出了一封岁月泛黄的信封。

“这，”他抽出了信封中数张字迹密密麻麻、因年深日久而焦黄的羊皮纸页，“实际上便是约翰·格里姆兰的绝笔，天晓得这是在多少年前写就的。那是十年前他刚自蒙古探险归来后不久亲手交予我的。正是在那之后不久，他遭遇了平生第一次诡异发作。

“他将这密封的信封郑重交到我手中，令我起誓必将其妥善隐匿，在他未死之前绝不可拆阅；唯有在他死后，我方可开启信封，研读其中的字句，并分毫不差地遵照其中的指令行事。不仅如此，他还逼我起誓，无论他在交付信封之后再言说了何等言辞、做出了何等举动，我都必须一如既往、坚定不移地照最初的叮嘱去办。

===Original===

‘For,’ he said with a fearful smile, ‘the flesh is weak but I am a man of my word, and though I might, in a moment of weakness, wish to retract, it is far, far too late now. You may never understand the matter, but you are to do as I have said.’ ”
“Well?”
“Well,” again Conrad wiped his brow, “tonight as he lay writhing in his death-agonies, his wordless howls were mingled with frantic admonitions to me to bring him the envelope and destroy it before his eyes! As he yammered this, he forced himself up on his elbows and with eyes starting and hair standing straight up on his head, he screamed at me in a manner to chill the blood. And he was shrieking for me to destroy the envelope, not to open it; and once he howled in his delirium for me to hew his body into pieces and scatter the bits to the four winds of heaven!”
An uncontrollable exclamation of horror escaped my dry lips.
“At last,” went on Conrad, “I gave in. Remembering his commands ten years ago, I at first stood firm, but at last, as his screeches grew unbearably desperate, I turned to go for the envelope, even though that meant leaving him alone. But as I turned, with one last fearful convulsion in which blood-flecked foam flew from his writhing lips, the life went from his twisted body in a single great wrench.”
He fumbled at the parchment.
“I am going to carry out my promise. The directions herein seem fantastic and may be the whims of a disordered mind, but I gave my word. They are, briefly, that I place his corpse on the great black ebony table in his library, with seven black candles burning about him. The doors and windows are to be firmly closed and fastened. Then, in the darkness which precedes dawn, I am to read the formula, charm or spell which is contained in a smaller, sealed envelope inside the first, and which I have not yet opened.”
“But is that all?” I cried. “No provisions as to the disposition of his fortune his estate﻿—or his corpse?”
“Nothing. In his will, which I have seen elsewhere, he leaves estate and fortune to a certain oriental gentleman named in the document as﻿—Malik Tous!”
“What!” I cried, shaken to my soul. “Conrad, this is madness heaped on madness! Malik Tous﻿—good God! No mortal man was ever so named!

===Chinese===

“‘盖因，’他当年带着一抹令人毛骨悚然的惨笑说道，‘肉体终归羸弱，而老夫乃一诺千金之人，纵使在意志薄弱之际欲生悔意，如今亦悔之晚矣、太迟太迟了。汝或许终生不能参透其中奥义，但唯须谨遵老夫所嘱行事。’”

“后来呢？”

“后来，”康拉德再次揩拭额角冷汗，“今夜当他躺在床上遭受濒死剧痛的狂乱撕扯之际，那不成语调的惨嚎声中，竟夹杂着绝望发狂的凄厉喝令，逼我立刻将信封取来并在他眼前焚毁！他一边语无伦次地嚎叫着，一边用手肘死死撑起身躯，眼球暴突，满头长发根根倒竖，用足以冻结周身骨血的凄厉嗓音冲我咆哮！他发疯般尖叫着要我毁掉信封，万万不可启封；在神智狂乱之际，他甚至狂嚎着要我将他的尸骸剁成肉泥碎块，抛洒给九天罡风！”

一声无法抑制的极度惊骇呼喊从我干裂的双唇间脱口而出。

“最终，”康拉德续道，“我屈服了。念及他十年前的严令，我起初尚自强撑咬牙坚持；但最终，随着他的尖叫愈发绝望惨烈至令人无法忍受，我终于转身欲去取信，哪怕这意味着将他一人孤零零留在房中。然而就在我转身之际，伴随着最后一次可怖的剧烈痉挛，血沫自他抽搐扭曲的双唇间狂喷而出，生机便在这一记剧烈扭曲中自他扭曲的残躯中彻底断绝。”

他的手指在羊皮纸上不安地摩挲。

“我打算履行自己的誓言。这上头的指示看似荒诞不经，或许不过是一个神智错乱之人的疯癫臆想，但我既已许下诺言。简而言之，他要求我将其遗体安放在藏书室那张巨大的黑檀木长桌上，周遭环绕点燃七支黑色蜡烛。所有门窗皆须紧紧封闭锁死。随后，在黎明前的至暗时刻，我须诵读内层那个更小密封信封中所载的咒语符咒，那一封我至今尚不曾拆封。”

“难道仅此而已？”我惊呼道，“关于其财产田产的处置——关于其遗体的安葬——竟全无半点交待？”

“全无一字。而在我于他处得见的正式遗嘱中，他将全部庄园田产与金银家业，尽数赠予一位在文书中被指名为……玛利克·图斯（Malik Tous）的东方绅士！”

“什么！”我骇得魂飞魄散，失声尖叫，“康拉德，这简直是疯癫之上叠加疯癫！玛利克·图斯——我的老天！世间肉体凡胎何曾有过这般名号！

===Original===

That is the title of the foul god worshipped by the mysterious Yezidis﻿—they of Mount Alamout the Accursed﻿—whose Eight Brazen Towers rise in the mysterious wastes of deep Asia. His idolatrous symbol is the brazen peacock. And the Muhammadans, who hate his demon-worshipping devotees, say he is the essence of the evil of all the universes﻿—the Prince of Darkness﻿—Ahriman﻿—the old Serpent﻿—the veritable Satan! And you say Grimlan names this mythical demon in his will?”
“It is the truth,” Conrad’s throat was dry. “And look﻿—he has scribbled a strange line at the corner of this parchment: ‘Dig me no grave; I shall not need one.’ ”
Again a chill wandered down my spine.
“In God’s name,” I cried in a kind of frenzy, “let us get this incredible business over with!”
“I think a drink might help,” answered Conrad, moistening his lips. “It seems to me I’ve seen Grimlan go into this cabinet for wine﻿—” He bent to the door of an ornately carved mahogany cabinet, and after some difficulty opened it.
“No wine here,” he said disappointedly, “and if ever I felt the need of stimulants﻿—what’s this?”
---
He drew out a roll of parchment, dusty, yellowed and half covered with spiderwebs. Everything in that grim house seemed, to my nervously excited senses, fraught with mysterious meaning and import, and I leaned over his shoulder as he unrolled it.
“It’s a record of peerage,” he said, “such a chronicle of births, deaths and so forth, as the old families used to keep, in the Sixteenth Century and earlier.”
“What’s the name?” I asked.
He scowled over the dim scrawls, striving to master the faded, archaic script.
“G-r-y-m﻿—I’ve got it﻿—Grymlann, of course. It’s the records of old John’s family﻿—the Grymlanns of Toad’s-heath Manor, Suffolk﻿—what an outlandish name for an estate! Look at the last entry.”
Together we read, “John Grymlann, borne, March 10, 1630.” And then we both cried out. Under this entry was freshly written, in a strange scrawling hand, “Died, March 10, 1930.” Below this there was a seal of black wax, stamped with a strange design, something like a peacock with a spreading tail.
Conrad stared at me speechless, all the color ebbed from his face. I shook myself with the rage engendered by fear.
“It’s the hoax of a madman!” I shouted. “The stage has been set with such great care that the actors have overstepped themselves.

===Chinese===

“那乃是神秘莫测的雅兹迪人（Yezidis）——那群盘踞在受诅的阿拉穆特山（Mount Alamout）、其八座黄铜之塔矗立于遥远亚洲神秘不毛之地的信徒——所顶礼膜拜的污秽恶神尊名！其膜拜的偶像邪徽乃是一只展开孔雀尾羽的黄铜孔雀。而切齿痛恨这群拜魔邪徒的穆斯林，更是宣称他乃是全宇宙一切极恶的渊薮——黑暗王子——阿里曼（Ahriman）——古蛇——不折不扣的撒旦本尊！你竟说格里姆兰在遗嘱中指名要将万贯家财留给这尊神话中的邪神恶魔？”

“千真万确，”康拉德喉头干涸发紧，“你且看——他在羊皮纸的一角潦草涂写了一行诡异字迹：‘莫掘我坟；我绝无消受之日。’”

又一阵透骨冰寒在我脊背上蜿蜒爬过。

“看在上帝的份上，”我在一种近乎疯狂的焦灼中尖叫道，“让我们赶紧将这桩荒诞绝伦的邪事了结掉吧！”

“我想喝口烈酒或许能壮壮胆，”康拉德舔着干裂的双唇说道，“我依稀记得曾见过格里姆兰自这处酒柜中取酒……”他弯下腰拉扯一座雕花繁复的红木矮柜柜门，费了九牛二虎之力方才将其拽开。

“这里头并无半滴烈酒，”他失望至极地叹道，“眼下我正万分需要一口烈酒压惊——咦，这是何物？”

---

他自柜中抽出一卷覆满灰尘、泛黄且缠绕着层层蛛网的羊皮纸卷。在这栋阴森凶宅之中，我高度紧绷敏锐的感官觉得万物皆浸透着神秘莫测的深意与凶兆，随着他将纸卷徐徐展开，我急不可耐地探身越过他的肩头凝眸望去。

“这是一卷贵族谱系谱牒，”他说，“是十六世纪及更早以前古老显赫家族惯常记载生卒年月等渊源的家谱。”

“姓氏为何？”我追问道。

他皱眉凝视着那模糊不清的墨迹，极力辨识着那些褪色古奥的字迹。

“G-r-y-m……我认出来了——是格里姆兰（Grymlann），果然不出所料。这是老约翰的家族谱系——萨福克郡（Suffolk）蟾蜍荒原庄园的格里姆兰家族——这庄园地名何等怪诞不经！快看这最后一栏条目！”

我们二人齐声诵读：“约翰·格里姆兰（John Grymlann），生于1630年3月10日（March 10, 1630）。”刹那间，我们二人齐齐失声惊呼。就在这行条目下方，赫然以一种古怪潦草的全新笔迹新添了一行字迹：“卒于1930年3月10日（March 10, 1930）。”在这行字迹下方，印着一枚漆黑的火漆封印，其上赫然拓印着一枚诡异的徽记，酷似一只展开绚烂尾羽的孔雀。

康拉德目瞪口呆地凝视着我，脸上血色尽褪。惊恐交加之下，一股源自绝望恐惧的暴怒自我胸中喷涌而出。

“这不过是一个疯子的恶作剧！”我嘶声咆哮道，“这出戏排演得过于煞费苦心，以至于戏子们自己露了马脚！

===Original===

Whoever they are, they have heaped up so many incredible effects as to nullify them. It’s all a very stupid, very dull drama of illusion.”
And even as I spoke, icy sweat stood out on my body and I shook as with an ague. With a wordless motion Conrad turned toward the stairs, taking up a large candle from a mahogany table.
“It was understood, I suppose,” he whispered, “that I should go through with this ghastly matter alone; but I had not the moral courage, and now I’m glad I had not.”
---
A still horror brooded over the silent house as we went up the stairs. A faint breeze stole in from somewhere and set the heavy velvet hangings rustling, and I visualized stealthy taloned fingers drawing aside the tapestries, to fix red gloating eyes upon us. Once I thought I heard the indistinct clumping of monstrous feet somewhere above us, but it must have been the heavy pounding of my own heart.
The stairs debouched into a wide dark corridor, in which our feeble candle cast a faint gleam which but illuminated our pale faces and made the shadows seem darker by comparison. We stopped at a heavy door, and I heard Conrad’s breath draw in sharply as a man’s will when he braces himself physically or mentally. I involuntarily clenched my fists until the nails bit into the palms; then Conrad thrust the door open.
A sharp cry escaped his lips. The candle dropped from his nerveless fingers and went out. The library of John Grimlan was ablaze with light, though the whole house had been in darkness when we entered it.
This light came from seven black candles placed at regular intervals about the great ebony table. On this table, between the candles﻿—I had braced myself against the sight. Now in the face of the mysterious illumination and the sight of the thing on the table, my resolution nearly gave way. John Grimlan had been unlovely in life; in death he was hideous. Yes, he was hideous even though his face was mercifully covered with the same curious silken robe, which, worked in fantastic birdlike designs, covered his whole body except the crooked claw-like hands and the bare withered feet.
A strangling sound came from Conrad. “My God!” he whispered; “what is this? I laid his body out on the table and placed the candles about it, but I did not light them, nor did I place that robe over the body! And there were bedroom slippers on his feet when I left﻿—”
He halted suddenly.

===Chinese===

“无论幕后之人究竟是谁，他们堆砌了太多不可思议的玄虚桥段，反倒彻底弄巧成拙。这通通不过是一场拙劣至极、乏味透顶的障眼法闹剧！”

然而话音未落，冰冷的冷汗已透湿了我的周身，我像打摆子般筛糠般不住战栗。康拉德一言不发地自红木方桌上抄起一根大蜡烛，默默转身朝楼梯走去。

“我想，依规矩而言，”他用几不可闻的耳语低声道，“本该由我孤身一人将这桩阴森之事执行到底；但我实在没有这般孤身涉险的精神魄力，而如今我反倒庆幸自己未曾逞强。”

---

当我们缓步拾级而上之际，一种死寂的窒息恐怖如重幔般笼罩着整栋深宅。一缕若有若无的阴风不知自何处悄然拂入，吹得沉重的天鹅绒挂毯簌簌作响；在我眼前不由自主地浮现出一双生着利爪的阴森鬼手正悄然拨开挂帘，用一双贪婪猩红的凶眸死死窥视着我们。某一刹那，我仿佛听闻头顶上方隐隐传来某种庞然巨足沉重拖沓的脚步声，但那多半只是我自己狂乱剧烈的心跳轰鸣。

楼梯尽头连通着一条幽暗宽阔的走廊，我们手中微弱的烛火仅能投下一抹凄清的微光，反倒将我们毫无血色的面孔照得愈发惨白，衬托得四周阴影愈发浓稠如墨。我们在沉重的房门前驻足停步，我听见康拉德猛地倒抽了一口冷气，宛如一个人在肉体或精神上即将硬扛重击时蓄力的模样。我不由自主地死死攥紧拳头，指甲深陷掌心皮肉；紧接着，康拉德一把推开了大门。

一声尖锐的惊叫自他唇间夺眶而出。蜡烛自他失控麻痹的指间脱手坠地，瞬间熄灭。约翰·格里姆兰的藏书室中竟是一片灯火通明——然而当我们踏入这栋宅邸之时，整座房屋分明深陷无边黑暗之中！

这片刺目的光亮，源自整齐环列在那张巨大黑檀木长桌四周的七支黑色蜡烛。而在长桌之上，在烛火环伺之间——我本已做好了目睹尸骸的心理戒备；然而此刻，面对这诡异莫测的通明光照以及长桌上摆放的那具异物，我周身的坚毅险些彻底崩溃。约翰·格里姆兰生前便生得丑陋不堪；而死后的面目更是可憎至极。不错，哪怕他的面容已被那袭织绣着怪诞鸟类图案的奇异丝袍慈悲地遮掩遮蔽，依旧狰狞得令人作呕——整具丝袍包裹着他的全躯，唯独暴露出宛如鹰爪般蜷曲干瘪的双手，以及枯槁赤裸的双足。

康拉德喉中发出一阵窒息般的呜咽：“我的上帝啊！”他耳语道，“这……这究竟是何物？我先前分明将他的遗体平陈在桌上，四周摆好了蜡烛，但我绝未曾将其点燃，更未曾将这袭丝袍覆在他身上！而且我离开之时，他的脚上分明还穿着一双卧室棉拖鞋……”

他的话语骤然僵死在半空。

===Original===

We were not alone in the death-room.
At first we had not seen him, as he sat in the great armchair in a farther nook of a corner, so still that he seemed a part of the shadows cast by the heavy tapestries. As my eyes fell upon him, a violent shuddering shook me and a feeling akin to nausea racked the pit of my stomach. My first impression was of vivid, oblique yellow eyes which gazed unwinkingly at us. Then the man rose and made a deep salaam, and we saw that he was an oriental. Now when I strive to etch him clearly in my mind, I can resurrect no plain image of him. I only remember those piercing eyes and the yellow, fantastic robe he wore.
We returned his salute mechanically and he spoke in a low, refined voice, “Gentlemen, I crave your pardon! I have made so free as to light the candles﻿—shall we not proceed with the business pertaining to our mutual friend?”
He made a slight gesture toward the silent bulk on the table. Conrad nodded, evidently unable to speak. The thought flashed through our minds at the same time, that this man had also been given a sealed envelope﻿—but how had he come to the Grimlan house so quickly? John Grimlan had been dead scarcely two hours and to the best of our knowledge no one knew of his demise but ourselves. And how had he got into the locked and bolted house?
The whole affair was grotesque and unreal in the extreme. We did not even introduce ourselves or ask the stranger his name. He took charge in a matter-of-fact way, and so under the spell of horror and illusion were we that we moved dazedly, involuntarily obeying his suggestions, given us in a low, respectful tone.
I found myself standing on the left side of the table, looking across its grisly burden at Conrad. The oriental stood with arms folded and head bowed at the head of the table, nor did it then strike me as being strange that he should stand there, instead of Conrad who was to read what Grimlan had written. I found my gaze drawn to the figure worked on the breast of the stranger’s robe, in black silk﻿—a curious figure, somewhat resembling a peacock and somewhat resembling a bat, or a flying dragon. I noted with a start that the same design was worked on the robe covering the corpse.
---
The doors had been locked, the windows fastened down. Conrad, with a shaky hand, opened the inner envelope and fluttered open the parchment sheets contained therein.

===Chinese===

停尸房中并非只有我们二人。

起初我们并未察觉到他的存在，因他深坐在角落更深处的一张巨大扶手椅中，沉寂得宛如厚重织锦挂毯所投下的浓重阴影的一部分。当我的目光猛然落在他身上的刹那，一阵剧烈的痉挛狠狠撕扯着我的躯体，一股近乎作呕的反胃感自胃底疯狂翻涌。我的第一印象，是他那双狭长斜飞、精光四射的黄色眼眸，正一瞬不瞬地死死凝视着我们。紧接着，那人缓缓起身，深深行了一个东方拱手鞠躬礼（salaam），我们方才看出他是一位东方人。如今当我极力在脑海中勾勒他的清晰面容时，却无论如何也唤不起关于他的分明形象；我唯独铭记着那双洞穿人心的森然眼眸，以及他身上所披的那袭怪诞繁复的黄色长袍。

我们机械般机械地回了一礼，他随即用低沉典雅的嗓音开口道：“诸位先生，恳请宽恕小人的冒昧！在下擅自点燃了烛火——不知我们眼下是否可以继续操办关于你我这位故友的正事了？”

他微微抬手，朝着桌上那团死寂的庞大躯体示意了一下。康拉德微微颔首，显然已惊骇得喉头哽咽无法言语。与此同时，一个念头在我们二人心中闪电般划过——此人定然同样收到了一封密封的信函！然而他究竟是如何如此神速地赶赴格里姆兰宅邸的？约翰·格里姆兰断气尚不足两个小时，据我们所知，除我们二人外世上绝无第三人知晓其死讯。况且，他又是如何潜入这栋锁链紧扣、门窗闩死的凶宅之中的？

整桩事端荒诞怪诞到了极致，透着一股彻头彻尾的虚幻与非真实感。我们甚至未曾互通名姓，亦未曾询问这位陌生客人的尊姓大名。他以一种顺理成章、理所当然的姿态掌控了一切，而我们深陷在极度恐怖与魔幻的桎梏之下，浑浑噩噩地行动着，下意识遵从着他用低沉恭顺语调所给出的每一道指令。

我发觉自己正伫立在长桌左侧，隔着桌上那具令人毛骨悚然的沉重负担与康拉德遥遥相对。那位东方人双臂抱胸，垂首肃立在桌案顶端；而在彼时彼刻，由他伫立在首席主位，而非由即将诵读格里姆兰手书的康拉德执掌，竟未让我感到丝毫突兀。我不由自主地被那陌生人胸前用黑丝线绣成的一枚诡异图案深深吸引——那是一幅奇特怪诞的图腾，兼具孔雀之形，又似巨型蝙蝠或飞天魔龙。我猛然一惊，赫然发觉覆盖在死者尸骸上的那袭丝袍之上，竟刺绣着完全如出一辙的邪异纹样。

---

房门已被牢牢反锁，窗户尽皆紧扣封死。康拉德用战栗颤抖的手指拆开了内层的信封，抖开了其内所装载的羊皮纸页。

===Original===

These sheets seemed much older than those containing the instructions to Conrad, in the larger envelope. Conrad began to read in a monotonous drone which had the effect of hypnosis on the hearer; so at times the candles grew dim in my gaze and the room and its occupants swam strange and monstrous, veiled and distorted like an hallucination. Most of what he read was gibberish; it meant nothing; yet the sound of it and the archaic style of it filled me with an intolerable horror.
“To ye contract elsewhere recorded, I, John Grymlann, herebye sweare by ye Name of ye Nameless One to keep goode faithe. Wherefore do I now write in blood these wordes spoken to me in thys grim & silent chamber in ye dedde citie of Koth, whereto no mortal manne hath attained but mee. These same wordes now writ down by mee to be rede over my bodie at ye appointed tyme to fulfill my parte of ye bargain which I entered intoe of mine own free will & knowledge beinge of rite mynd & fiftie years of age this yeare of 1680, AD. Here begynneth ye incantation:
“Before manne was, ye Elder ones were, & even yet their lord dwelleth amonge ye shadows to which if a manne sette his foote he maye not turn vpon his track.”
The words merged into a barbaric gibberish as Conrad stumbled through an unfamiliar language﻿—a language faintly suggesting the Phoenician, but shuddery with the touch of a hideous antiquity beyond any remembered earthly tongue. One of the candles flickered and went out. I made a move to relight it, but a motion from the silent oriental stayed me. His eyes burned into mine, then shifted back to the still form on the table.
The manuscript had shifted back into its archaic English.
“﻿—﻿—﻿And ye mortal which gaineth to ye black citadels of Koth & speaks with ye Darke Lord whose face is hidden, for a price maye he gain hys heartes desire, ryches & knowledge beyond countinge & lyffe beyond mortal span even two hundred & fiftie yeares.”
Again Conrad’s voice trailed off into unfamiliar gutturals. Another candle went out.
“﻿—﻿—﻿Let not ye mortal flynche as ye tyme draweth nigh for payement & ye fires of Hell laye hold vpon ye vytals as the sign of reckoninge. For ye Prince of Darkness taketh hys due in ye endde & he is not to bee cozened. What ye have promised, that shall ye deliver.

===Chinese===

这些纸页看上去比大信封里盛装给康拉德指令的信纸还要古旧得多。康拉德开始用单调平缓的嗡嗡低音诵读起来，那语调对聆听者产生了一种宛如催眠般的诡异效果；渐渐地，在我模糊的视线中，烛光开始变得飘忽昏暗，整间屋宇及其中的人影开始怪诞畸形地晃动扭曲，如梦似幻、影影绰绰，宛如置身于一场光怪陆离的癫狂幻觉之中。他所诵读的大半言辞皆是不成句读的蛮荒呓语；毫无意义；然而那诡谲的音节及其古奥阴鸷的文风，却令我胸中泛起一股不可遏制的蚀骨极恐。

“对于铭刻于他处之契约，我，约翰·格里姆兰（John Grymlann），谨以此向无名者（Nameless One）之尊名立下血誓，必当恪守信义。为此，老夫特将在这座死寂古城科斯（Koth）之幽暗静室中、向老夫耳语宣述之言辞以鲜血誊录于此，世间凡胎除老夫外未尝有一人涉足此地。此等言辞乃老夫亲笔录下，待大限之期降临之际，必当在老夫尸骸之上朗声诵读，以履行老夫于公元1680年（1680, AD）神志清明且年届五十岁（50岁）之际、凭自身明悟自愿缔结之盟约。咒文始于下：

“‘于凡人生息之前，旧日支配者（Elder ones）便已巍然永存；即便如今，彼等之君主仍栖居于重重幽影之间，凡人足迹若履其中，便再无回寰返照之期。’

随着康拉德在晦涩生疏的异域语言中磕磕绊绊前行，那些字句渐渐融汇成一阵野蛮可怖的含混乱语——那语调隐隐透着腓尼基语的幽邃，却浸透着超越尘世任何已知语言记忆的古老邪秽触感。一支蜡烛晃动了一下，悄然熄灭。我正欲上前重新将其引燃，那默立一旁的东方人仅轻轻一扬手便制止了我。他的双眸如火炭般直直刺入我的眼帘，随即重新低垂，凝注回长桌上那具死寂僵直的躯体之上。

羊皮手卷上的文辞重新切回了古奥晦涩的英语。

“‘……凡涉足科斯（Koth）之漆黑堡垒、得见那遮蔽真容之幽暗君主（Darke Lord）之凡人，付出相应代价，便可获赐平生所愿之万般欲求，享之不尽的金银珍宝与玄奥博识，以及超越肉体凡胎寿数之漫长生命，乃至长达两百五十年（250年）之久。’

康拉德的嗓音再次滑入晦涩生涩的喉音乱语之中。又一支蜡烛悄然熄灭。

“‘……大限结算之期日近，九幽阴火侵噬五脏六腑以为清算征兆之际，凡胎肉身切莫心生怯退畏葸。盖因黑暗王子（Prince of Darkness）终当收回其当得之债，且绝非凡智所能瞒天过海。凡汝亲口所应允者，便须如数奉纳交付。

===Original===

*Augantha na shuba*﻿—”
At the first sound of those barbaric accents, a cold hand of terror locked about my throat. My frantic eyes shot to the candles and I was not surprised to see another flicker out. Yet there was no hint of any draft to stir the heavy black hangings. Conrad’s voice wavered; he drew his hand across his throat, gagging momentarily. The eyes of the oriental never altered.
“﻿—﻿—﻿Amonge ye sonnes of men glide strange shadows forever. Men see ye tracks of ye talones but not ye feete that make them. Over ye souls of men spread great black wingges. There is but one Black Master though men calle hym Sathanas & Beelzebub & Apolleon & Ahriman & Malik Tous﻿—”
---
Mists of horror engulfed me. I was dimly aware of Conrad’s voice droning on and on, both in English and in that other fearsome tongue whose horrific import I scarcely dared try to guess. And with stark fear clutching at my heart, I saw the candles go out, one by one. And with each flicker, as the gathering gloom darkened about us, my horror mounted. I could not speak, I could not move; my distended eyes were fixed with agonized intensity on the remaining candle. The silent oriental at the head of that ghastly table was included in my fear. He had not moved nor spoken, but under his drooping lids, his eyes burned with devilish triumph; I knew that beneath his inscrutable exterior he was gloating fiendishly﻿—but why﻿—*why?*
But I *knew* that the moment the extinguishing of the last candle plunged the room into utter darkness, some nameless, abominable thing would take place. Conrad was approaching the end. His voice rose to the climax in gathering crescendo.
