At irregular intervals I dream it, and each time, lately”﻿—he hesitated and then went on﻿—“each time lately, the thing has been getting closer﻿—closer﻿—the waving of the grass marks his progress and he nears me with each dream; and when he reaches me, then﻿—”
He stopped short, then without a word rose abruptly and entered the house. The rest of us sat silent for awhile, then followed him, for it was late.
---
How long I slept I do not know, but I woke suddenly with the impression that somewhere in the house someone had laughed, long, loud and hideously, as a maniac laughs. Starting up, wondering if I had been dreaming, I rushed from my room, just as a truly horrible shriek echoed through the house. The place was now alive with other people who had been awakened, and all of us rushed to Faming’s room, whence the sounds had seemed to come.
Faming lay dead upon the floor, where it seemed he had fallen in some terrific struggle.

===Chinese===

“整整一天，我皆在以超越肉体凡胎极限的超人潜能苦苦支撑，身后如影随形的滔天恐怖死死鞭笞着我向前。每当我拖着虚浮麻痹的双腿将身躯向前抛掷，每当我瘫倒在草丛中如濒死般贪婪抽吸空气，我都用一种令人毛骨悚然的狂热眼神死死盯着苍穹上的骄阳。当一个人为了逃命而与太阳赛跑时，太阳的飞驰是何等神速！这是一场注定输光的绝望竞逐，当我眼睁睁看着红日渐渐沉向地平线、而我原指望能在日落前赶抵的山丘依旧如在天边般遥不可及时，我心中便已知晓了一切。”

他的嗓音压得极低，我们不由自主地纷纷探身前倾凑近于他；他双手死死深抠着椅背扶手，鲜血自他的下唇缓缓淌落。

“随后太阳落山了，阴影席卷天地，我踉跄前冲，摔倒、爬起、再度摇晃前行。我笑啊，狂笑，狂笑！随后我的笑声戛然而止，因冷月破空升起，将整片茫茫草原勾勒出一副凄清冰冷的银色剪影。惨白的光芒横扫过整座大地，而明月本身却猩红如血。我猛然回首望向自己逃来的漫漫长路——而在极远……极深……极后方……”——我们所有人尽皆毛发倒竖、身体前倾得更近；他的话音宛如一道自幽冥飘来的死灵耳语——“在极深远处的草丛里……我……看……见……草……浪……在……翻……滚。荒原上全无半点晚风，但那高耸的荒草却在惨白月华下徐徐分开、左右剧烈摇曳，化作一道狭长弯曲、蜿蜒曲折的移动波浪……相隔尚在极远之处，却在分分秒秒无情逼近。”他的声音彻底消歇。

有人终于打破了死寂：“后来呢……？”

“随后我便惊醒了。至今为止，我尚从未亲眼目睹过那头肮脏巨怪的真身。但这便是那场永恒纠缠索命的梦魇，童年时我总在尖叫啼哭中醒来，成年后我总在透骨冷汗中惊醒。它以毫无规律的间隔反复发作，而每一次……最近每做一次，”他迟疑了片刻，续道，“最近每一次，那东西都逼得越来越近……越来越近……荒草翻滚的浪潮标志着它的步步逼近，每一场梦中它都离我更近一步；而当它终于追上我的那一刻，那时……”

他的话语骤然僵死在半空，旋即未发一言霍然起身，头也不回地快步走入了宅邸大门。我们余下的人在原处面面相觑、沉默了良久，随后亦纷纷起身回房，因夜色已然极深。

---

我究竟沉睡了多久，我浑然不知，但我是被一阵突如其来的诡异声响猛然惊醒的——脑海中的清晰印象告诉我，宅子里的某个角落方才有人发出了大笑，漫长、尖利而狞恶，恰如一个失心疯狂徒的临死狂笑。我翻身跃起，尚自怀疑自己是否还在梦中，就在此刻，一声当真令人毛骨悚然的凄厉绝望惨叫瞬间在整座宅邸中轰然回荡！整间大宅瞬间被从睡梦中惊醒的众人唤醒，所有人发疯般一齐冲向法明的卧室——方才的惨叫分明正是自该处传出。

法明僵死在地板上，看情形，生前分明曾在此处经历过一场惊天动地的殊死搏斗。

===Original===

There was no mark upon him, but his face was terribly distorted; as the face of a man who had been crushed by some superhuman force﻿—such as some gigantic snake.

===Chinese===

他的躯体上找不见半点利刃或钝器的创痕，然而他的整张面孔却彻底狰狞扭曲变形；宛如一个活人被某种超越尘世极限的恐怖伟力硬生生挤压碾碎时所留下的面容——恰似被某种硕大无朋的滔天巨蟒活生生绞杀一般。

## The Fearsome Touch of Death / 死亡的可怖触碰
===Original===

> As long as midnight cloaks the earth

With shadows grim and stark,

God save us from the Judas kiss

Of a dead man in the dark.

Old Adam Farrel lay dead in the house wherein he had lived alone for the last twenty years. A silent, churlish recluse, in his life he had known no friends, and only two men had watched his passing.
Dr. Stein rose and glanced out the window into the gathering dusk.
“You think you can spend the night here, then?” he asked his companion.
This man, Falred by name, assented.
“Yes, certainly. I guess it’s up to me.”
“Rather a useless and primitive custom, sitting up with the dead,” commented the doctor, preparing to depart, “but I suppose in common decency we will have to bow to precedence. Maybe I can find someone who’ll come over here and help you with your vigil.”
Falred shrugged his shoulders. “I doubt it. Farrel wasn’t liked﻿—wasn’t known by many people. I scarcely knew him myself, but I don’t mind sitting up with the corpse.”
Dr. Stein was removing his rubber gloves, and Falred watched the process with an interest that almost amounted to fascination. A slight, involuntary shudder shook him at the memory of touching these gloves﻿—slick, cold, clammy things, like the touch of death.
“You may get lonely tonight, if I don’t find anyone,” the doctor remarked as he opened the door. “Not superstitious, are you?”
Falred laughed. “Scarcely. To tell the truth, from what I hear of Farrel’s disposition, I’d rather be watching his corpse than have been his guest in life.”
The door closed and Falred took up his vigil. He seated himself in the only chair the room boasted, glanced casually at the formless, sheeted bulk on the bed opposite him, and began to read by the light of the dim lamp which stood on the rough table.
Outside the darkness gathered swiftly, and finally Falred laid down his magazine to rest his eyes. He looked again at the shape which had, in life, been the form of Adam Farrel, wondering what quirk in the human nature made the sight of a corpse not only so unpleasant, but such an object of fear to many. Unthinking ignorance, seeing in dead things a reminder of death to come, he decided lazily, and began idly contemplating as to what life had held for this grim and crabbed old man, who had neither relatives nor friends, and who had seldom left the house wherein he had died.

===Chinese===

> 只要子夜仍为大地披上重裘，
> 笼罩着凄厉而阴森的重重幽影，
> 上帝啊，求你救我们脱离那黑暗之中
> 死人所印下的犹大之吻。

老亚当·法雷尔（Adam Farrel）咽气了，遗体横陈在他独居了整整二十年的老宅之中。他生前是个沉默寡言、乖戾暴躁的孤僻隐士，平生未曾结识过半个挚友，临终之际唯有两人守候在他塌前。

斯坦医生（Dr. Stein）站起身，透过窗棂望向外头渐浓的暮色。

“那么，你确信今晚能在此守夜？”他转向身旁的同伴问道。

那人名叫法尔雷德（Falred），点头应承了下来。

“是的，自然可以。我想这事总得有人来担。”

“通宵守灵这等习俗，未免过于陈腐无用且流于原始蛮荒，”医生一边准备动身离开，一边评论道，“不过我想出于起码的人情体统，我们总得依循惯例。或许我能在外头找个愿意过来同你搭伴守夜的热心人。”

法尔雷德耸了耸肩。“我看难。法雷尔生前人缘极差——认识他的人少之又少。连我自己同他亦素昧平生，不过我倒不在乎独自陪着这具尸首坐上一夜。”

斯坦医生正在褪下手上的橡胶手套，法尔雷德凝神注视着这一动作，其专注程度近乎着迷。一想起方才触碰到那副手套时的触感，他便不由自主地微微打了个寒战——滑腻、冰冷、黏湿之物，宛如死神的冰冷触碰。

“若我没能替你寻得帮手，今夜你一人独处或许会颇感孤寂，”医生拉开房门时随口叮嘱道，“你该不会疑神疑鬼吧？”

法尔雷德笑了起来。“断不至于。实情而言，依我所听闻的法雷尔那副臭脾气，我宁可夜里守着他的尸骸，也绝不愿在他生前当他的座上宾。”

房门合拢，法尔雷德正式开始了守灵。他在屋内唯一的一把木椅上落座，漫不经心地扫了一眼正对面床榻上那具被床单覆盖的无形隆起，随后借着粗陋方桌上昏暗油灯的微光，开始翻阅手头的杂志。

门外的夜色迅速沉降压下，最终，法尔雷德合上了杂志好让双眼稍作歇息。他再度凝眸望向那具生前曾名为亚当·法雷尔的身形轮廓，暗自纳罕究竟是人类天性中的何等扭曲怪癖，竟让一具冰冷的尸骸不仅显得如此令人嫌恶，更沦为世人普遍极度恐惧的对象。不过是不假思索的蒙昧无知，在死物身上看到了自身未来必将迎来的死亡写照罢了，他懒洋洋地下了断语，随后漫不经心地推想，对于这个既无亲眷亦无至交、生前极少迈出这座咽气之宅的冷酷乖戾老翁而言，他的一生究竟曾拥有过何等意义。

===Original===

The usual tales of miser-hoarded wealth had accumulated, but Falred felt so little interest in the whole matter that it was not even necessary for him to overcome any temptation to pry about the house for possible hidden treasure.
He returned to his reading with a shrug. The task was more boresome than he had thought for. After a while he was aware that every time he looked up from his magazine and his eyes fell upon the bed with its grim occupant, he started involuntarily as if he had, for an instant, forgotten the presence of the dead man and was unpleasantly reminded of the fact. The start was slight and instinctive, but he felt almost angered at himself. He realized, for the first time, the utter and deadening silence which enwrapped the house﻿—a silence apparently shared by the night, for no sound came through the window. Adam Farrel had lived as far apart from his neighbors as possible, and there was no other house within hearing distance.
Falred shook himself as if to rid his mind of unsavory speculations, and went back to his reading. A sudden vagrant gust of wind whipped through the window, in which the light in the lamp flickered and went out suddenly. Falred, cursing softly, groped in the darkness for matches, burning his fingers on the hot lamp chimney. He struck a match, re-lighted the lamp, and glancing over at the bed, got a horrible mental jolt. Adam Farrel’s face stared blindly at him, the dead eyes wide and blank, framed in the gnarled gray features. Even as Falred instinctively shuddered, his reason explained the apparent phenomenon: the sheet that covered the corpse had been carelessly thrown across the face and the sudden puff of wind had disarranged and flung it aside.
Yet there was something grisly about the thing, something fearsomely suggestive﻿—as if, in the cloaking dark, a dead hand had flung aside the sheet, just as if the corpse were about to rise.﻿ ﻿…
Falred, an imaginative man, shrugged his shoulders at these ghastly thoughts and crossed the room to replace the sheet. The dead eyes seemed to stare at him malevolently, with an evilness that transcended the dead man’s churlishness in life. The workings of a vivid imagination, Falred knew, and he re-covered the gray face, shrinking as his hand chanced to touch the cold flesh﻿—slick and clammy, the touch of death.

===Chinese===

乡野间免不了积聚了种种关于守财奴秘藏金银财宝的风言风语，但法尔雷德对整桩事全无半点兴致，以至于他甚至根本无须去克制任何在屋里翻箱倒柜搜寻可能隐匿宝藏的贪念。

他耸了耸肩，重新翻开杂志。然而这份消遣远比他预想的更加乏味枯燥。过了一阵，他猛然警觉，每当自己从杂志中抬起头、目光落在那张躺着阴森死者的床榻上时，他都会不由自主地悚然一惊，仿佛在方才的刹那间他已然遗忘了死人的存在，随后又极不情愿地被现实残酷提醒。那惊颤微弱而纯属本能，但他却不由得对自己暗生恼怒。他生平第一次真切意识到，整座大宅正被一股何等绝对而令人窒息的死寂死死包裹——这股死寂显然与夜色融为一体，因窗外未曾透进半点风吹草动。亚当·法雷尔生前尽可能避开所有邻里孤居一隅，方圆听力所及之内根本找不见第二间人烟。

法尔雷德使劲甩了甩头，仿佛极力想将脑海中那些不祥的臆想彻底驱散，随后重新埋头阅读。突然间，一股穿堂怪风呼啸掠过窗棂，油灯中的火苗猛烈摇曳了一下，骤然熄灭。法尔雷德低声咒骂了一句，在漆黑中摸索着寻找火柴，指尖狠狠烫在了滚烫的玻璃灯罩上。他划亮了一根火柴，重新引燃油灯，然而当他转头扫向床榻的刹那，整个人在精神上遭受了一记令人毛骨悚然的重击！亚当·法雷尔的面孔正空洞死寂地直勾勾盯着他，那双暴突僵死的死鱼眼睁得滚圆空洞，镶嵌在沟壑纵横的铁青五官之中。尽管法尔雷德下意识打了个寒战，他的理性在瞬息间便对眼前的异象给出了解释：先前覆盖在尸身上的床单不过是草草搭在脸上，方才那阵穿堂怪风将其吹乱掀翻在了一旁。

然而这桩变故中却透着一股说不出的阴森可怖，透着某种令人心惊肉跳的诡异暗示——仿佛在方才遮蔽视线的黑暗深处，一只僵死冰冷的手掌曾悄然将床单一把扯开，恰似那具死尸正欲翻身而起一般……

法尔雷德是个想象力极丰沛之人，他耸肩挥散了这些可怖的妄念，起身穿过房间去重新抚平床单。那双死人的眼睛仿佛正带着滔天的恶毒死死盯着他，其邪念远甚于死者生前那点微不足道的乖戾脾性。纯粹是过剩想象力的作祟，法尔雷德心知肚明；他重新遮盖住了那张死灰色的面庞，而当手掌不经意间触碰到冰冷皮肉的刹那，他下意识剧烈缩手——滑腻、黏湿，这正是死神的冰冷触碰。

===Original===

He shuddered with the natural revulsion of the living for the dead, and went back to his chair and magazine.
At last, growing sleepy, he lay down upon a couch which, by some strange whim of the original owner, formed part of the room’s scant furnishings, and composed himself for slumber. He decided to leave the light burning, telling himself that it was in accordance with the usual custom of leaving lights burning for the dead; for he was not willing to admit to himself that already he was conscious of a dislike for lying in the darkness with the corpse. He dozed, awoke with a start and looked at the sheeted form on the bed. Silence reigned over the house, and outside it was very dark.
The hour was approaching midnight, with its accompanying eerie domination over the human mind. Falred glanced again at the bed where the body lay and found the sight of the sheeted object most repellent. A fantastic idea had birth in his mind and grew, that beneath the sheet, the mere lifeless body had become a strange, monstrous thing, a hideous, conscious being, that watched him with eyes which burned through the fabric of the cloth. This thought﻿—a mere fantasy, of course﻿—he explained to himself by the legends of vampires, undead, ghosts and suchlike﻿—the fearsome attributes with which the living have cloaked the dead for countless ages, since primitive man first recognized in death something horrid and apart from life. Man feared death, thought Falred, and some of his fear of death took hold on the dead so that they, too, were feared. And the sight of the dead engendered grisly thoughts, gave rise to dim fears of hereditary memory, lurking back in the dark corners of the brain.
At any rate, that silent, hidden thing was getting on his nerves. He thought of uncovering the face, on the principle that familiarity breeds contempt. The sight of the features, calm and still in death, would banish, he thought, all such wild conjectures as were haunting him in spite of himself. But the thought of those dead eyes staring in the lamplight was intolerable; so at last he blew out the light and lay down. This fear had been stealing upon him so insidiously and gradually that he had not been aware of its growth.

===Chinese===

他因生灵对死物那股与生俱来的本能生理厌恶而剧烈战栗，随后快步退回木椅与杂志之旁。

及至最终，睡意渐浓，他在一张长榻上仰卧躺下——出于前任房主的某种怪癖，这张长榻成了屋内稀疏陈设的一部分，他阖眼准备安然入睡。他决定任由油灯彻夜长明，他在心底宽慰自己，这完全符合为亡人留一盏长明灯的民间老规矩；因他骨子里绝不愿承认，自己心底其实已然极度排斥同这具死尸一同躺在伸手不见五指的黑暗之中。他迷迷糊糊打着盹，猛地一惊醒转，凝眸望向床上那具被床单严密覆盖的身影。死一般的沉寂笼罩着大宅，窗外漆黑如墨。

时辰已然逼近子夜，连带着将它那股纠祟人心的诡异魔力沉沉压在凡俗心神之上。法尔雷德再度侧目望向横陈尸骸的床榻，惊觉眼前那具蒙着白单的异物显得前所未有地令人作呕。一个荒诞不经的念头在他脑海中悄然萌生并疯狂疯长：在那薄薄的床单之下，那具本该毫无生机的肉体死胎已然化作了一具诡谲畸形的魔物，一个生着独立意识的可憎活物，正隔着粗糙的布料用一双喷薄着凶焰的眼眸死死窥视着他。这一念头——纯粹是妄念，毋庸置疑——他用吸血鬼、亡灵、孤魂野鬼等古老传说向自己解释着——自史前原始人类初次在死亡中洞悉某种与生命彻底割裂的可憎异质以来，千百年来生灵便是用这等可怖的属性将死者层层包裹。世人畏惧死亡，法尔雷德思忖，而这份对死亡的无边恐惧又移情投射在死尸身上，以至于连死人本身亦成了畏惧的渊薮。而凝视死尸，往往会在脑海最幽暗的角落里勾起血腥阴森的恐怖臆想，唤醒潜伏在血脉记忆深处的朦胧远古极恐。

无论如何，那个死寂隐匿的物体已然彻底击溃了他的神经。他甚至动念想去掀开死者的面布，秉持着久视则不怪的心理信条。他心想，亲眼目睹那在死亡中沉寂安详的五官轮廓，定能将这些不由自主缠绕自己的狂乱臆测驱散殆尽。然而一想到那双死人在油灯下空洞圆睁的目光，他便感到一股无法忍受的窒息；于是最终，他一口气吹熄了油灯，径直躺下。这份恐惧是以如此潜移默化、步步为营的阴险姿态悄然将他吞噬，以至于他甚至未曾察觉其疯狂滋长的心路历程。

===Original===

With the extinguishing of the light, however, and the blotting out of the sight of the corpse, things assumed their true character and proportions, and Falred fell asleep almost instantly, on his lips a faint smile for his previous folly.
---
He awakened suddenly. How long he had been asleep he did not know. He sat up, his pulse pounding frantically, the cold sweat beading his forehead. He knew instantly where he was, remembered the other occupant of the room. But what had awakened him? A dream﻿—yes, now he remembered﻿—a hideous dream in which the dead man had risen from the bed and stalked stiffly across the room with eyes of fire and a horrid leer frozen on his gray lips. Falred had seemed to lie motionless, helpless; then as the corpse reached a gnarled and horrible hand, he had awakened.
He strove to pierce the gloom, but the room was all blackness and all without was so dark that no gleam of light came through the window. He reached a shaking hand toward the lamp, then recoiled as if from a hidden serpent. Sitting here in the dark with a fiendish corpse was bad enough, but he dared not light the lamp, for fear that his reason would be snuffed out like a candle at what he might see. Horror, stark and unreasoning, had full possession of his soul; he no longer questioned the instinctive fears that rose in him. All those legends he had heard came back to him and brought a belief in them. Death was a hideous thing, a brain-shattering horror, imbuing lifeless men with a horrid malevolence. Adam Farrel in his life had been simply a churlish but harmless man; now he was a terror, a monster, a fiend lurking in the shadows of fear, ready to leap on mankind with talons dipped deep in death and insanity.
Falred sat there, his blood freezing, and fought out his silent battle. Faint glimmerings of reason had begun to touch his fright when a soft, stealthy sound again froze him. He did not recognize it as the whisper of the night wind across the windowsill. His frenzied fancy knew it only as the tread of death and horror. He sprang from the couch, then stood undecided. Escape was in his mind but he was too dazed to even try to formulate a plan of escape. Even his sense of direction was gone. Fear had so stultified his mind that he was not able to think consciously. The blackness spread in long waves about him and its darkness and void entered into his brain.

===Chinese===

然而，随着灯火的熄灭以及死尸轮廓彻底没入黑暗，周遭事物反倒重新显露出了它们本来的面目与尺度，法尔雷德几乎在顷刻间便沉沉睡去，唇边甚至掠过一抹因方才愚蠢举止而泛起的自嘲浅笑。

---

他是被猛然惊醒的。他究竟沉睡了多久，他浑然不知。他霍然坐起身，周身脉搏狂乱暴跳，额角沁出粒粒冰冷透骨的汗珠。他几乎在瞬息间便洞悉了自己身在何方，记起了屋内同处一室的另一个存在。但究竟是何物将他惊醒？一场噩梦——是的，此刻他记起来了——一场令人魂飞魄散的惊悚噩梦：在那梦境中，死人自床榻上僵直爬起，双眸喷吐着熊熊烈焰，灰败的双唇上凝固着一抹狰狞可怖的冷酷狞笑，迈着僵硬的关节跨步穿过房间逼近而来。梦中的法尔雷德仿佛瘫软在床动弹不得、求生无门；随后，就在那具死尸伸出一只枯槁畸形的鬼手抓来的刹那，他猛然惊醒了过来。

他极力试图穿透幽暗看清四周，但屋内一片漆黑如墨，窗外亦伸手不见五指，没有半点微光能穿透窗棂。他伸出一只颤抖的手掌摸向油灯，旋即如遭遇潜伏的毒蛇般触电般缩了回来。在这浓稠的黑暗中同这具恶灵般的死尸共处一室已然足够糟糕，但他绝无胆量重新点燃油灯——因他深恐自己在目睹眼前真相的刹那，自己的理智会如蜡烛般被瞬间活活吹灭。纯粹、毫无理性可言的赤裸极恐，彻底全盘占据了他的灵魂；他再也无法质疑心头翻涌而起的本能惊惧。他平生听闻过的一切阴森传说在此刻尽皆死灰复燃，令他深信不疑。死亡乃是一桩可憎至极的浩劫，一场足以碾碎脑髓的灭顶极恐，能将毫无生机的死尸淬炼出滔天的恶毒杀机。亚当·法雷尔生前不过是个性格乖戾却人畜无害的老翁；而如今他已化作了一尊凶煞、一头魔物、一尊潜伏在恐惧阴影深处的恶鬼，随时准备挥舞着浸透死亡与癫狂的利爪向生灵发起绝命扑杀。

法尔雷德僵坐在黑暗中，周身血液彻底冻结成冰，在心底苦苦进行着无声的殊死挣扎。当理智的微弱火花刚刚开始尝试抚慰他崩溃的心神之际，一声轻柔、鬼祟的异响再度将他彻底冻结。他根本无法辨识出那不过是晚风拂过窗台的低语微鸣。在他狂乱癫狂的臆想之中，那分明正是死神与大恐怖逼近的冰冷足音。他自长榻上猛然弹跳而起，随即进退维谷地僵立当场。逃出生天的念头在脑海中疯狂尖叫，但他整个人却眩晕混乱得甚至无法构建出哪怕一条逃跑的路线。甚至连基本的前后左右方向感亦荡然无存。恐惧彻底摧残麻痹了他的心智，令他根本无法进行哪怕最起码的清醒思考。黑浪如惊涛骇浪般在周遭翻卷涌动，其浓重如铁的虚无与漆黑彻底钻入了他的脑髓之中。

===Original===

His motions, such as they were, were instinctive. He seemed shackled with mighty chains and his limbs responded sluggishly, like an imbecile’s.
A terrible horror grew up in him and reared its grisly shape, that the dead man was behind him, was stealing upon him from the rear. He no longer thought of lighting the lamp; he no longer thought of anything. Fear filled his whole being; there was room for nothing else.
He backed slowly away in the darkness, hands behind him, instinctively feeling the way. With a terrific effort he partly shook the clinging mists of horror from him, and, the cold sweat clammy upon his body, strove to orient himself. He could see nothing, but the bed was across the room, in front of him. He was backing away from it. There was where the dead man was lying, according to all rules of nature; if the thing were, as he felt, behind him, then the old tales were true: death did implant in lifeless bodies an unearthly animation, and dead men did roam the shadows to work their ghastly and evil will upon the sons of men. Then﻿—great God!﻿—what was man but a wailing infant, lost in the night and beset by frightful things from the black abysses and the terrible unknown voids of space and time? These conclusions he did not reach by any reasoning process; they leaped full-grown into his terror-dazed brain. He worked his way slowly backward, groping, clinging to the thought that the dead man *must* be in front of him.
Then his back-flung hands encountered something﻿—something slick, cold and clammy﻿—like the touch of death. A scream shook the echoes, followed by the crash of a falling body.
---
The next morning they who came to the house of death found two corpses in the room. Adam Farrel’s sheeted body lay motionless upon the bed, and across the room lay the body of Falred, beneath the shelf where Dr. Stein had absentmindedly left his gloves﻿—rubber gloves, slick and clammy to the touch of a hand groping in the dark﻿—a hand of one fleeing his own fear﻿—rubber gloves, slick and clammy and cold, like the touch of death.

===Chinese===

他的动作，若尚能称之为动作，纯属盲目的动物本能。他仿佛被无形的万钧铁链死死锁缚，四肢宛如白痴般迟钝僵硬、反应艰难。

一股令人窒息的滔天恐怖在他胸中疯狂暴涨，化作一具狰狞的魔影：死人就在他的身后，正自他背脊正后方无声无息地潜行逼近。他再也顾不上引燃油灯；他再也顾不上思考尘世间的任何事物。恐惧塞满了他的全部灵魂；再无半点缝隙容纳其余心念。

他在绝对的黑暗中步步向后倒退，双手背在身后，下意识地摸索着退路。凭借着超人般的极力挣扎，他勉强挥散了少许缠绕心头的粘稠恐怖迷雾，周身覆满冰冷滑腻的冷汗，竭力辨识着方位。视线中空无一物，但依照常理，床榻应当在房间的另一端，就在他的正前方。他此刻正向着远离床榻的方向步步倒退。依照自然界的一切造化常理，死人此刻理应平躺在那张床上；倘若那魔物真如他所感知的这般潜伏在他身后，那么古老的传说便是千真万确的真理：死亡确实能将超自然的邪秽生机注入冰冷的死尸之中，死人确实能在重重阴影中游荡徘徊，将他们可怖阴毒的凶念施加在尘世人子的头颅之上。如此说来——至尊的上帝啊！——凡人算个什么东西，不过是在夜色中啼哭求告的婴孩，在黑暗深渊与时空不可知虚空的重重可怖凶煞围困下任人宰割！这番推论绝非出自任何理性的逻辑推理；而是宛如完全成形的梦魇狂潮般瞬间轰入了他被恐惧震慑得千疮百孔的大脑。他在黑暗中艰难地向后倒退摸索，死死抓牢心头仅存的最后一根稻草——死人“必然”就在他的正前方。

就在此刻，他背在身后摸索探寻的双手触碰到了某种东西——某种滑腻、冰冷、黏湿之物——宛如死神的冰冷触碰。

一声凄厉绝望的惨叫撕裂了漫漫长夜，紧接着是一声身躯轰然倒地的沉闷巨响。

---

翌日清晨，赶赴这座凶宅之人推开房门，在屋内赫然发现了两具冰冷的尸骸。亚当·法雷尔覆着床单的尸身依然纹丝不动地平陈在床榻之上，而在房间正对面、在斯坦医生昨夜心不在焉遗落手套的木架下方，瘫倒着法尔雷德僵硬的躯体——正是那副橡胶手套，在黑暗中摸索奔逃自身心魔的颤抖双手触碰之下，显得那般滑腻、黏湿而冰冷，恰如死神的冰冷触碰。

## The Fire of Ashurbanipal / 亚述巴尼拔之火
===Original===

Yar Ali squinted carefully down the blue barrel of his Lee-Enfield, called devoutly on Allah and sent a bullet through the brain of a flying rider.
“*Allahu akbar!*”
The big Afghan shouted in glee, waving his weapon above his head, “God is great! By Allah, sahib, I have sent another one of the dogs to Hell!”
His companion peered cautiously over the rim of the sandpit they had scooped with their hands. He was a lean and wiry American, Steve Clarney by name.
“Good work, old horse,” said this person. “Four left. Look﻿—they’re drawing off.”
The white-robed horsemen were indeed reining away, clustering together just out of accurate rifle-range, as if in council. There had been seven when they had first swooped down on the comrades, but the fire from the two rifles in the sandpit had been deadly.
“Look, sahib﻿—they abandon the fray!”
Yar Ali stood up boldly and shouted taunts at the departing riders, one of whom whirled and sent a bullet that kicked up sand thirty feet in front of the pit.
“They shoot like the sons of dogs,” said Yar Ali in complacent self-esteem. “By Allah, did you see that rogue plunge from his saddle as my lead went home? Up, sahib; let us run after them and cut them down!”
Paying no attention to this outrageous proposal﻿—for he knew it was but one of the gestures Afghan nature continually demands﻿—Steve rose, dusted off his breeches and gazing after the riders, now white specks far out on the desert, said musingly: “Those fellows ride as if they had some set purpose in mind﻿—not a bit like men running from a licking.”
“Aye,” agreed Yar Ali promptly and seeing nothing inconsistent with his present attitude and recent bloodthirsty suggestion, “they ride after more of their kind﻿—they are hawks who give up their prey not quickly. We had best move our position quickly, Steve sahib. They will come back﻿—maybe in a few hours, maybe in a few days﻿—it all depends on how far away lies the oasis of their tribe. But they will be back. We have guns and lives﻿—they want both. And behold.”
The Afghan levered out the empty shell and slipped a single cartridge into the breech of his rifle.
“My last bullet, sahib.”
Steve nodded. “I’ve got three left.”
The raiders whom their bullets had knocked from the saddle had been looted by their own comrades. No use searching the bodies which lay in the sand for ammunition. Steve lifted his canteen and shook it.

===Chinese===

亚尔·阿里顺着李-恩菲尔德步枪泛着蓝光的枪管仔细瞄准，虔诚地呼告真主之名，将一颗子弹送入了一名狂奔骑手的头颅。
“*真主至大（Allahu akbar）！*”
身材魁梧的阿富汗人兴高采烈地大喊起来，在头顶挥舞着武器：“真主至大！凭真主起誓，老爷（sahib），我又把一条狗送进了地狱！”
他的同伴小心翼翼地从他们徒手刨出的沙坑边缘探出头来。那是个精瘦结实的美国人，名叫史蒂夫·克拉尼。
“干得好，老兄，”此人说道，“还剩4个。看——他们退开了。”
那些白袍骑手果然勒转马头，聚拢在刚刚脱离精准步枪射程之外的地方，似乎在商议对策。他们最初突袭这两个伙伴时共有7人，但沙坑中两支步枪射出的火力极其致命。
“看，老爷——他们放弃战斗了！”
亚尔·阿里大胆地站起身，冲着远去的骑手大声嘲弄，其中一人猛然回身射出一枪，掀起的沙尘落在沙坑前方30英尺（约9米）处。
“他们射击就像狗崽子一样，”亚尔·阿里得意洋洋自鸣得意地说道，“凭真主起誓，我的铅弹打中时，你看到那个恶棍从马鞍上栽下去了吗？起来，老爷，让我们追上去把他们砍翻！”
史蒂夫对这个荒唐的提议不予理会——因为他知道这不过是阿富汗人的天性所不断驱使的姿态——他站起身，拍打掉马裤上的灰尘，凝视着那些远在荒漠上已化作白色斑点的骑手，若有所思地说道：“那些家伙策马的架势仿佛心怀某种明确企图——一点也不像吃了败仗逃跑的人。”
“对，”亚尔·阿里立刻表示赞同，丝毫没觉得自己当下的态度与刚才嗜血的提议有何矛盾，“他们是去搬救兵了——他们是绝不轻易放弃猎物的鹰隼。我们最好尽快转移阵地，史蒂夫老爷。他们会回来的——也许几个小时后，也许几天后——全看他们部族的绿洲隔得有多远。但他们一定会回来。我们有枪也有命——他们两样都想要。而且你看。”
阿富汗人退掉空弹壳，将最后一发子弹推进步枪枪膛。
“我最后一颗子弹了，老爷。”
史蒂夫点了点头：“我还剩3颗。”
那些被他们子弹击落马鞍的突袭者已被自己的同伙搜刮干净。去沙地里的尸体上搜寻弹药毫无意义。史蒂夫拿起水壶晃了晃。

===Original===

Not much water remained. He knew that Yar Ali had only a little more than he, though the big Afridi, bred in a barren land, had used and needed less water than did the American; although the latter, judged from a white man’s standards, was hard and tough as a wolf. As Steve unscrewed the canteen cap and drank very sparingly, he mentally reviewed the chain of events that had led them to their present position.
Wanderers, soldiers of fortune, thrown together by chance and attracted to each other by mutual admiration, he and Yar Ali had wandered from India up through Turkistan and down through Persia, an oddly assorted but highly capable pair. Driven by the restless urge of inherent wanderlust, their avowed purpose﻿—which they swore to and sometimes believed themselves﻿—was the accumulation of some vague and undiscovered treasure, some pot of gold at the foot of some yet unborn rainbow.
Then in ancient Shiraz they had heard of the Fire of Ashurbanipal. From the lips of an ancient Persian trader, who only half believed what he repeated to them, they heard the tale that he in turn had heard from the babbling lips of delirium, in his distant youth. He had been a member of a caravan, fifty years before, which, wandering far on the southern shore of the Persian Gulf trading for pearls, had followed the tale of a rare pearl far into the desert.
The pearl, rumored found by a diver and stolen by a sheikh of the interior, they did not find, but they did pick up a Turk who was dying of starvation, thirst and a bullet wound in the thigh. As he died in delirium, he babbled a wild tale of a silent dead city of black stone set in the drifting sands of the desert far to the westward, and of a flaming gem clutched in the bony fingers of a skeleton on an ancient throne.
He had not dared bring it away with him, because of an overpowering brooding horror that haunted the place, and thirst had driven him into the desert again, where Bedouins had pursued and wounded him. Yet he had escaped, riding hard until his horse fell under him. He died without telling how he had reached the mythical city in the first place, but the old trader thought he must have come from the northwest﻿—a deserter from the Turkish army, making a desperate attempt to reach the Gulf.

===Chinese===

水剩得不多了。他知道亚尔·阿里水壶里的水只比他略多一点，不过这位在不毛之地长大的健硕阿夫里迪人，平日所耗所需的水量本就比美国人少；尽管以后者的白人标准来衡量，史蒂夫已如荒原狼般坚韧强悍。史蒂夫拧开水壶盖，极其克制地抿了一小口，脑海中默默回溯着将他们引向如今境地的这一连串变故。
他们是漂泊者、亡命佣兵，因偶然结识，又因彼此钦佩而走到一起；他和亚尔·阿里从印度一路漫游，北上穿过突厥斯坦，再南下穿越波斯，是一对看似奇特却手段极硬的搭档。在骨子里那股按捺不住的漂泊冲动驱使下，他们自称的目标——对此他们发誓笃信，有时连自己也深信不疑——就是寻觅某种虚无缥缈且未被发现的宝藏，追寻某道尚未诞生的彩虹尽头埋藏的一罐黄金。
随后，在古老的设拉子，他们听闻了亚述巴尼拔之火的传说。从一位年迈波斯商人的口中——那位老人对自己转述的传说也只信了一半——他们听到了那段在老人遥远的青年时代、从神志不清的胡话中听来的往事。50年前，老人曾是一支商队的成员，他们在波斯湾南岸漫游做珍珠贸易时，顺着一颗罕见珍珠的传闻深入沙漠腹地。
那颗据传由采珠人发现并被内地酋长窃走的珍珠，他们未能寻获，但他们捡到了一个濒死的土耳其人，此人因饥饿、干渴以及大腿上的枪伤已奄奄一息。在他临死前的谵妄中，他语无伦次地吐露了一桩离奇的传说：在遥远西方风沙漫卷的荒漠深处，耸立着一座死寂的黑石死城，而在一张远古王座的骸骨指骨间，紧握着一颗炽烈燃烧的宝石。
他不敢将宝石带走，因为盘踞在那片废墟上的恐怖阴影令人窒息；极度干渴将他再次逼入大漠，途中又遭贝都因人追击打伤。然而他拼死逃了出来，拼命疾驰直至坐骑倒毙。他至死也没说清自己最初究竟是如何抵达那座神话般的城市的，但老商人推测他必定是从西北方来的——一个土耳其军队的逃兵，孤注一掷试图逃往海湾。

===Original===

The men of the caravan had made no attempt to plunge still further into the desert in search of the city; for, said the old trader, they believed it to be the ancient, ancient City of Evil spoken of in the *Necronomicon* of the mad Arab Al-hazred﻿—the city of the dead on which an ancient curse rested. Legends named it vaguely: the Arabs called it *Beled-el-Djinn*, the City of Devils, and the Turks, *Kara-Shehr*, the Black City. And the gem was that ancient and accursed jewel belonging to a king of long ago, whom the Grecians called Sardanapalus and the Semitic peoples Ashurbanipal.
---
Steve had been fascinated by the tale. Admitting to himself that it was doubtless one of the ten thousand cock-and-bull myths mooted about the East, still there was a possibility that he and Yar Ali had stumbled onto a trace of that pot of rainbow gold for which they searched. And Yar Ali had heard hints before of a silent city of the sands; tales had followed the eastbound caravans over the high Persian uplands and across the sands of Turkistan, into the mountain country and beyond﻿—vague tales, whispers of a black city of the djinn, deep in the hazes of a haunted desert.
So, following the trail of the legend, the companions had come from Shiraz to a village on the Arabian shore of the Persian Gulf, and there had heard more from an old man who had been a pearl-diver in his youth. The loquacity of age was on him and he told tales repeated to him by wandering tribesmen who had them in turn from the wild nomads of the deep interior; and again Steve and Yar Ali heard of the still black city with giant beasts carved of stone, and the skeleton sultan who held the blazing gem.
And so, mentally swearing at himself for a fool, Steve had made the plunge, and Yar Ali, secure in the knowledge that all things lay on the lap of Allah, had come with him. Their scanty supply of money had been just sufficient to provide riding-camels and provisions for a bold flying invasion of the unknown. Their only chart had been the vague rumors that placed the supposed location of Kara-Shehr.
There had been days of hard travel, pushing the beasts and conserving water and food. Then, deep in the desert they invaded, they had encountered a blinding sand-wind in which they had lost the camels.

===Chinese===

商队的人并未试图进一步深入荒漠寻觅那座城池；因为老商人说，他们认定那就是疯癫阿拉伯人阿尔-哈兹莱德在《死灵之书》（*Necronomicon*）中所记载的那座古老至极的邪恶之城——那座笼罩在远古诅咒之下的死者之城。传说中对它的称呼含混模糊：阿拉伯人称其为“贝莱德-埃尔-吉恩（Beled-el-Djinn）”，即魔鬼之城；土耳其人则称其为“卡拉-谢赫尔（Kara-Shehr）”，即黑城。而那颗宝石，正属于一位久远过去的古王，希腊人称其为萨尔达纳帕卢斯，闪米特各族则称其为亚述巴尼拔。
史蒂夫·克拉尼和亚尔·阿里曾见过荒野中那些风蚀残损的废墟，听过无数类似的狂乱传说；换作其他任何一则故事，他们或许都会一笑置之，然后将其抛诸脑后。但不知为何，这则关于那座被遗忘城池与燃烧巨钻的怪诞故事，却牢牢攫住了他们的想象力。也许是老商人叙述时那种阴郁怪诞的语气，也许是那土耳其逃兵垂死时狂乱言语所带有的诡异真实感，无论如何，他们决心一试。
两人穿越波斯湾来到阿拉伯半岛海岸，用他们微薄的金币尽可能多地购买了武器和弹药。至于向导，则一个也雇不到。那些狂暴傲慢的贝都因人对法兰克人（西方人）怒目而视，即便他们本族的某些部族，涉足内陆荒漠也难逃厄运。而当克拉尼提及贝莱德-埃尔-吉恩时，那些长须酋长便吐唾沫、扯胡须，在胸前比划手势以辟邪魅，并且严词拒绝伴随他们前往。
于是，这两个伙伴买了两峰强健的骆驼，载足水囊和给养，在无人伴随的情况下策马向西，一头扎进了广袤无垠的红色沙漠。
那是一场艰苦卓绝的跋涉，他们日夜兼程驱赶牲口，极力节约水粮。然而，在深入沙漠腹地之后，他们遭遇了一场遮天蔽日的狂暴沙暴，在这场沙暴中他们失去了骆驼。

===Original===

After that came long miles of staggering through the sands, battered by a flaming sun, subsisting on rapidly dwindling water from their canteens, and food Yar Ali had in a pouch. No thought of finding the mythical city now. They pushed on blindly, in hope of stumbling upon a spring; they knew that behind them no oases lay within a distance they could hope to cover on foot. It was a desperate chance, but their only one.
Then white-clad hawks had swooped down on them, out of the haze of the skyline, and from a shallow and hastily scooped trench the adventurers had exchanged shots with the wild riders who circled them at top speed. The bullets of the Bedouins had skipped through their makeshift fortifications, knocking dust into their eyes and flicking bits of cloth from their garments, but by good chance neither had been hit.
Their one bit of luck, reflected Clarney, as he cursed himself for a fool. What a mad venture it had been, anyway! To think that two men could so dare the desert and live, much less wrest from its abysmal bosom the secrets of the ages! And that crazy tale of a skeleton hand gripping a flaming jewel in a dead city﻿—bosh! What utter rot! He must have been crazy himself to credit it, the American decided with the clarity of view that suffering and danger bring.
“Well, old horse,” said Steve, lifting his rifle, “let’s get going. It’s a toss-up if we die of thirst or get sniped off by the desert-brothers. Anyway, we’re doin’ no good here.”
“God gives,” agreed Yar Ali cheerfully. “The sun sinks westward. Soon the coolness of night will be upon us. Perhaps we shall find water yet, sahib. Look, the terrain changes to the south.”
Clarney shaded his eyes against the dying sun. Beyond a level, barren expanse of several miles width, the land did indeed become more broken; aborted hills were in evidence. The American slung his rifle over his arm and sighed.
“Heave ahead; we’re food for the buzzards anyhow.”
---
The sun sank and the moon rose, flooding the desert with weird silver light. Drifted sand glimmered in long ripples, as if a sea had suddenly been frozen into immobility. Steve, parched fiercely by a thirst he dared not fully quench, cursed beneath his breath. The desert was beautiful beneath the moon, with the beauty of a cold marble lorelei to lure men to destruction. What a mad quest!

===Chinese===

此后便是在沙海中步履蹒跚的漫长跋涉，烈日如火般炙烤着他们，只能靠水壶里急剧减少的水和亚尔·阿里皮囊里的干粮勉强维生。此时已再无寻找那座神话城市的念头。他们茫然前行，只希望能侥幸撞见一眼清泉；他们深知在身后徒步所及的距离内绝无半座绿洲。这是一场孤注一掷的赌博，却是他们唯一的生路。
随后，身披白袍的鹰隼自天际的昏黄烟霾中扑杀而来，在一道仓促刨出的浅沟里，冒险者们与以极快速度策马盘旋的狂野骑手展开了对射。贝都因人的子弹呼啸着掠过他们简陋的工事，掀起的尘土迷了双眼，擦破了他们的衣衫，但万幸的是两人皆未中弹。
克拉尼暗自咒骂自己是个蠢货，自嘲这真是唯一的侥幸。无论如何，这趟冒险简直荒唐透顶！居然以为凭两个人就能挑衅大漠并活下来，更不用说从大漠深渊般的怀抱中夺取岁月尘封的秘密了！还有那个在死城里骸骨之手紧攥燃烧宝石的荒谬故事——一派胡言！纯属无稽之谈！在苦难与险境带来的清醒反思中，美国人断定自己当初一定是疯了才会相信它。
“好吧，老兄，”史蒂夫举起步枪说，“我们该动身了。是渴死还是被沙漠兄弟狙杀，半斤八两。反正待在这儿没有任何好处。”
“真主赐福，”亚尔·阿里爽朗地附和道，“太阳正向西沉。很快夜间的凉爽就会降临。或许我们还能找到水，老爷。看，南边的地形起变化了。”
克拉尼手搭凉棚遮挡残阳。越过几英里宽平坦荒凉的旷野，地势确实变得更加崎岖破碎；突兀残缺的丘陵隐约显现。美国人将步枪挂在臂弯上，叹了口气。
“走吧，反正我们迟早是秃鹰的盘中餐。”
---
红日西坠，明月升起，给大漠披上一层诡异的银辉。沙丘在如水月色下泛起层层涟漪，仿佛一片汪洋骤然冻结成冰雕。史蒂夫忍受着不敢彻底排解的焦渴，低声咒骂着。月光下的荒漠美丽绝伦，犹如冷酷大理石雕凿的罗蕾莱女妖，诱使凡人走向毁灭。何等疯狂的探寻！

===Original===

his weary brain reiterated; the Fire of Ashurbanipal retreated into the mazes of unreality with each dragging step. The desert became not merely a material wasteland, but the gray mists of the lost eons, in whose depths dreamed sunken things.
Clarney stumbled and swore; was he failing already? Yar Ali swung along with the easy, tireless stride of the mountain man, and Steve set his teeth, nerving himself to greater effort. They were entering the broken country at last, and the going became harder. Shallow gullies and narrow ravines knifed the earth with wavering patterns. Most of them were nearly filled with sand, and there was no trace of water.
“This country was once oasis country,” commented Yar Ali. “Allah knows how many centuries ago the sand took it, as the sand has taken so many cities in Turkistan.”
They swung on like dead men in a gray land of death. The moon grew red and sinister as she sank, and shadowy darkness settled over the desert before they had reached a point where they could see what lay beyond the broken belt. Even the big Afghan’s feet began to drag, and Steve kept himself erect only by a savage effort of will. At last they toiled up a sort of ridge, on the southern side of which the land sloped downward.
“We rest,” declared Steve. “There’s no water in this hellish country. No use in goin’ on forever. My legs are stiff as gun-barrels. I couldn’t take another step to save my neck. Here’s a kind of stunted cliff, about as high as a man’s shoulder, facing south. We’ll sleep in the lee of it.”
“And shall we not keep watch, Steve sahib?”
“We don’t,” answered Steve. “If the Arabs cut our throats while we’re asleep, so much the better. We’re goners anyhow.”
With which optimistic observation Clarney lay down stiffly in the deep sand. But Yar Ali stood, leaning forward, straining his eyes into the elusive darkness that turned the star-flecked horizons to murky wells of shadow.
“Something lies on the skyline to the south,” he muttered uneasily. “A hill? I cannot tell, or even be sure that I see anything at all.”
“You’re seeing mirages already,” said Steve irritably. “Lie down and sleep.”
And so saying Steve slumbered.
The sun in his eyes awoke him. He sat up, yawning, and his first sensation was that of thirst. He lifted his canteen and wet his lips. One drink left. Yar Ali still slept.

===Chinese===

他疲惫的大脑反复回响着这句话；随着每一次沉重的脚步，亚述巴尼拔之火都愈发退入虚无缥缈的幻境之中。荒漠不再仅仅是一片物质上的荒原，更像是湮没岁月的灰色迷雾，在深渊之中沉睡着深潜之物。
克拉尼踉跄了一下，咒骂出声；难道自己这就撑不住了？亚尔·阿里迈着山地人那种从容不倦的步伐大步向前，史蒂夫咬紧牙关，鼓起更大的毅力支撑下去。他们终于踏入了破碎起伏的荒丘地带，道路变得愈发艰难。浅沟与狭窄的冲沟在大地上划出交错蜿蜒的纹路。其中大多数几乎被流沙填满，不见半点水迹。
“这片地方曾经是片绿洲，”亚尔·阿里评述道，“只有真主知晓多少个世纪前黄沙吞噬了它，就像黄沙吞噬了突厥斯坦的诸多古城一样。”
他们如同灰色死亡国度里的亡魂般麻木前行。残月在西沉时变得猩红而险恶，在他们抵达能看清破碎带前方景象的高处之前，阴郁的黑暗已彻底笼罩了大漠。就连健硕的阿富汗人双脚也开始变得沉重，而史蒂夫全凭一股顽强的意志死撑着不肯倒下。终于，他们艰难地攀上了一道隆起的山脊，南面的地势向下倾斜。
“休息一下，”史蒂夫宣布，“这见鬼的地方没有水。盲目走下去毫无意义。我的双腿硬得像枪管。为了保命我都迈不开另一道步子了。这儿有一道低矮的石崖，大概有人肩膀那么高，背朝南。我们就在崖壁背风处睡吧。”
“难道我们不守夜吗，史蒂夫老爷？”
“不守了，”史蒂夫回答，“如果阿拉伯人趁我们睡觉时抹了我们的脖子，反倒省事。反正我们横竖都是死。”
克拉尼带着这番乐观的评语，僵硬地仰躺在厚厚的细沙中。但亚尔·阿里依然站着，身子前倾，竭力将目光穿透变幻莫测的夜色，星光斑驳的天际线在此刻化为了深沉幽暗的阴影深渊。
“南边的天际线上有什么东西，”他不安地低语道，“是一座山？我说不准，甚至不确定自己是否真的看到了什么。”
“你已经开始看到海市蜃楼了，”史蒂夫烦躁地说，“躺下睡吧。”
说完，史蒂夫便沉沉睡去。
刺眼的阳光唤醒了他。他坐起身打了个哈欠，首先感到的便是剧烈的焦渴。他举起水壶润了润嘴唇。还剩最后一小口水。亚尔·阿里仍在熟睡。

===Original===

Steve’s eyes wandered over the southern horizon and he started. He kicked the recumbent Afghan.
“Hey, wake up, Ali. I reckon you weren’t seeing things after all. There’s your hill﻿—and a queer-lookin’ one, too.”
The Afridi woke as a wild thing wakes, instantly and completely, his hand leaping to his long knife as he glared about for enemies. His gaze followed Steve’s pointing fingers and his eyes widened.
“By Allah and by Allah!” he swore. “We have come into a land of djinn! That is no hill﻿—it is a city of stone in the midst of the sands!”
Steve bounded to his feet like a steel spring released. As he gazed with bated breath, a fierce shout escaped his lips. At his feet the slope of the ridge ran down into a wide and level expanse of sand that stretched away southward. And far away, across those sands, to his straining sight the “hill” slowly took shape, like a mirage growing from the drifting sands.
He saw great uneven walls, massive battlements; all about crawled the sands like a living, sensate thing, drifted high about the walls, softening the rugged outlines. No wonder that at first glance the whole had appeared like a hill.
“Kara-Shehr!” Clarney exclaimed fiercely. “Beled-el-Djinn! The city of the dead! It wasn’t a pipe-dream after all! We’ve found it﻿—by Heaven, we’ve found it! Come on! Let’s go!”
---
Yar Ali shook his head uncertainly and muttered something about evil djinn under his breath, but he followed. The sight of the ruins had swept from Steve his thirst and hunger, and the fatigue that a few hours’ sleep had not fully overcome. He trudged on swiftly, oblivious to the rising heat, his eyes gleaming with the lust of the explorer. It was not altogether greed for the fabled gem that had prompted Steve Clarney to risk his life in that grim wilderness; deep in his soul lurked the age-old heritage of the white man, the urge to seek out the hidden places of the world, and that urge had been stirred to the depths by the ancient tales.
Now as they crossed the level wastes that separated the broken land from the city, they saw the shattered walls take clearer form and shape, as if they grew out of the morning sky. The city seemed built of huge blocks of black stone, but how high the walls had been there was no telling because of the sand that drifted high about their base; in many places they had fallen away and the sand hid the fragments entirely.

===Chinese===

史蒂夫的目光掠过南方的天际线，猛地一震。他踹了踹身旁躺着的阿富汗人。
“喂，醒醒，阿里。看来你昨晚还真没眼花。那儿有一座山——而且模样古怪得很。”
阿夫里迪人像野兽般瞬间彻底惊醒，手立刻按在长刀上，警惕地扫视四周寻找敌人。他的目光顺着史蒂夫所指的方向望去，双眼猛然睁大。
“凭真主起誓，凭真主起誓！”他惊呼道，“我们闯进了吉恩魔灵的领地！那根本不是山——那是黄沙腹地的一座石城！”
史蒂夫如同一根被释放的钢弹簧般一跃而起。当他屏息凝视时，一声激动的呐喊脱口而出。在他脚下，山脊的斜坡向下延伸进一片向南铺展的广阔平坦沙地。而在沙地的极远处，在他的极力眺望下，那座“山”正缓缓显出轮廓，宛如从流沙中升腾而起的海市蜃楼。
他看到了巨大凹凸的城墙、宏伟的堞楼垛口；四周的黄沙如拥有生命的知觉之物般蠕动蔓延，在城墙周围高高堆积，柔化了粗粝峻拔的轮廓。难怪第一眼望去整个遗迹宛如一座山丘。
“卡拉-谢赫尔！”克拉尼激动万分地喊道，“贝莱德-埃尔-吉恩！死者之城！这根本不是痴人说梦！我们找到了——天哪，我们找到了！快来！我们走！”
---
亚尔·阿里狐疑地摇了摇头，嘴里低声念叨着凶恶的魔灵，但他还是跟了上去。废墟的景象驱散了史蒂夫的饥渴，以及数小时睡眠未能完全消除的疲惫。他快步跋涉，全然不顾渐渐升腾的酷热，眼中闪烁着探险家炽烈的狂热。促使史蒂夫·克拉尼在这片冷酷荒野中舍命一搏的，并不仅仅是对那颗传说宝石的贪婪；在他灵魂深处，潜藏着白人古老的血脉遗产——探寻世界隐秘角落的渴望，而古老传闻已将这种冲动彻底激发。
此刻，当他们穿越分隔破碎山地与城池的平坦荒原时，他们看见残破的城墙轮廓愈发清晰鲜明，仿佛正从晨空中拔地而起。整座城池似乎由黑色巨石砌成，但城墙究竟曾有多高已无法判断，因为流沙在基座周围堆积得极高；许多地段城墙早已坍塌，残垣断壁被黄沙完全掩埋。

===Original===

The sun reached her zenith and thirst intruded itself in spite of zeal and enthusiasm, but Steve fiercely mastered his suffering. His lips were parched and swollen, but he would not take that last drink until he had reached the ruined city. Yar Ali wet his lips from his own canteen and tried to share the remainder with his friend. Steve shook his head and plodded on.
In the ferocious heat of the desert afternoon they reached the ruin, and passing through a wide breach in the crumbling wall, gazed on the dead city. Sand choked the ancient streets and lent fantastic form to huge, fallen and half-hidden columns. So crumbled into decay and so covered with sand was the whole that the explorers could make out little of the original plan of the city; now it was but a waste of drifted sand and crumbling stone over which brooded, like an invisible cloud, an aura of unspeakable antiquity.
But directly in front of them ran a broad avenue, the outline of which not even the ravaging sands and winds of time had been able to efface. On either side of the wide way were ranged huge columns, not unusually tall, even allowing for the sand that hid their bases, but incredibly massive. On the top of each column stood a figure carved from solid stone﻿—great, somber images, half human, half bestial, partaking of the brooding brutishness of the whole city. Steve cried out in amazement.
“The winged bulls of Nineveh! The bulls with men’s heads! By the saints, Ali, the old tales are true! The Assyrians did build this city! The whole tale’s true! They must have come here when the Babylonians destroyed Assyria﻿—why, this scene’s a dead ringer for pictures I’ve seen﻿—reconstructed scenes of old Nineveh! And look!”
He pointed down the broad street to the great building which reared at the other end, a colossal, brooding edifice whose columns and walls of solid black stone blocks defied the winds and sands of time. The drifting, obliterating sea washed about its foundations, overflowing into its doorways, but it would require a thousand years to inundate the whole structure.
“An abode of devils!” muttered Yar Ali, uneasily.
“The temple of Baal!” exclaimed Steve. “Come on! I was afraid we’d find all the palaces and temples hidden by the sand and have to dig for the gem.”
“Little good it will do us,” muttered Yar Ali. “Here we die.”
“I reckon so.” Steve unscrewed the cap of his canteen.

===Chinese===

骄阳升至天顶，尽管心中满怀热忱，干渴仍旧无可阻挡地侵袭而来，但史蒂夫以极强的毅力克制着痛苦。他的双唇干裂肿胀，但在抵达废墟之前，他坚决不肯喝下那最后一滴水。亚尔·阿里用水壶里的水润了润嘴唇，试图将剩余的水分给朋友。史蒂夫摇了摇头，继续艰难跋涉。
在荒漠午后的酷热中，他们终于抵达成遗迹，从坍塌城墙的一处宽阔豁口走入，凝视着这座死寂之城。黄沙阻塞了古老的街道，给倒塌半埋的巨大石柱平添了怪诞的轮廓。整座城市衰败风化得如此厉害，覆盖着如此厚的黄沙，探险者们几乎无法辨认出城市原本的布局；如今这里不过是一片由流沙与残石构成的荒原，其上笼罩着一股难以言喻的古老气息，宛如一团无形的阴云。
但在他们正前方延伸着一条宽阔的大道，即便风沙与岁月的侵蚀也未能将其抹去。宽道两侧伫立着一排排巨大的石柱，即便算上掩埋柱基的黄沙，它们不算异常高耸，却沉重结实得令人难以置信。每根石柱顶端都矗立着一尊由整石雕凿而成的雕像——巨大沉郁的造像，半人半兽，散发着笼罩全城的阴冷兽性。史蒂夫惊愕地喊出声来。
“尼尼微的翼牛！人首牛身像！天哪，阿里，古老的传说是真的！这座城果然是亚述人建的！整个故事全是真的！巴比伦人毁灭亚述时，他们一定是逃到了这里——天啊，这景象跟我看过的旧尼尼微复原图一模一样！快看！”
他指向宽阔街道尽头矗立的一座巨大建筑，那是一座宏伟阴郁的殿堂，其黑石砌筑的立柱与厚重墙体抵御了风沙与岁月的侵袭。流沙侵蚀的大海在其地基四周冲刷蔓延，溢入大门，但要想彻底淹没整座建筑，至少需要上千年。
“魔鬼的巢穴！”亚尔·阿里不安地低语道。
“巴力神庙！”史蒂夫高呼，“快来！我原本还担心宫殿和神庙全被黄沙掩埋，得掘地三尺才能找宝石呢。”
“那对我们毫无益处，”亚尔·阿里咕哝道，“我们会死在这里。”
“我猜也是。”史蒂夫拧开水壶盖。

===Original===

“Let’s take our last drink. Anyway, we’re safe from the Arabs. They’d never dare come here, with their superstitions. We’ll drink and then we’ll die, I reckon, but first we’ll find the jewel. When I pass out, I want to have it in my hand. Maybe a few centuries later some lucky son-of-a-gun will find our skeletons﻿—and the gem. Here’s to him, whoever he is!”
With which grim jest Clarney drained his canteen and Yar Ali followed suit. They had played their last ace; the rest lay on the lap of Allah.
---
They strode up the broad way, and Yar Ali, utterly fearless in the face of human foes, glanced nervously to right and left, half expecting to see a horned and fantastic face leering at him from behind a column. Steve himself felt the somber antiquity of the place, and almost found himself fearing a rush of bronze war chariots down the forgotten streets, or to hear the sudden menacing flare of bronze trumpets. The silence in dead cities was so much more intense, he reflected, than that on the open desert.
They came to the portals of the great temple. Rows of immense columns flanked the wide doorway, which was ankle-deep in sand, and from which sagged massive bronze frameworks that had once braced mighty doors, whose polished woodwork had rotted away centuries ago. They passed into a mighty hall of misty twilight, whose shadowy stone roof was upheld by columns like the trunks of forest trees. The whole effect of the architecture was one of awesome magnitude and sullen, breathtaking splendor, like a temple built by somber giants for the abode of dark gods.
Yar Ali walked fearfully, as if he expected to awake sleeping gods, and Steve, without the Afridi’s superstitions, yet felt the gloomy majesty of the place lay somber hands on his soul.
No trace of a footprint showed in the deep dust on the floor; half a century had passed since the affrighted and devil-ridden Turk had fled these silent halls. As for the Bedouins, it was easy to see why those superstitious sons of the desert shunned this haunted city﻿—and haunted it was, not by actual ghosts, perhaps, but by the shadows of lost splendors.
As they trod the sands of the hall, which seemed endless, Steve pondered many questions: How did these fugitives from the wrath of frenzied rebels build this city? How did they pass through the country of their foes?﻿—for Babylonia lay between Assyria and the Arabian desert.

===Chinese===

“让我们喝下最后一口水吧。不管怎样，我们不用担心阿拉伯人了。凭他们的迷信，绝不敢涉足此地。我想，我们喝完水，接着就会死去，但在此之前我们得找到宝石。当我断气时，我希望手里攥着它。也许几个世纪后，某个走运的家伙会发现我们的骸骨——还有那颗宝石。敬他一杯，不管他是谁！”
克拉尼开了一句冷酷的玩笑，将水壶一饮而尽，亚尔·阿里也照做了。他们已经亮出了最后的底牌；剩下的全凭真主裁决。
---
他们踏上宽阔的大道，面对人类敌人毫不畏惧的亚尔·阿里，此刻却紧张地左右张望，仿佛生怕随时会有一张生着尖角的诡异面孔从石柱后探出狞笑。史蒂夫自己也感受到了此地沉郁古奥的压迫感，几乎发觉自己正害怕青铜战车会突然从被遗忘的街道上轰鸣驶过，或是惊惧于青铜号角突如其来的肃杀长鸣。他暗想，死城里的寂静，比辽阔荒漠上的寂静要沉重可怖得多。
他们来到了宏伟神庙的门廊前。一排排巨大的石柱矗立在宽阔的大门两侧，门前积沙没足，上面歪斜挂着沉重的青铜构件，昔日曾用来加固威严的巨门，而精雕细琢的木门本身早在数百年前便已腐朽殆尽。他们步入了一座薄暮朦胧的宏伟殿堂，昏暗的石质穹顶由犹如森林树干般的立柱支撑。整座建筑给人的感觉是令人战栗的宏大与阴冷夺目的壮丽，宛如冷酷的巨人为黑暗诸神构筑的居所。
亚尔·阿里战战兢兢地行走着，仿佛唯恐惊醒沉睡的神祇；而没有阿夫里迪人那些迷信念头的史蒂夫，却也感到此地阴郁威严的气息将沉重的手掌按在了他的灵魂之上。
厚厚的积尘地面上不见丝毫足迹痕迹；自从那个惊恐万状、被魔鬼纠缠的土耳其人逃离这死寂殿堂以来，已经过去了半个世纪。至于贝都因人，很容易明白那些迷信的荒漠之子为何对这座遭诅咒的魔城避之不及——而它确实阴魂不散，或许并非游荡着真正的亡魂，而是昔日遗落辉煌的阴影。
当他们行进在这看似无边无际的殿堂细沙上时，史蒂夫心中泛起诸多疑问：这些逃脱狂乱叛军怒火的流亡者，究竟是如何建起这座城市的？他们又是如何穿过敌国领土的？——要知道，巴比伦正横亘在亚述与阿拉伯沙漠之间。

===Original===

Yet there had been no other place for them to go; westward lay Syria and the sea, and north and east swarmed the “dangerous Medes,” those fierce Aryans whose aid had stiffened the arm of Babylon to smite her foe to the dust.
Possibly, thought Steve, Kara-Shehr﻿—whatever its name had been in those dim days﻿—had been built as an outpost border city before the fall of the Assyrian empire, whither survivals of that overthrow fled. At any rate it was possible that Kara-Shehr had outlasted Nineveh by some centuries﻿—a strange, hermit city, no doubt, cut off from the rest of the world.
Surely, as Yar Ali had said, this was once fertile country, watered by oases; and doubtless in the broken country they had passed over the night before, there had been quarries that furnished the stone for the building of the city.
Then what caused its downfall? Did the encroachment of the sands and the filling up of the springs cause the people to abandon it, or was Kara-Shehr a city of silence before the sands crept over the walls? Did the downfall come from within or without? Did civil war blot out the inhabitants, or were they slaughtered by some powerful foe from the desert? Clarney shook his head in baffled chagrin. The answers to those questions were lost in the maze of forgotten ages.
“*Allahu akbar!*” They had traversed the great shadowy hall and at its further end they came upon a hideous black stone altar, behind which loomed an ancient god, bestial and horrific. Steve shrugged his shoulders as he recognized the monstrous aspect of the image﻿—aye, that was Baal, on which black altar in other ages many a screaming, writhing, naked victim had offered up its naked soul. The idol embodied in its utter, abysmal and sullen bestiality the whole soul of this demoniac city. Surely, thought Steve, the builders of Nineveh and Kara-Shehr were cast in another mold from the people of today. Their art and culture were too ponderous, too grimly barren of the lighter aspects of humanity, to be wholly human, as modern man understands humanity. Their architecture was repellent; of high skill, yet so massive, sullen and brutish in effect as to be almost beyond the comprehension of moderns.
The adventurers passed through a narrow door which opened in the end of the hall close to the idol, and came into a series of wide, dim, dusty chambers connected by column-flanked corridors.

===Chinese===

然而当时他们已无处可去；西面是叙利亚和大海，北方与东方则盘踞着“凶险的米底人”，正是那些凶悍的雅利安人协助巴比伦强固臂膀，将强敌击得粉碎。
史蒂夫心想，卡拉-谢赫尔——无论它在那个幽暗年代叫什么名字——或许早在亚述帝国覆灭前就已作为前哨边城建成，城破后的幸存者便逃奔至此。无论如何，卡拉-谢赫尔很可能比尼尼微多延续了几个世纪——毫无疑问，那是一座与世隔绝的奇特隐世孤城。
正如亚尔·阿里所说，这里曾经定是一片绿洲滋养的丰饶沃土；而在他们昨夜途经的破碎丘陵中，无疑曾有为筑城提供石料的采石场。
那么究竟是什么导致了它的覆灭？是风沙的蚕食与泉眼的枯竭迫使居民弃城而去，还是早在流沙漫过城墙之前，卡拉-谢赫尔就已沦为死寂之城？覆灭是来自内部还是外部？是内战将居民抹杀殆尽，还是被来自大漠的强敌屠戮一空？克拉尼困惑懊丧地摇了摇头。这些谜题的答案早已湮没在被遗忘的岁月迷宫中。
“*真主至大！*”他们穿过了阴影重重的宏伟殿堂，在殿堂深处撞见了一座狰狞可怖的黑石祭坛，祭坛后耸立着一尊面目狰狞、凶残丑陋的远古神祇。史蒂夫耸了耸肩，认出了这尊雕像的怪诞面貌——不错，那就是巴力，在远古时代的黑石祭坛上，无数尖叫扭曲的赤裸祭品曾向它献出赤裸的灵魂。这尊偶像以其彻底、深邃而阴鸷的兽性，体现了这座魔城浑然一体的灵魂。史蒂夫暗想，尼尼微与卡拉-谢赫尔的建造者们，必定与今日的人类截然不同。他们的艺术与文明过于沉重，过于冷酷地剥离了人性中明快轻松的一面，按照现代人对人性的理解，他们几乎算不上是纯粹的人类。他们的建筑风格令人望而生畏；技艺精湛，却散发着如此沉重、阴鸷与凶残的气息，令现代人难以理解。
冒险者们穿过祭坛旁殿堂尽头开出的一扇狭窄侧门，走入了一系列由列柱回廊相连的宽大、阴暗、落满灰尘的殿室。

===Original===

Along these they strode in the gray ghostly light, and came at last to a wide stair, whose massive stone steps led upward and vanished in the gloom. Here Yar Ali halted.
“We have dared much, sahib,” he muttered. “Is it wise to dare more?”
Steve, aquiver with eagerness, yet understood the Afghan’s mind. “You mean we shouldn’t go up those stairs?”
“They have an evil look. To what chambers of silence and horror may they lead? When djinn haunt deserted buildings, they lurk in the upper chambers. At any moment a demon may bite off our heads.”
“We’re dead men anyhow,” grunted Steve. “But I tell you﻿—you go on back through the hall and watch for the Arabs while I go upstairs.”
“Watch for a wind on the horizon,” responded the Afghan gloomily, shifting his rifle and loosening his long knife in its scabbard. “No Bedouin comes here. Lead on, sahib. Thou’rt mad after the manner of all Franks, but I would not leave thee to face the djinn alone.”
So the companions mounted the massive stairs, their feet sinking deep into the accumulated dust of centuries at each step. Up and up they went, to an incredible height, until the depths below merged into a vague gloom.
“We walk blind to our doom, sahib,” muttered Yar Ali. “*Allah il allah*﻿—and Muhammad is his Prophet! Nevertheless, I feel the presence of slumbering Evil and never again shall I hear the wind blowing up the Khyber Pass.”
Steve made no reply. He did not like the breathless silence that brooded over the ancient temple, nor the grisly gray light that filtered from some hidden source.
Now above them the gloom lightened somewhat and they emerged into a vast circular chamber, grayly illumined by light that filtered in through the high, pierced ceiling. But another radiance lent itself to the illumination. A cry burst from Steve’s lips, echoed by Yar Ali.
---
Standing on the top step of the broad stone stair, they looked directly across the broad chamber, with its dust-covered heavy tile floor and bare black stone walls. From about the center of the chamber, massive steps led up to a stone dais, and on this dais stood a marble throne. About this throne glowed and shimmered an uncanny light, and the awestruck adventurers gasped as they saw its source. On the throne slumped a human skeleton, an almost shapeless mass of moldering bones.

===Chinese===

在灰白幽暗的光线中，他们沿着这些回廊大步前行，最终来到一段宽阔的阶梯前，厚重的石阶向上延伸，隐没在黑暗之中。亚尔·阿里在此停住了脚步。
“老爷，我们冒的险已经够大了，”他低语道，“再往深处走还理智吗？”
史蒂夫虽急不可耐，却也能理解阿富汗人的心思。“你是说我们不该登上那道阶梯？”
“看着凶险得很。谁知道通往什么充满死寂与恐怖的密室？每当吉恩魔灵盘踞废弃建筑时，往往潜伏在顶层殿室。随时可能有恶魔咬下我们的脑袋。”
“反正我们也是死路一条，”史蒂夫咕哝道，“但听我说——你回殿堂守着提防阿拉伯人，我一个人上去。”
“在天际线上提防一阵风还差不多，”阿富汗人阴郁地应道，挪了挪步枪，松了松刀鞘中的长刀，“贝都因人绝不敢来这儿。带路吧，老爷。你像所有法兰克人一样狂妄发疯，但我绝不会留下你独自面对魔灵。”
于是两个伙伴踏上了厚重的石阶，每迈一步，双脚都深深陷入数百年积攒的尘埃中。他们一级又一级往上攀爬，登上了令人难以置信的高度，直到下方的深处融为一片模糊的幽暗。
“我们正盲目走向毁灭，老爷，”亚尔·阿里低声念叨，“*万物非主，唯有真主*——穆罕默德是真主的使者！然而我感到了潜伏邪恶的气息，我恐怕再也听不到开伯尔山口呼啸的风声了。”
史蒂夫没有回答。他不喜欢笼罩古庙的那种窒息般的死寂，也不喜欢不知从何处透进来的惨白灰光。
此时他们上方的阴暗逐渐退去，两人踏入了一间巨大的圆形大殿，从高耸有孔洞的穹顶倾泻而下的天光将殿内映照得一片灰白。但照亮大殿的还有另一股光芒。一声惊呼自史蒂夫唇间迸出，亚尔·阿里也随之失声叫了出来。
---
站在宽阔石阶的最顶层，他们直接望穿了整座宽广的大殿，地面铺着厚重的尘封地砖，周围是光秃秃的黑石高墙。在大殿中央附近，厚重的台阶通向一座石制高台，高台上伫立着一张大理石王座。在王座四周，闪烁流转着一股不可思议的光晕，震撼不已的冒险者们看清光源后倒吸了一口冷气。王座上瘫坐着一具人类骸骨，几乎已化作一堆面目全非的腐朽枯骨。

===Original===

A fleshless hand sagged outstretched upon the broad marble throne-arm, and in its grisly clasp there pulsed and throbbed like a living thing, a great crimson stone.
The Fire of Ashurbanipal! Even after they had found the lost city Steve had not really allowed himself to believe that they would find the gem, or that it even existed in reality. Yet he could not doubt the evidence of his eyes, dazzled by that evil, incredible glow. With a fierce shout he sprang across the chamber and up the steps. Yar Ali was at his heels, but when Steve would have seized the gem, the Afghan laid a hand on his arm.
“Wait!” exclaimed the big Muhammadan. “Touch it not yet, sahib! A curse lies on ancient things﻿—and surely this is a thing triply accursed! Else why has it lain here untouched in a country of thieves for so many centuries? It is not well to disturb the possessions of the dead.”
“Bosh!” snorted the American. “Superstitions! The Bedouins were scared by the tales that have come down to ’em from their ancestors. Being desert-dwellers they mistrust cities anyway, and no doubt this one had an evil reputation in its lifetime. And nobody except Bedouins have seen this place before, except that Turk, who was probably half demented with suffering.
“These bones may be those of the king mentioned in the legend﻿—the dry desert air preserves such things indefinitely﻿—but I doubt it. May be Assyrian﻿—most likely Arab﻿—some beggar that got the gem and then died on that throne for some reason or other.”
The Afghan scarcely heard him. He was gazing in fearful fascination at the great stone, as a hypnotized bird stares into a serpent’s eye.
“Look at it, sahib!” he whispered. “What is it? No such gem as this was ever cut by mortal hands! Look how it throbs and pulses like the heart of a cobra!”
Steve was looking, and he was aware of a strange undefined feeling of uneasiness. Well versed in the knowledge of precious stones, he had never seen a stone like this. At first glance he had supposed it to be a monster ruby, as told in the legends. Now he was not sure, and he had a nervous feeling that Yar Ali was right, that this was no natural, normal gem. He could not classify the style in which it was cut, and such was the power of its lurid radiance that he found it difficult to gaze at it closely for any length of time. The whole setting was not one calculated to soothe restless nerves.

===Chinese===

一只无肉的枯骨手掌向前摊开在大理石扶手上，而在其阴森可怖的掌握之中，一颗巨大的深红宝石宛如活物般脉动震颤着。
亚述巴尼拔之火！即便在找到了失落之城后，史蒂夫也从未真正允许自己相信他们真能找到那颗宝石，甚至未曾确信它是否真实存在。然而，被那邪恶、不可思议的光晕晃花了双眼的他，无法怀疑自己亲眼所见。他发出一声狂热的大喊，飞步穿过大殿跃上台阶。亚尔·阿里紧随其后，但当史蒂夫伸手要去抓那颗宝石时，阿富汗人一把按住了他的手臂。
“等等！”高大的穆斯林惊呼道，“别碰它，老爷！古老之物皆带诅咒——而这件东西必定受了三重诅咒！否则在这个盗贼横行的国度里，它怎会安然沉睡了这么多个世纪无人敢动？扰乱死者的遗物绝非善事。”
“胡扯！”美国人嗤之以鼻，“迷信罢了！贝都因人是被祖辈流传下来的传说吓破了胆。作为荒漠住民，他们本就猜忌城池，毫无疑问这座城在鼎盛时期就恶名昭彰。除了贝都因人，以前根本没人见过这地方，唯独那个土耳其人见过，而他当时大概已经被折磨得半疯了。
“这具枯骨或许就是传说中提到的那个国王——干燥的荒漠空气能将这类尸骨无限期保存——但我表示怀疑。也许是亚述人，极可能是阿拉伯人——某个走运得到宝石的乞丐，出于某种原因死在了这张王座上。”
阿富汗人几乎没听进他的话。他正怀着恐惧与痴迷凝视着那颗巨大宝石，就像一只被毒蛇目光催眠的飞鸟。
“看着它，老爷！”他耳语道，“那究竟是什么东西？凡人的双手绝不可能雕琢出这样的宝石！看它跳动脉动的模样，简直就像眼镜蛇的心脏！”
史蒂夫凝视着宝石，心中涌起一股莫名异样且难以言表的强烈不安。他深谙宝石鉴赏之道，却从未见过这般宝石。第一眼看去，他以为正如传说所述是一颗硕大无朋的红宝石。此刻他却不敢确定了，心中隐隐生出一种紧张的预感，觉得亚尔·阿里说得没错——这绝非天然寻常的宝石。他无法辨别其切割工艺的流派，而其妖异光芒的力量如此摄人心魄，以至于他发现自己很难长时间近距离直视它。眼前的整座环境也绝非能安抚紧绷神经之所。

===Original===

The deep dust on the floor suggested an unwholesome antiquity; the gray light evoked a sense of unreality, and the heavy black walls towered grimly, hinting at hidden things.
“Let’s take the stone and go!” muttered Steve, an unaccustomed panicky dread rising in his bosom.
“Wait!” Yar Ali’s eyes were blazing, and he gazed, not at the gem, but at the sullen stone walls. “We are flies in the lair of the spider! Sahib, as Allah lives, it is more than the ghosts of old fears that lurk over this city of horror! I feel the presence of peril, as I have felt it before﻿—as I felt it in a jungle cavern where a python lurked unseen in the darkness﻿—as I felt it in the temple of Thuggee where the hidden stranglers of Siva crouched to spring upon us﻿—as I feel it now, tenfold!”
Steve’s hair prickled. He knew that Yar Ali was a grim veteran, not to be stampeded by silly fear or senseless panic; he well remembered the incidents referred to by the Afghan, as he remembered other occasions upon which Yar Ali’s Oriental telepathic instinct had warned him of danger before that danger was seen or heard.
“What is it, Yar Ali?” he whispered.
The Afghan shook his head, his eyes filled with a weird mysterious light as he listened to the dim occult promptings of his subconsciousness.
“I know not; I know it is close to us, and that it is very ancient and very evil. I think﻿—” Suddenly he halted and wheeled, the eerie light vanishing from his eyes to be replaced by a glare of wolf-like fear and suspicion.
“Hark, sahib!” he snapped. “Ghosts or dead men mount the stair!”
Steve stiffened as the stealthy pad of soft sandals on stone reached his ear.
“By Judas, Ali!” he rapped; “something’s out there﻿—”
The ancient walls reechoed to a chorus of wild yells as a horde of savage figures flooded the chamber. For one dazed insane instant Steve believed wildly that they were being attacked by re-embodied warriors of a vanished age; then the spiteful crack of a bullet past his ear and the acrid smell of powder told him that their foes were material enough. Clarney cursed; in their fancied security they had been caught like rats in a trap by the pursuing Arabs.
---
Even as the American threw up his rifle, Yar Ali fired point-blank from the hip with deadly effect, hurled his empty rifle into the horde and went down the steps like a hurricane, his three-foot Khyber knife shimmering in his hairy hand.

===Chinese===

地板上的厚厚尘埃透着一股令人不适的陈腐气息；灰白的光线唤起恍若隔世的虚幻感，沉重黑石高墙森然耸立，仿佛暗藏玄机。
“拿上宝石赶紧走吧！”史蒂夫低语道，心底涌起一股罕见的恐慌与惧意。
“等等！”亚尔·阿里的双眼炽烈如火，他死死盯视的不是宝石，而是那些阴郁冰冷的石墙。“我们是落入蛛网的苍蝇！老爷，凭真主起誓，在这座恐怖之城上空徘徊的，绝不仅仅是昔日恐惧的幽灵！我感受到了危机的降临，正如我从前感受过的那样——正如我在丛林洞穴中感受到黑暗中潜伏着未曾谋面的巨蟒——正如我在刺客派神庙中感受到湿婆的隐秘绞杀者蜷伏欲扑——正如我此刻所感，强烈十倍！”
史蒂夫头皮发麻。他深知亚尔·阿里是身经百战的铁血老兵，绝不会被虚妄的恐惧或毫无意义的惊惶吓倒；他清楚记得阿富汗人所提及的那些险境，正如他记得在其他多次生死关头，亚尔·阿里东方人特有的通灵直觉往往在危险被肉眼所见、被双耳所闻之前就已发出警报。
“到底怎么了，亚尔·阿里？”他低声问道。
阿富汗人摇了摇头，眼中充盈着诡谲神秘的光芒，倾听着潜意识深处幽暗晦涩的警示。
“我不知道；我只知道它离我们很近，古老至极且邪恶至极。我想——”突然他止住话头猛然转身，眼中的诡异光芒瞬间消散，取而代之的是狼一般警觉凶狠的惧意与戒备。
“听，老爷！”他厉声喝道，“有鬼魂或是死人正在登阶！”
轻柔草鞋踏在石阶上的微弱脚步声传入耳中，史蒂夫浑身骤然紧绷。
“见鬼，阿里！”他急促地喊道，“外头有东西——”
伴随着汹涌涌入大殿的一群野蛮凶悍的身影，整座古老殿堂激荡起一阵狂暴的嚎叫声。在一瞬间发懵错乱的狂想中，史蒂夫甚至以为他们正遭受某个消亡时代的复生武士突袭；然而子弹掠过耳畔的刺耳爆鸣与扑鼻而来的刺鼻火药味告诉他，眼前的敌人实打实是血肉之躯。克拉尼怒骂出声；在虚妄的安全感中，他们竟被尾随而来的阿拉伯人如瓮中捉鳖般死死堵住。
---
就在美国人猛然举起步枪的刹那，亚尔·阿里已从胯侧贴脸开火，一枪夺命，随后将打空的步枪狠狠砸进敌群，如狂风般冲下台阶，毛茸茸的大手中3英尺（约1米）长的开伯尔战刀闪烁着寒光。

===Original===

Into his gusto for battle went real relief that his foes were human. A bullet ripped the turban from his head, but an Arab went down with a split skull beneath the hillman’s first, shearing stroke.
A tall Bedouin clapped his gun-muzzle to the Afghan’s side, but before he could pull the trigger, Clarney’s bullet scattered his brains. The very number of the attackers hindered their onslaught on the big Afridi, whose tigerish quickness made shooting as dangerous to themselves as to him. The bulk of them swarmed about him, striking with simitar and rifle-stock while others charged up the steps after Steve. At that range there was no missing; the American simply thrust his rifle muzzle into a bearded face and blasted it into a ghastly ruin. The others came on, screaming like panthers.
And now as he prepared to expend his last cartridge, Clarney saw two things in one flashing instant﻿—a wild warrior who, with froth on his beard and a heavy simitar uplifted, was almost upon him, and another who knelt on the floor drawing a careful bead on the plunging Yar Ali. Steve made an instant choice and fired over the shoulder of the charging swordsman, killing the rifleman﻿—and voluntarily offering his own life for his friend’s; for the simitar was swinging at his own head. But even as the Arab swung, grunting with the force of the blow, his sandaled foot slipped on the marble steps and the curved blade, veering erratically from its arc, clashed on Steve’s rifle-barrel. In an instant the American clubbed his rifle, and as the Bedouin recovered his balance and again heaved up the simitar, Clarney struck with all his rangy power, and stock and skull shattered together.
Then a heavy ball smacked into his shoulder, sickening him with the shock.
As he staggered dizzily, a Bedouin whipped a turban-cloth about his feet and jerked viciously. Clarney pitched headlong down the steps, to strike with stunning force. A gunstock in a brown hand went up to dash out his brains, but an imperious command halted the blow.
“Slay him not, but bind him hand and foot.”
As Steve struggled dazedly against many gripping hands, it seemed to him that somewhere he had heard that imperious voice before.
---
The American’s downfall had occurred in a matter of seconds.

===Chinese===

面对人类敌人，他甚至发自内心地松了一口气，激昂的战意瞬间爆发。一颗子弹擦飞了他头上的缠头巾，但山地人凌厉的第一刀横劈而下，便将一名阿拉伯人的头颅劈作两半。
一名身材高大的贝都因人将枪口顶住了阿富汗人的肋下，但尚未扣动扳机，克拉尼射出的子弹便击碎了他的头颅。进攻者人数过多反而阻碍了他们对这名高大阿夫里迪人的围攻，而他如猛虎般的迅疾身手让敌人放枪时甚至投鼠忌器。大半敌人蜂拥围堵着他，用弯刀和枪托疯狂劈砸，另一些人则尖叫着朝台阶上的史蒂夫冲杀上来。在如此近的距离内绝无脱靶的可能；美国人直接将步枪枪口戳进一张满脸络腮胡的面孔，轰然将其轰成一片血肉模糊的废墟。其余人发出如美洲豹般的尖叫继续猛扑。
此刻，正当他准备射出最后一发子弹时，克拉尼在电光石火的刹那瞥见了两幅画面——一名胡须挂着唾沫、高举沉重弯刀的狂暴武士已扑到近前；而在地面上，另一人正单膝跪地，仔细瞄准在敌群中冲杀的亚尔·阿里。史蒂夫在一瞬间做出了决断，越过扑来的刀客肩头开火，击毙了那名步枪手——这无异于为了朋友自愿舍弃自己的性命；因为那柄弯刀正朝着他自己的头颅呼啸劈下。然而，就在那名阿拉伯人全力劈砍、因用力而闷哼的瞬间，他脚下的草鞋在大理石台阶上猛然一滑，弯刀轨迹偏斜，狠狠砸在史蒂夫的枪管上火星四溅。刹那间，美国人反手轮起步枪，趁着贝都因人稳住重心再度举刀的当口，倾尽全身力气一枪托砸下，枪托与头骨应声粉碎。
紧接着，一颗沉重的铅弹重重击中了他的肩膀，剧烈的震荡令他一阵恶心眩晕。
在他摇摇欲坠的瞬间，一名贝都因人猛地将缠头布套住了他的双脚并用力一扯。克拉尼一头从台阶上栽了下去，重重砸在地上陷入半昏迷。一只褐色大手举起枪托正要将他的头颅砸碎，一声威严的号令骤然喝止了这一击。
“别杀他，把他手脚捆起来。”
当史蒂夫在无数双大手的按压下眩晕挣扎时，他恍惚觉得，自己从前在什么地方听过这个充满威严的声音。
---
美国人的落败发生在转瞬之间。

===Original===

Even as Steve’s second shot had cracked, Yar Ali had half severed a raider’s arm and himself received a numbing blow from a rifle-stock on his left shoulder. His sheepskin coat, worn despite the desert heat, saved his hide from half a dozen slashing knives. A rifle was discharged so close to his face that the powder burnt him fiercely, bringing a bloodthirsty yell from the maddened Afghan. As Yar Ali swung up his dripping blade the rifleman, ashy-faced, lifted his rifle above his head in both hands to parry the downward blow, whereat the Afridi, with a yelp of ferocious exultation, shifted as a jungle-cat strikes and plunged his long knife into the Arab’s belly. But at that instant a rifle-stock, swung with all the hearty ill-will its wielder could evoke, crashed against the giant’s head, laying open the scalp and dashing him to his knees.
With the dogged and silent ferocity of his breed, Yar Ali staggered blindly up again, slashing at foes he could scarcely see, but a storm of blows battered him down again, nor did his attackers cease beating him until he lay still. They would have finished him in short order then, but for another peremptory order from their chief; whereupon they bound the senseless knife-man and flung him down alongside Steve, who was fully conscious and aware of the savage hurt of the bullet in his shoulder.
He glared up at the tall Arab who stood looking down at him.
“Well, sahib,” said this one﻿—and Steve saw he was no Bedouin﻿—“do you not remember me?”
Steve scowled; a bullet wound is no aid to concentration.
“You look familiar﻿—by Judas!﻿—you are! Nureddin El Mekru!”
“I am honored! The sahib remembers!” Nureddin salaamed mockingly. “And you remember, no doubt, the occasion on which you made me a present of﻿—this?”
The dark eyes shadowed with bitter menace and the sheikh indicated a thin white scar on the angle of his jaw.
“I remember,” snarled Clarney, whom pain and anger did not tend to make docile. “It was in Somaliland, years ago. You were in the slave-trade then. A wretch of a nigger escaped from you and took refuge with me. You walked into my camp one night in your high-handed way, started a row and in the ensuing scrap you got a butcher-knife across your face. I wish I’d cut your lousy throat.”
“You had your chance,” answered the Arab. “Now the tables are turned.”

===Chinese===

就在史蒂夫射出第二枪的同时，亚尔·阿里已斩断了一名突袭者的半截手臂，但他自己的左肩也挨了枪托一记重击，整条手臂酸麻脱力。尽管顶着沙漠酷热，他身上穿着的羊皮大衣却替他挡下了半打劈来的飞刀。一支步枪在他脸颊旁骤然开火，枪药的灼烧让他剧痛无比，激得发狂的阿富汗人爆发出嗜血的怒吼。当亚尔·阿里抡起淌血的长刀时，面如死灰的枪手双手将步枪横在头顶试图格挡劈下的刀势，阿夫里迪人发出一声凶残狂喜的咆哮，如丛林猛兽扑食般骤然变招，将长刀狠狠捅入了那名阿拉伯人的小腹。但就在那一瞬间，另一柄饱含恶意的枪托狠狠砸在了这个巨汉的后脑上，头皮崩裂，将他砸得双膝跪地。
凭借本族骨子里的冥顽与狠厉，亚尔·阿里盲目地挣扎着重新站起，挥刀乱砍向他几乎看不清的敌人，但狂风暴雨般的重击再次将他砸翻在地，直到他彻底不再动弹，围攻者才停止殴打。若非首领再度下达严令，他们当场就会将他剁成肉泥；随后他们将昏迷的刀客捆绑结实，扔在史蒂夫身旁。史蒂夫神智完全清醒，肩膀上的枪伤正传来钻心的剧痛。
他怒视着站在身前俯视自己的高大阿拉伯人。
“怎么，老爷，”此人开口道——史蒂夫看出他绝非普通的贝都因人——“你不认得我了吗？”
史蒂夫眉头紧锁；枪伤剧痛可无助于集中精神。
“你看着眼熟——见鬼！——是你！努列丁·埃尔·梅克鲁！”
“荣幸之至！老爷还记得我！”努列丁嘲讽地行了个礼，“想必你也不会忘记，当年你送给我这份——大礼的场面吧？”
那双阴鸷的双眸闪烁着阴冷的杀意，酋长指了指自己下颌角上一道淡淡的白痕伤疤。
“我记得，”克拉尼咬牙切齿地说道，痛苦与愤怒丝毫没让他变得温顺，“那是几年前在索马里兰。当时你在做奴隶贩运的勾当。一个可怜的黑奴从你手里逃出来跑到我的营地避难。那天夜里你跋扈地闯进我的营地找茬挑衅，随后的混战中有人用屠刀在你脸上划了一记。我真后悔当初没割断你那肮脏的喉咙。”
“你曾经有过机会，”阿拉伯人回答，“现在风水轮流转了。”

===Original===

“I thought your stamping-ground lay west,” growled Clarney; “Yemen and the Somali country.”
“I quit the slave-trade long ago,” answered the sheikh. “It is an outworn game. I led a band of thieves in Yemen for a time; then again I was forced to change my location. I came here with a few faithful followers, and by Allah, those wild men nearly slit my throat at first. But I overcame their suspicions, and now I lead more men than have followed me in years.
“They whom you fought off yesterday were my men﻿—scouts I had sent out ahead. My oasis lies far to the west. We have ridden for many days, for I was on my way to this very city. When my scouts rode in and told me of two wanderers, I did not alter my course, for I had business first in Beled-el-Djinn. We rode into the city from the west and saw your tracks in the sand. We followed them, and you were blind buffalo who heard not our coming.”
Steve snarled. “You wouldn’t have caught us so easy, only we thought no Bedouin would dare come into Kara-Shehr.”
Nureddin nodded. “But I am no Bedouin. I have traveled far and seen many lands and many races, and I have read many books. I know that fear is smoke, that the dead are dead, and that djinn and ghosts and curses are mists that the wind blows away. It was because of the tales of the red stone that I came into this forsaken desert. But it has taken months to persuade my men to ride with me here.
“But﻿—I am here! And your presence is a delightful surprise. Doubtless you have guessed why I had you taken alive; I have more elaborate entertainment planned for you and that Pathan swine. Now﻿—I take the Fire of Ashurbanipal and we will go.”
---
He turned toward the dais, and one of his men, a bearded one-eyed giant, exclaimed, “Hold, my lord! Ancient evil reigned here before the days of Muhammad! The djinn howl through these halls when the winds blow, and men have seen ghosts dancing on the walls beneath the moon. No man of mortals has dared this black city for a thousand years﻿—save one, half a century ago, who fled shrieking.
“You have come here from Yemen; you do not know the ancient curse on this foul city, and this evil stone, which pulses like the red heart of Satan! We have followed you here against our judgment, because you have proven yourself a strong man, and have said you hold a charm against all evil beings.

===Chinese===

“我以为你的地盘在西边，”克拉尼低吼道，“也门和索马里一带。”
“我很久以前就不做贩奴勾当了，”酋长回答，“那行当早过时了。我曾带人在也门做了一阵马贼；后来又被迫换了个地盘。我带着几个忠心耿耿的部下来到这里，凭真主起誓，这些野人起初差点割断我的喉咙。但我消除了他们的猜忌，现在追随我的人马比过去许多年都要多。
“昨天跟你们交手的人正是我的部下——我派出去探路的前哨。我的绿洲远在西方。我们已经策马狂奔了许多天，因为我本来就要前往这座城池。当前哨回来向我报告有两名漂泊者时，我并未改变行军路线，因为我首先在贝莱德-埃尔-吉恩有要事要办。我们从西面进城，并在沙地上发现了你们的足迹。我们一路循迹而来，而你们却像瞎了眼的野牛，对我们的逼近浑然不觉。”
史蒂夫冷笑一声：“要不是我们以为绝没有贝都因人敢进卡拉-谢赫尔，你们哪能这么轻易得手。”
努列丁点了点头：“但我可不是贝都因人。我游历四方，见过许多土地和不同的种族，读过许多书。我知道恐惧不过是一缕青烟，死人就是死人，魔灵、鬼魂和诅咒不过是风一吹就散的薄雾。正是因为那颗红宝石的传说，我才涉足这片被遗弃的荒原。但我花了数月时间才劝服手下随我一同骑马来到这里。
“不过——我现在就在这里！而你们的出现真是一桩令人愉悦的意外。想必你们猜得出我为何要留活口；我为你们还有那头普什图肥猪准备了更精致的款待。现在——我拿上亚述巴尼拔之火，我们便动身。”
---
他转身走向高台，他的手下中一名满脸络腮胡的独眼巨汉惊呼道：“大人，住手！在穆罕默德降世之前，远古邪祟便盘踞于此！狂风呼啸时，魔灵便在这座殿堂里哀嚎，人们曾在月光下看见鬼魂在城墙上起舞。一千年间没有凡人敢涉足这座黑城——除了半个世纪（50年）前仓皇尖叫逃出的那个人。
“您是从也门远道而来的；您不知道笼罩在这座污秽之城上的远古诅咒，不知道这颗宛如撒旦赤红心脏般跳动的邪恶宝石！我们违背自己的判断跟随您来到这里，全因您展现了强者的魄力，并且自称拥有抵御一切恶灵的护身符。

===Original===

You said you but wished to look on this mysterious gem, but now we see it is your intention to take it for yourself. Do not offend the djinn!”
“Nay, Nureddin, do not offend the djinn!” chorused the other Bedouins. The sheikh’s own hard-bitten ruffians, standing in a compact group somewhat apart from the Bedouins, said nothing; hardened to crimes and deeds of impiety, they were less affected by the superstitions of the desert men, to whom the dread tale of the accursed city had been repeated for centuries. Steve, even while hating Nureddin with concentrated venom, realized the magnetic power of the man, the innate leadership that had enabled him to overcome thus far the fears and traditions of ages.
“The curse is laid on infidels who invade the city,” answered Nureddin, “not on the Faithful. See, in this chamber have we overcome our *kafar* foes!”
A white-bearded desert hawk shook his head.
“The curse is more ancient than Muhammad, and recks not of race or creed. Evil men reared this black city in the dawn of the Beginnings of Days. They oppressed our ancestors of the black tents, and warred among themselves; aye, the black walls of this foul city were stained with blood, and echoed to the shouts of unholy revel and the whispers of dark intrigues.
“Thus came the stone to the city: there dwelt a magician at the court of Ashurbanipal, and the black wisdom of ages was not denied to him. To gain honor and power for himself, he dared the horrors of a nameless vast cavern in a dark, untraveled land, and from those fiend-haunted depths he brought that blazing gem, which is carved of the frozen flames of Hell! By reason of his fearful power in black magic, he put a spell on the demon which guarded the ancient gem, and so stole away the stone. And the demon slept in the cavern unknowing.
“So this magician﻿—Xuthltan by name﻿—dwelt in the court of the sultan Ashurbanipal and did magic and forecast events by scanning the lurid deeps of the stone, into which no eyes but his could look unblinded. And men called the stone the Fire of Ashurbanipal, in honor of the king.
“But evil came upon the kingdom and men cried out that it was the curse of the djinn, and the sultan in great fear bade Xuthltan take the gem and cast it into the cavern from which he had taken it, lest worse ill befall them.

===Chinese===

您曾说只是想亲眼看一看这颗神秘的宝石，但现在我们看出来了，您打算将它据为己有。千万不要触怒魔灵！”
“是啊，努列丁，千万别触怒魔灵！”其他贝都因人齐声附和。酋长身旁那群久经沙场的凶悍亡命徒，自成一队站在距离贝都因人不远的地方，一言不发；他们习惯了罪恶与离经叛道，受荒漠蛮人那些迷信传说的影响较小，而贝都因人数百年来一直在耳闻关于这座遭诅咒古城的恐怖传说。史蒂夫虽然对努列丁满怀刻骨仇恨，却也不得不承认此人身上具备一种强大的号召力，一种天生的统御之能，使他迄今为止能够压制住数百年沉淀的恐惧与传统。
“诅咒是降给侵入这座城市的异教徒的，”努列丁回答，“而不是降给真主的信徒。看，在这间大殿里，我们不是已经降服了这些卡菲尔（卡拉法，不信道者）仇敌吗！”
一名留着白胡须的沙漠老者摇了摇头。
“这道诅咒比穆罕默德还要古老，从不问种族与信仰。太初创世之初，邪恶之人便筑起了这座黑城。他们残酷压迫我们黑帐篷里的祖先，彼此征战不休；不错，这座污秽之城的黑墙浸透了鲜血，回荡着不洁狂欢的喧嚣与阴暗密谋的私语。
“那颗宝石是这样来到这座城市的：在亚述巴尼拔的宫廷里有一位巫师，古往今来的黑暗奥秘皆为他所洞悉。为了给自己谋求无上的荣耀与权柄，他涉足一片黑暗荒无人迹的蛮荒之地，闯入了一座不可名状的无底巨窟深渊，从那魔影重重的深处带回了这颗璀璨夺目的宝石，它是由地狱凝固的烈火雕凿而成！凭借他高深可怖的黑魔法，他向守护远古宝石的恶魔施下咒语，从而盗走了宝石。那恶魔便在洞穴中昏睡，浑然不知。
“于是，这位名叫祖斯尔坦的巫师居于苏丹亚述巴尼拔的宫廷，通过凝视宝石深处的妖异光芒施展法术、预言未来，除了他自己，任何人的眼睛直视宝石都会被灼瞎。人们为了尊崇国王，便将那颗宝石称为亚述巴尼拔之火。
“然而厄运降临到了王国头上，人们纷纷惊呼这是魔灵的诅咒，苏丹在极度惊恐之下，命祖斯尔坦带走宝石并将其扔回原本取出的洞穴，以免招致更可怕的灾祸。

===Original===

“Yet it was not the magician’s will to give up the gem wherein he read strange secrets of pre-Adamite days, and he fled to the rebel city of Kara-Shehr, where soon civil war broke out and men strove with one another to possess the gem. Then the king who ruled the city, coveting the stone, seized the magician and put him to death by torture, and in this very room he watched him die; with the gem in his hand the king sat upon the throne﻿—even as he has sat throughout the centuries﻿—even as now he sits!”
The Arab’s finger stabbed at the moldering bones on the marble throne, and the wild desert men blenched; even Nureddin’s own scoundrels recoiled, catching their breath, but the sheikh showed no sign of perturbation.
“As Xuthltan died,” continued the old Bedouin, “he cursed the stone whose magic had not saved him, and he shrieked aloud the fearful words which undid the spell he had put upon the demon in the cavern, and set the monster free. And crying out on the forgotten gods, Cthulhu and Koth and Yog-Sothoth, and all the pre-Adamite Dwellers in the black cities under the sea and the caverns of the earth, he called upon them to take back that which was theirs, and with his dying breath pronounced doom on the false king, and that doom was that the king should sit on his throne holding in his hand the Fire of Ashurbanipal until the thunder of Judgment Day.
“Thereat the great stone cried out as a live thing cries, and the king and his soldiers saw a black cloud spinning up from the floor, and out of the cloud blew a fetid wind, and out of the wind came a grisly shape which stretched forth fearsome paws and laid them on the king, who shriveled and died at their touch. And the soldiers fled screaming, and all the people of the city ran forth wailing into the desert, where they perished or gained through the wastes to the far oasis towns. Kara-Shehr lay silent and deserted, the haunt of the lizard and the jackal. And when some of the desert-people ventured into the city they found the king dead on his throne, clutching the blazing gem, but they dared not lay hand upon it, for they knew the demon lurked near to guard it through all the ages﻿—as he lurks near even as we stand here.”
The warriors shuddered involuntarily and glanced about, and Nureddin said, “Why did he not come forth when the Franks entered the chamber?

===Chinese===

“然而，这位巫师不愿放弃这颗能让他解读亚当之前远古时代离奇秘奥的宝石，他逃奔到了叛乱之城卡拉-谢赫尔，城中很快爆发了内战，人们为争夺宝石自相残杀。随后，统治该城的国王觊觎这颗宝石，擒获了巫师并将其施以酷刑折磨致死，就在这间大殿里，国王亲眼看着他咽气；国王手里紧握着宝石端坐于王座之上——正如千百年来他一直坐着的那样——正如他此刻依然坐着的那样！”
阿拉伯人的手指猛然指向大理石王座上那具腐朽的白骨，荒漠蛮人们吓得面如土色；就连努列丁手下的那些亡命暴徒也倒吸凉气连连后退，唯独酋长神色自若，不见丝毫慌乱。
“在祖斯尔坦临死之际，”老贝都因人继续说道，“他诅咒了这颗未能保全他的宝石，他声嘶力竭地喊出了可怕的咒语，解除了他在洞穴中施加给恶魔的禁制，将那头怪物释放了出来。他向被遗忘的诸神——克苏鲁（Cthulhu）、科斯（Koth）与犹格-索托斯（Yog-Sothoth），以及所有居住在海底黑城与大地裂窟中亚当之前的太古旧日支配者哀号祈求，恳求他们收回属于他们的造物，并用临终的最后一口气向篡位伪王降下毁灭的诅咒——诅咒那国王永世端坐于王座之上，手握亚述巴尼拔之火，直到审判日的天雷炸响。
“刹那间，那颗巨钻宛如活物般尖声啼鸣，国王和卫兵看见一团黑云从地板上盘旋腾起，云雾中吹出一阵腐恶的阴风，风中走出一具可怖的身影，伸出令人生畏的魔爪按在国王身上，国王在触碰之下萎缩毙命。卫兵尖叫着四散奔逃，全城百姓哭号着涌入大漠，随后在大漠中暴毙或九死一生穿过荒原逃往遥远的绿洲市镇。卡拉-谢赫尔陷入死寂荒芜，沦为蜥蜴与胡狼的盘踞之地。当某些荒漠部族涉足这座孤城时，他们发现国王死在王座上，紧紧攥着那颗燃烧的宝石，但他们无人敢伸手碰触，因为他们知道恶魔正潜伏在附近，千百年来寸步不离地守护着它——正如我们站在此刻，它依然潜伏在近旁。”
武士们不由自主地打了个寒战，环顾四周，努列丁冷笑道：“既然如此，当法兰克人进入大殿时，它为何不现身？

===Original===

Is he deaf, that the sound of the combat has not awakened him?”
“We have not touched the gem,” answered the old Bedouin, “nor had the Franks molested it. Men have looked on it and lived; but no mortal may touch it and survive.”
---
Nureddin started to speak, gazed at the stubborn, uneasy faces and realized the futility of argument. His attitude changed abruptly.
“I am master here,” he snapped, dropping a hand to his holster. “I have not sweat and bled for this gem to be balked at the last by groundless fears! Stand back, all! Let any man cross me at the peril of his head!”
He faced them, his eyes blazing, and they fell back, cowed by the force of his ruthless personality. He strode boldly up the marble steps, and the Arabs caught their breath, recoiling toward the door; Yar Ali, conscious at last, groaned dismally. God! thought Steve, what a barbaric scene!﻿—bound captives on the dust-heaped floor, wild warriors clustered about, gripping their weapons, the raw acrid scent of blood and burnt powder still fouling the air, corpses strewn in a horrid welter of blood, brains and entrails﻿—and on the dais, the hawk-faced sheikh, oblivious to all except the evil crimson glow in the skeleton fingers that rested on the marble throne.
A tense silence gripped all as Nureddin stretched forth his hand slowly, as if hypnotized by the throbbing crimson light. And in Steve’s subconsciousness there shuddered a dim echo, as of something vast and loathsome waking suddenly from an age-long slumber. The American’s eyes moved instinctively toward the grim cyclopean walls. The jewel’s glow had altered strangely; it burned a deeper, darker red, angry and menacing.
“Heart of all evil,” murmured the sheikh, “how many princes died for thee in the Beginnings of Happenings? Surely the blood of kings throbs in thee. The sultans and the princesses and the generals who wore thee, they are dust and are forgotten, but thou blazest with majesty undimmed, fire of the world﻿—”
Nureddin seized the stone. A shuddery wail broke from the Arabs, cut through by a sharp inhuman cry. To Steve it seemed, horribly, that the great jewel had cried out like a living thing! The stone slipped from the sheikh’s hand. Nureddin might have dropped it; to Steve it looked as though it leaped convulsively, as a live thing might leap.

===Chinese===

难道它是聋子，连打斗的声音都没能将它惊醒吗？”
“我们没有碰触宝石，”老贝都因人阴郁地说道，“只要没人触碰宝石，恶魔便沉睡不起。然而，一旦有人将宝石从国王手中拿走，那沉睡之物便会苏醒！大人，我们求您，住手吧！”
“闭嘴！”努列丁厉声喝道，他的耐心彻底耗尽，“滚出去，你们这群迷信的蠢狗！我的弟兄们，去搜那两个异教徒的身，把他们的武器都带上。走，上台阶！”
然而贝都因人动也不动。他们退回石阶顶端，聚作一团，惊恐不安地拔出佩刀，紧握着步枪。
“你们这些胆小如鼠的狗崽子！”酋长破口大骂，不再理会他们，大步迈上大理石台阶。
史蒂夫屏住呼吸，甚至亚尔·阿里也挣扎着跪起身，瞪大双眼注视着。
那是一幅令史蒂夫终生难忘的画面：空旷阴郁的大殿，惨淡灰光穿透高耸穹顶斜斜倾泻，照耀着站在台阶上的高挑阿拉伯人，他身披雪白长袍，头戴华贵头巾，右手高高举起闪烁寒光的弯刀，目光贪婪而炽烈地盯视着王座上的白骨遗骸。
而在王座之上，瘫坐着那具残缺不全、毫无血肉的枯骨，其枯瘦如爪的手臂向前平伸，森森指骨间紧握着那颗深红妖异的璀璨宝石，宛如一颗跳动的活体心脏，在死寂大殿中投下猩红跳跃的光华。
努列丁踏上了王座前最后的台阶。
“大人！以真主之名！”一名贝都因人撕心裂肺地尖叫起来。
努列丁一把抓住了宝石。阿拉伯人群中爆发出一阵令人毛骨悚然的哀嚎，旋即被一声尖锐刺耳、非人的啼鸣撕裂。在史蒂夫耳中，极其骇人的是，那颗巨钻竟然宛如活物般惨叫了一声！宝石从酋长手中滑落。也许是努列丁失手脱落；但在史蒂夫眼中，那宝石仿佛剧烈抽搐一跃而起，宛如活物搏动腾空！

===Original===

It rolled from the dais, bounding from step to step, with Nureddin springing after it, cursing as his clutching hand missed it. It struck the floor, veered sharply, and despite the deep dust, rolled like a revolving ball of fire toward the back wall. Nureddin was close upon it﻿—it struck the wall﻿—the sheikh’s hand reached for it.
A scream of mortal fear ripped the tense silence. Without warning the solid wall had opened. Out of the black wall that gaped there, a tentacle shot and gripped the sheikh’s body as a python girdles its victim, and jerked him headlong into the darkness. And then the wall showed blank and solid once more; only from within sounded a hideous, high-pitched, muffled screaming that chilled the blood of the listeners. Howling wordlessly, the Arabs stampeded, jammed in a battling, screeching mass in the doorway, tore through and raced madly down the wide stairs.
Steve and Yar Ali, lying helplessly, heard the frenzied clamor of their flight fade away into the distance, and gazed in dumb horror at the grim wall. The shrieks had faded into a more horrific silence. Holding their breath, they heard suddenly a sound that froze the blood in their veins﻿—the soft sliding of metal or stone in a groove. At the same time the hidden door began to open, and Steve caught a glimmer in the blackness that might have been the glitter of monstrous eyes. He closed his own eyes; he dared not look upon whatever horror slunk from that hideous black well. He knew that there are strains the human brain cannot stand, and every primitive instinct in his soul cried out to him that this thing was nightmare and lunacy. He sensed that Yar Ali likewise closed his eyes, and the two lay like dead men.
---
Clarney heard no sound, but he sensed the presence of a horrific evil too grisly for human comprehension﻿—of an Invader from Outer Gulfs and far black reaches of cosmic being. A deadly cold pervaded the chamber, and Steve felt the glare of inhuman eyes sear through his closed lids and freeze his consciousness. If he looked, if he opened his eyes, he knew stark black madness would be his instant lot.
He felt a soul-shakingly foul breath against his face and knew that the monster was bending close above him, but he lay like a man frozen in a nightmare. He clung to one thought: neither he nor Yar Ali had touched the jewel this horror guarded.

===Chinese===

宝石从高台上滚落，顺着台阶级级弹跳，努列丁飞身扑了上去，眼见抓空不由咒骂连连。宝石砸在地板上猛然转向，尽管尘土极厚，它依然宛如一团旋转飞驰的火球，笔直滚向后墙。努列丁紧追不舍——宝石撞上了墙壁——酋长探手抓了过去。
一声濒死的惨嚎撕裂了紧绷的死寂。毫无征兆地，坚实的石墙洞开了。从骤然敞开的漆黑墙壁中，猛然射出一根粗大的触手，如巨蟒绞杀猎物般死死缠住了酋长的身躯，猛然将他整个人拽进了无底的黑暗之中。紧接着，墙壁再度恢复了原本严丝合缝的平整；唯有墙壁深处传出一阵骇人听闻、尖锐短促却被闷住的惨叫，令闻者毛骨悚然。阿拉伯人发出语无伦次的嚎叫四散奔逃，争先恐后堵在大门前挤成一团，互相推搡哭喊着撕开一条出路，发疯似地顺着宽阔的阶梯狂奔而下。
史蒂夫和亚尔·阿里无助地躺在地上，听着他们狂乱奔逃的喧嚣声渐行渐远，陷入呆滞与极度的恐惧中死死盯着那堵冷酷的石墙。惨叫声早已平息，取而代之的是更加令人毛骨悚然的死寂。他们屏住呼吸，突然听到了一声令全身血液瞬间冻结的声音——那是金属或石头在槽道中轻轻滑动的声响。与此同时，隐秘的暗门缓缓开启，史蒂夫在黑暗深渊中瞥见了一道微光，那仿佛是某种庞然巨怪狰狞眼瞳的寒芒。他猛然闭上了双眼；无论有什么可怖的怪物正从那黑暗深渊中潜伏爬出，他都绝不敢直视一眼。他深知人类的大脑承受能力有着极限，而他灵魂深处的每一缕原始本能都在向他尖叫：眼前的一切纯属梦魇与疯癫。他隐约感觉到亚尔·阿里同样紧闭双眼，两人如死尸般僵卧在地。
---
克拉尼听不见任何声音，但他清晰感受到了某种凡人理性无法企及的深邃恶意的逼近——那是一位来自外层深渊、来自宇宙辽远幽暗边缘的入侵者。致命的酷寒笼罩了整间大殿，史蒂夫甚至能感到那非人眼眸射出的灼人寒芒穿透了紧闭的眼睑，将他的神志完全冻结。他心知肚明，若是敢看一眼，若是敢睁开眼睛，瞬间陷入赤裸裸的疯癫将是他唯一的下场。
他感到一股令人灵魂震颤的恶臭呼气扑打在自己脸上，知道那庞然巨怪正俯身贴近自己，但他如被梦魇封冻般僵卧不动。他脑海中死死抓住一个念头：无论是他还是亚尔·阿里，都未曾触碰过这头恐怖恶灵所守护的宝石。

===Original===

Then he no longer smelled the foul odor, the coldness in the air grew appreciably less, and he heard again the secret door slide in its groove. The fiend was returning to its hiding-place. Not all the legions of Hell could have prevented Steve’s eyes from opening a trifle. He had only a glimpse as the hidden door slid to﻿—and that one glimpse was enough to drive all consciousness from his brain. Steve Clarney, iron-nerved adventurer, fainted for the only time in his checkered life.
How long he lay there Steve never knew, but it could not have been long, for he was roused by Yar Ali’s whisper, “Lie still, sahib, a little shifting of my body and I can reach thy cords with my teeth.”
Steve felt the Afghan’s powerful teeth at work on his bonds, and as he lay with his face jammed into the thick dust, and his wounded shoulder began to throb agonizingly﻿—he had forgotten it until now﻿—he began to gather the wandering threads of his consciousness, and it all came back to him.
